Spin(7)-Orbifold Resolutions
V. F. Majewski

Abstract
. We establish a general analytic and geometric framework for resolving Spin(7)-orbifolds. These spaces arise naturally as boundary points in the moduli space of exceptional holonomy metrics, and smooth Gromov–Hausdorff resolutions can be viewed as paths from the boundary to the interior of the moduli space. Our construction combines algebraic and symplectic techniques with analytic gluing methods: singular strata are replaced by families of adiabatic torsion-free, asymptotically conical fibred spaces whose existence is controlled by the analysis of Dirac-type operators on orbifold resolutions. We formulate obstruction conditions in terms of string cohomology, prove existence results for torsion-free Spin(7)-structures extending Joyce’s theorem [ Joy96a ] to the setting of orbifold resolutions, and construct new families of compact Spin(7)-manifolds. By dimensional reduction, our results also recover, generalise and extend the results of Joyce and Karigiannis [ JK21 ] on G 2 G_{2} -orbifold resolutions. This unifies and extends all known resolution methods for exceptional holonomy orbifolds.



1.
Preface

Special holonomy spaces, namely Riemannian orbifolds or manifolds in dimensions six, seven, and eight, with holonomy groups contained in
{\mathrm{SU}}(3)
,
{\mathrm{G}}_{2}
and
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
respectively, play an essential role at the intersection of modern mathematics and physics. These Ricci-flat spaces were initially identified through Berger’s classification
[
Ber55
]
of holonomy groups of Riemannian manifolds and have since become an active and emerging area of research at the intersection of differential geometry (specifically Riemannian geometry), geometric analysis (including geometric measure theory), gauge theory, enumerative algebraic geometry, and theories such as superstring, M-, and F-theory. The groups
G_{2}
and
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
represent the sole exceptional classes in Berger’s list, and thus, spaces with these holonomy groups are referred to as exceptional holonomy spaces.

The Berger list of possible occurring holonomy groups of simply connected, irreducible, non-symmetric Riemannian manifolds.
[
Ber55
]

Riemannian orbifolds with special holonomy form a natural class of mild singular spaces and frequently appear as boundary points in moduli spaces of smooth special holonomy metrics. In particular, they arise as singular limits of families of smooth manifolds with controlled geometry, and often carry residual structure compatible with the limiting geometry.

This paper will be devoted inter alia to the construction of paths in the moduli spaces of special holonomy metrics that degenerate to a singular special holonomy structure such as an orbifold, representing paths from the bulk of the moduli spaces reaching the boundaries. There is different viewpoint of such degenerating paths. Instead of asking for a family of Riemannian spaces that degenerates to a Riemannian orbifold, we will fix a Riemannian orbifold and seek for a family of “less singular” Riemannian spaces that “converges” to the Riemannian orbifold in the so called adiabatic/orbifold limit. We will refer to such families as smooth Gromov-Hausdorff resolutions.

Are all special holonomy orbifolds contained in the boundary of the moduli space of special holonomy metric, or equivalently, can we construct smooth Gromov-Hausdorff resolutions of special holonomy orbifolds?

In the case of Calabi-Yau spaces, i.e. spaces with holonomy contained in
{\mathrm{SU}}(n)
, algebraic geometry plays a crucial supporting role in the differential geometric and geometric analytic aspects. In particular, the proof of the Calabi conjecture
[
Yau18
]
guarantees the existence of Calabi-Yau metrics on special Kähler manifolds. Unfortunately, for
G_{2}
- and
\mathrm{Spin}(7)
-manifolds, as far as we know, one cannot work within the algebraic category to assist in proving analogous statements in exceptional holonomy.

Historically, the first examples of compact manifolds with holonomy
G_{2}
and
\mathrm{Spin}(7)
were constructed by Joyce
[
Joy96c
,
Joy96b
,
Joy96a
]
, who resolved families of flat
G_{2}
- and
\mathrm{Spin}(7)
-orbifolds. In these works, Joyce established existence results for torsion-free
G_{2}
- and
\mathrm{Spin}(7)
-structures. Later, Kovalev, Lee, Corti, Haskins, Nordström and Pacini
[
Kov03
,
KL11
,
CHNP15a
,
Nor23
]
developed the (extra) twisted connected sum construction for
G_{2}
-manifolds by gluing pairs of ACyl-
G_{2}
-manifolds.

More recently, Joyce and Karigiannis
[
JK21
]
established a first step toward a general resolution theory for
G_{2}
-orbifolds by constructing resolutions of compact
G_{2}
-orbifolds with codimension-four singular strata of isotropy
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
. However, construction methods for
\mathrm{Spin}(7)
-manifolds remain less developed. Beyond Joyce’s resolutions of flat orbifolds
[
Joy96a
]
and his construction of
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-orbifold resolutions of weighted projective spaces
[
Joy99
]
, no general method exists so far, and therefore, an extension of the work of Joyce and Karigiannis is needed to advance research in this direction.

This paper develops a general analytic and geometric framework for resolving
\mathrm{Spin}(7)
-orbifolds. The goal is to desingularise compact Riemannian orbifolds equipped with torsion-free
\mathrm{Spin}(7)
-structures in order to construct new compact
\mathrm{Spin}(7)
-manifolds. This generalises Joyce’s original resolutions of flat orbifolds to the non-flat case and relies on the analytic framework for Dirac operators on orbifold resolutions established in
[
Maj25b
]
.

Beyond providing new examples, these resolutions are central to understanding the global structure of the moduli space of torsion-free
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-metrics. Singular orbifolds naturally appear as boundary points of this moduli space, and smooth resolutions can be interpreted as paths leading back into its interior. From this perspective, the present work contributes to the broader question of which singular geometries can occur as limits of smooth exceptional holonomy spaces, and how the completion of their moduli spaces should be described analytically and geometrically.

This paper is based on parts of the author’s thesis
[
Maj25a
]
and coincides in major part. We will continue with outlining the structure of the paper.

Section
2
introduces the foundational background on
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structures on orbifolds. We begin with a review of octonionic linear algebra and Cayley forms, setting the stage for our geometric constructions. We will discuss the appearance of singular geometries such as
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-orbifolds in the boundary of the moduli space of
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-manifolds. Lastly, we will follow the original ideas of Joyce
[
Joy96a
]
and discuss the deformation problem of constructing genuine torsion-free
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structures form ones with small torsion. In contrast to the original work of Joyce, we will phrase this as a first order, non-linear differential equation whose linearisation is given by Hodge-de Rham operator
D\coloneqq{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}^{*}
.

Section
3
develops the Hodge theory on resolutions of Riemannian orbifolds. We begin by introducing the notion of tame smooth Gromov–Hausdorff resolutions: smooth families of Riemannian manifolds degenerating in a controlled way to a given orbifold. Such resolutions may be interpreted as paths in the moduli space of Riemannian metrics that approach its boundary. To model the local geometry near singular strata, we introduce the notion of conically fibred (CF) structures and their singular analogues (CFS spaces). Resolutions of orbifolds are then constructed by gluing asymptotically conically fibred (ACF) spaces into the singular neighbourhoods.

From the analytic perspective, the central problem is to understand how Hodge theory behaves under such degenerations. This is crucial for solving the nonlinear deformation problem of torsion-free
\mathrm{Spin}(7)
-structures, since it requires constructing a uniformly bounded right-inverse for the Hodge–de Rham operator along the resolution family. Building on the uniform elliptic theory of Dirac operators on orbifold resolutions developed in
[
Maj25b
]
, we address the guiding question:

How does the Hodge theory of a smooth resolution behave as it degenerates to a Riemannian orbifold?

The guiding idea is that the Hodge theory of the resolution should be described in terms of the model operators on the conically fibred singular (CFS) and on the asymptotically conically fibred (ACF) pieces used in the resolution.

Let
(X,g)
be a Riemannian orbifold,
(N_{0},g_{0})
the normal cone bundle of a singular stratum
S\subset X
and
(N_{t^{2}\cdot\zeta},g_{t^{2}\cdot\zeta})
a family of asymptotically conically fibred spaces resolving the normal cone structure. Using this data we will construct an interpolating smooth Gromov-Hausdorff resolution

\displaystyle\rho_{\zeta;t}\colon(X_{\zeta;t},g^{pre}_{\zeta;t})\dashrightarrow(X,g)

by gluing the ACF space into the orbifold, along a CF tubular neighbourhood of the singular strata. Later, we will see that
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structures on orbifold resolutions constructed in an analogous way will only be “close” to such interpolating resolution due to the nonlinearity of the correspondence of
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structures to Riemannian structures. We refer to this as the tame condition. Tame resolutions can be understood by first analysing the interpolating case.

Let in the following
D
denote the Hodge-de Rham operator with respect to
g
,
\widehat{D}_{0}
its normal operator, i.e. the leading order differential operator in an expansion of
D
close to the singular stratum and
\widehat{D}_{t^{2}\cdot\zeta}
a resolution of the dilated normal operator and
D^{pre}_{\zeta;t}
the Hodge-de Rham operator with respect to
g^{pre}_{\zeta;t}
. These operators act on differential forms on the CFS-, the CF- and the ACF-pieces of the orbifold resolution

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Omega^{\bullet}(X)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Omega^{\bullet}(X)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Omega^{\bullet}(N_{0})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Omega^{\bullet}(N_{0})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Omega^{\bullet}(N_{\zeta})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Omega^{\bullet}(N_{\zeta})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Omega^{\bullet}(X_{\zeta;t})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Omega^{\bullet}(X_{\zeta;t})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{D}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{pre}_{\zeta;t}}

We proceed by constructing adapted Hölder spaces, depending on a decay/growth rate
\beta
toward the singular stratum, and constant
\iota
and

, and a choice of APS-type boundary conditions, such that

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Omega^{k+1,\alpha;\bullet}_{{\mathrm{CFS}};\beta;\epsilon}(X;{\mathrm{APS}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Omega^{k,\alpha;\bullet}_{{\mathrm{CFS}};\beta-1;\epsilon}(X)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Omega^{k+1,\alpha;\bullet}_{{\mathrm{CF}};\beta}(\widehat{D}_{0};{\mathrm{APS}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Omega^{k,\alpha;\bullet}_{{\mathrm{CF}};\beta-1}(N_{0})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{D}\Omega^{k+1,\alpha;\bullet}_{{\mathrm{ACF}};\beta;t}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta};{\mathrm{APS}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{C}\Omega^{k,\alpha;\bullet}_{{\mathrm{ACF}};\beta-1;t}(N_{\zeta})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{D}\Omega^{k+1,\alpha;\bullet}_{\beta;t}(X_{\zeta;t})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{C}\Omega^{k,\alpha;\bullet}_{\beta-1;t}(X_{\zeta;t})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{D}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{pre}_{\zeta;t}}

are Fredholm maps. Moreover, the realisation of
\widehat{D}_{0}
is an isomorphism. Let
m={\mathrm{codim}}(S)
and
\phi
be a constant depending on the geometry of
(N_{\zeta},g_{\zeta})
. Then the following theorem relates the kernel

\displaystyle{\mathrm{ker}}(D^{pre}_{\zeta;t})\coloneqq\mathcal{H}^{\bullet}(X_{\zeta;t},g^{pre}_{\zeta;t}),

i.e. the space of harmonic forms on
(X_{\zeta;t},g_{\zeta;t})
to the approximative kernel
{\mathrm{xker}}_{\beta}(D^{pre}_{\zeta;t})
and cokernel
{\mathrm{xcoker}}_{\beta-1}(D^{pre}_{\zeta;t})
. These are the direct sum (more precisely a fibreproduct) of the kernel and cokernel of the above realisations of
D
and
\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}
.

Theorem
3.2
.

[
Maj25b
, Thm. 9.1]

Let
\beta\notin\mathcal{C}(\widehat{D}_{0})
and

\displaystyle\iota\geq

\displaystyle\phi-m/2-\beta\hskip 28.45274pt\text{and}\hskip 28.45274pt\pi\geq\phi+m/2+\beta-1-\alpha

whereby
\phi\geq 1
. The map

(1)

\displaystyle D^{pre}_{\zeta;t}\colon\mathfrak{D}\Omega^{\bullet;k+1,\alpha}_{\beta;t}(X_{\zeta;t})\rightarrow\mathfrak{C}\Omega^{\bullet;k,\alpha}_{\beta-1;t}(X_{\zeta;t})

is a bounded Fredholm map, whose kernel and cokernel satisfy

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{H}^{\bullet}(X_{\zeta;t},g^{pre}_{\zeta;t})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{xker}}_{\beta}(D^{pre}_{\zeta;t})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{xcoker}}_{\beta-1}(D^{pre}_{\zeta;t})}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\beta;t}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{ob}}_{\beta;t}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\beta;t}}

Furthermore, the uniform elliptic estimate

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Psi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{\beta;t}}\lesssim\left|\left|D^{pre}_{\zeta;t}\Psi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{k,\alpha}_{\beta-1;t}}

holds for all
\Psi\in{\mathrm{xker}}_{\beta}(D_{\zeta;t}^{pre})^{\perp}
. Hence, if
{\mathrm{ob}}_{\beta;t}=0
there exists uniform bounded map

\displaystyle R^{pre}_{\zeta;t}\colon\mathfrak{C}\Omega^{\bullet;k,\alpha}_{\beta-1;t}(X_{\zeta;t},\wedge^{\bullet}T^{*}X_{\zeta;t})\rightarrow\mathfrak{D}\Omega^{\bullet;k+1,\alpha}_{\beta;t}(X_{\zeta;t})

that restricts to a right-inverse.

We continue to show, that for Riemannian orbifold resolutions that are tamed by
g^{pre}_{\zeta}
the following holds true.

Proposition
3.4
.

[
Maj25b
, Prop. 9.4]

Let
\rho_{t}\colon(X_{t},g_{t})\dashrightarrow(X,g)
be a
(k+1)
-tame smooth Gromov-Hausdorff resolution with respect to
\rho_{\zeta;t}\colon(X_{\zeta;t},g^{pre}_{\zeta;t})\dashrightarrow(X,g)
. The
L^{2}
-projection induces a natural identification

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{\bullet}(X_{\zeta;t},g^{pre}_{\zeta;t})\cong\mathcal{H}^{\bullet}(X_{\zeta;t},g_{t}).

and Hodge theory yields a further identification

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{\bullet}(X_{\zeta;t},g^{pre}_{\zeta;t})\cong{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(X_{\zeta;t})\cong{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(X)\oplus{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(N_{\zeta})/{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(S).

If moreover
{\mathrm{ob}}_{\beta;t}=0
, then

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{\bullet}(X_{\zeta;t},g^{pre}_{\zeta;t})\cong{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(X)\oplus{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(S,\mathcal{H}^{\bullet}(N_{\zeta}/S))

and
D_{t}
admits a uniform bounded right-inverse, i.e. there exists a map Fredholm map

\displaystyle R_{t}\colon\mathfrak{C}\Omega^{\bullet;k,\alpha}_{\beta-1;t}(X_{\zeta;t})\rightarrow\mathfrak{D}\Omega^{\bullet;k+1,\alpha}_{\beta;t}(X_{\zeta;t})

satisfying

\displaystyle\left|\left|R_{t}\Psi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{k+1,\alpha}_{\beta;t}}\lesssim\left|\left|\Psi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{k,\alpha}_{\beta-1;t}}.

We will refer to a tame smooth Gromov-Hausdorff resolution
\rho_{\zeta;t}\colon(X_{\zeta;t},g_{\zeta;t})\dashrightarrow(X,g)
as being isentropic if the obstruction map
{\mathrm{ob}}_{\beta;t}=0
vanishes for a
\beta\leq 0
. An isentropic process in statistical physics is a process that is both adiabatic and reversible. In the same sense, the harmonic forms on an isentropic resolution are isomorphic to their adiabatic limit.

In Section
4
we will use these analytic results to analyse smooth Gromov-Hausdorff resolutions of
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-orbifolds. We will identify a condition on the ACF-
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structure on
N_{\zeta}
, which we will outline now.

Geometric Set-Up:
Let
X
be a compact, oriented eight orbifold,
\Phi\in\Omega^{4}(X)
be a
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structure and let
D\coloneqq{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}^{*_{\Phi}}
denote the associated Hodge-de Rham operator. The
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structure
\Phi
is torsion-free if and only if it is harmonic and hence, defines an element in

\displaystyle\Phi\in\mathcal{H}^{4}(X,g_{\Phi})={\mathrm{ker}}_{{\mathrm{CFS}};0}(D)\coloneqq{\mathrm{ker}}\left(D\colon\Omega^{k+1,\alpha;\bullet}_{{\mathrm{CFS}};\beta;\epsilon}(X;{\mathrm{APS}})\rightarrow\Omega^{k,\alpha;\bullet}_{{\mathrm{CFS}};\beta-1;\epsilon}(X)\right)

Further, let
S
denote a singular stratum of codimension
m
whose isotropy group
\Gamma
is conjugate to a finite subgroup of
\mathrm{Spin}(7)
. The restriction of the Cayley form
\Phi
to this singular stratum induces a
\mathrm{Spin}(7)
-structure on its normal cone bundle. This gives rise to a noncompact, conical fibration (CF)

\displaystyle\nu_{0}\colon(N_{0},\Phi_{0})\rightarrow(S,g_{S}).

The
\mathrm{Spin}(7)
-structure on the normal cone satisfies

\displaystyle\Phi_{0}\in{\mathrm{ker}}_{{\mathrm{CF}};0}(\widehat{D}_{0})\subset\Omega^{4}(N_{0}).

a condition we refer to as “adiabatic torsion-free”.

1

In the spirit of adiabatic, coassociative Kovalev-Lefschetz-fibrations studied in
[
Don16
]
.

Construction of Resolution:
We will now outline the main steps of the construction.

(1)

First, we need to construct families of asymptotically conical fibred (ACF)
\mathrm{Spin}(7)
-manifolds

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(N_{t^{2}\cdot\zeta},\Phi_{t^{2}\cdot\zeta})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(N_{0},\Phi_{0})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(S,g_{S})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{t^{2}\cdot\zeta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu_{\zeta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu_{0}}

converging to
(N_{0},\Phi_{0})
in the smooth Gromov-Hausdorff sense.
2
2

2

Here smooth Gromov-Hausdorff convergence refers to the convergence of the underlying metric spaces in the Gromov-Hausdorff topology and a
C^{\infty}_{loc}
-convergence of the
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structures.

(2)

We construct the family
(X_{\zeta,t},\Phi^{pre}_{\zeta;t})
by gluing in increasingly large portions of the ACF spaces into shrinking tubular neighbourhoods of the singular strata, and by interpolating between the torsion-free structure
\Phi
and the family
\Phi^{t}_{\zeta}
.

(3)

The torsion of the preglued
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-orbifold resolution, is concentrated close to the exceptional set of the resolution. If the size of the torsion shrinks to zero in the adiabatic limit, we wish to deform the preglued
\Phi^{pre}_{\zeta;t}
to a torsion-free one, and hence an element in

\displaystyle\Phi_{\zeta;t}\in{\mathrm{ker}}(D_{\zeta;t})\cap\Gamma(X_{\zeta;t},Cay_{+}(X_{\zeta;t}))\subset\mathcal{H}^{4}(X_{\zeta;t},g_{\Phi_{\zeta;t}}).

This nonlinear deformation problem is linearised, up to a gauge fixing, by the Hodge-de Rham operator. Assume now that the resolution
\rho_{\zeta;t}\colon(X_{\zeta;t},\Phi_{\zeta;t})\dashrightarrow(X,\Phi)
is isentropic for
\beta=0
. Then

\displaystyle{\mathrm{ker}}(D_{\zeta;t})\cong{\mathrm{ker}}(D^{pre}_{\zeta;t})\cong{\mathrm{xker}}_{0}(D^{pre}_{\zeta;t})\coloneqq\mathfrak{Ker}_{{\mathrm{ACF}};0}(\widehat{D}_{\zeta})\times_{{\mathrm{ker}}_{{\mathrm{CF}};0}(\widehat{D}_{0})}{\mathrm{ker}}_{{\mathrm{CFS}};0}(D).

Here,
\mathfrak{Ker}_{{\mathrm{ACF}};\beta}(\widehat{D}_{\zeta})
denotes the kernel of an adiabatic limit of the family of operators
\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}
. Consequently, we identify

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{4}(X_{\zeta;t},\Phi_{\zeta;t})\ni\Phi_{\zeta;t}=(\Phi_{\zeta}\times_{\Phi_{0}}\Phi)\in\mathfrak{Ker}_{{\mathrm{ACF}};0}(\widehat{D}_{\zeta})\times_{{\mathrm{ker}}_{{\mathrm{CF}};0}(\widehat{D}_{0})}\mathcal{H}^{4}(X,\Phi)

and thus need to find resolutions

\displaystyle\rho_{\zeta}\colon(N_{\zeta},\Phi_{\zeta})\dashrightarrow(N_{0},\Phi_{0})

for which,

\displaystyle\Phi_{\zeta}\in\mathfrak{Ker}_{{\mathrm{ACF}};0}(\widehat{D}_{\zeta}^{0})\subset\Omega^{4}(X).

This property can be again identified with
\Phi^{t}_{\zeta}
being adiabatic torsion free.

(4)

If
(X_{\zeta,t},\Phi^{pre}_{\zeta;t})
is a nonsingular, adiabatic and isentropic resolution, we can deform it to a genuine
\mathrm{Spin}(7)
-orbifold resolution

\displaystyle\rho_{\zeta;t}\colon(X_{\zeta;t},\Phi_{\zeta;t})\dashrightarrow(X,\Phi).

We will now outline the structure of this paper relative to the listed steps.

In order to construct ACF spaces as described in Step
1
, we need to understand resolutions of the normal cone fibres. In Section
4.4
we will focus on the cases
\mathbb{C}^{m/2}/\Gamma
with
\Gamma\subset{\mathrm{SU}}(m/2)
and
\mathbb{H}^{m/4}/\Gamma
with
\Gamma\subset{\mathrm{Sp}}(m/4)
. For these isotropy groups, algebraic and symplectic geometric methods such as the GIT-quotient and the (hyper-)Kähler quotient revised in Section
4.3
, allow us to construct universal moduli space of resolutions.

Parameter Space of ALE-Resolutions of
V/\Gamma
.

In Section
4.5
we will address Step
1
. We will use the constructed universal moduli spaces of resolutions of the normal cone fibres to construct families of ACF manifolds that are asymptotic to the normal cone bundle of the singular strata specifically for strata of type A
3
3

3

Type A singularities are defined to be singular strata with local model
\mathbb{R}^{8-m}\times\mathbb{R}^{m}/\Gamma
such that
\Gamma
acts freely outside the origin.
with isotropy group
\Gamma\subset\mathrm{SU}(m/2)
. Using the equivariant universal moduli spaces of resolutions of the normal cone fibres, discussed in Section
4.4
, we construct stratified parameter bundles

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}\colon\mathfrak{P}\rightarrow S

of stability conditions of resolutions of the normal cone fibres and universal moduli bundles

\displaystyle\mathfrak{k}\colon\mathfrak{M}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P},

whose fibres consist of resolutions of the normal cone fibres. The fibres over the regular stratum
\mathfrak{P}\backslash\mathfrak{W}
consist of smooth resolutions. We further construct a universal four form
\Phi_{\mathfrak{M}}\in\Omega^{4}(\mathfrak{M})
and prove the following proposition.

Proposition
4.10
/
4.14
.

Let
\zeta\in\Gamma(S,\mathfrak{P})
. Then

\displaystyle(N_{\zeta},\Phi_{\zeta})=(\zeta^{*}\mathfrak{M},\zeta^{*}\Phi_{\mathfrak{M}})

is
\nu_{\zeta}:(N_{\zeta},g_{\zeta})\rightarrow(S,g_{S})
a Riemannian fibration, equipped with the
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structure
\Phi_{\zeta}
. Moreover, for
0\in\Gamma(S,\mathfrak{P})
the bundle coincides with the normal cone bundle

\displaystyle\nu_{0}:(N_{0},\Phi_{0})\rightarrow(S,g_{S})

and
\zeta^{*}\rho_{\mathfrak{M}}=\rho_{\zeta}
equips
(N_{\zeta},\Phi_{\zeta})
with the structure of an ACF-
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structure

\displaystyle\rho_{\zeta}:(N_{\zeta},\Phi_{\zeta})\dashrightarrow(N_{0},\Phi_{0}).

of rate
-4
or
-6
respectively. Outside an exceptional set

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{\zeta}\coloneqq\rho_{\zeta}^{-1}(0_{S})

of codimension two the space
N_{\zeta}
is a truncated conical fibration over
\vartheta:Y\rightarrow S
.

Furthermore, we show that the stratification of the parameter bundle gives rise to wall-crossing phenomena, which are captured by the following propositions.

Proposition
4.12
/
4.16

(Wall-Crossing Formulae)
.

The space
N_{\zeta}
admits a smooth structure depending on the intersection of the image of
\zeta
and the walls
\mathfrak{W}\subset\mathfrak{P}
:

(i)

If
\mathrm{im}(\zeta)\subset\mathfrak{P}\setminus\mathfrak{W}
, then
(N_{\zeta},g_{\zeta})
is a smooth Riemannian manifold.

(ii)

If
\mathrm{im}(\zeta)
intersects
\mathfrak{W}
transversely, then
(N_{\zeta},g_{\zeta})
is a stratified Riemannian space.

The main results of this section are the following theorems.

Theorem
4.8
/
4.9
.

Given a singular stratum of type A of codimension four or six, and a section
\zeta\in\Gamma(S,\mathfrak{P})\cong\Omega^{2}_{+}(S,\mathfrak{H})
or
\zeta\in\Gamma(S,\mathfrak{P})
, respectively we can construct families of ACF
\mathrm{Spin}(7)
-structures

\displaystyle\rho_{\zeta}\colon(N_{\zeta},\Phi_{\zeta})\dashrightarrow(N_{0},\Phi_{0}),

resolving the CF normal cone
\mathrm{Spin}(7)
-structure. Moreover, these families are adiabatic torsion-free if
\zeta
is harmonic or parallel respectively.

In Section
4.6
we discuss how all orbifold resolutions constructed via the above method can be viewed as a converging subfamily within a finite-dimensional “universal orbifold resolution”:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rho_{\mathfrak{X}}}:(\mathfrak{X},\Phi^{pre}_{\mathfrak{X}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(X,\Phi)}}

As established in Section
2.6
, the deformation operator for torsion-free
\mathrm{Spin}(7)
-structures can be identified with the Hodge-de Rham operator on the orbifold resolution. To solve the nonlinear gluing problem, namely, deforming the adiabatic torsion-free preglued
\mathrm{Spin}(7)
-structure to a genuine torsion-free structure, we need to construct and uniformly bound a right inverse for the linearized operator.

In Section
5
we will apply the results of Section
3.3
to the case described below. In order to construct a uniform bounded right inverse for the family of Hodge-de Rham operators, it remains to be shown that the resolution is isentropic, i.e. obstruction map
{\mathrm{ob}}_{\beta;t}
vanishes. We compute the real cohomology of the adiabatic torsion-free resolution
X_{\zeta;t}
and prove the following theorem.

Theorem
5.1
.

Let
(X,\Phi)
be a compact
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-orbifold, and
S\subset X
a single singular stratum of codimension
m=4
and of isotropy
\Gamma\subset{\mathrm{SU}}(2)
. Let
\zeta\in\Omega^{2}_{+}(S,\mathfrak{H})
be harmonic. The resolution

\displaystyle\rho_{\zeta;t}:(X_{\zeta;t},\Phi_{\zeta;t})\dashrightarrow(X,\Phi)

is isentropic if and only if

\displaystyle{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(X_{\zeta;t},\mathbb{R})\cong{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}_{{\mathrm{CR}}}(X,\mathbb{R})

are isomorphic as graded vector spaces.

Here
{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}_{{\mathrm{CR}}}(X,\mathbb{R})
denotes the Chen-Ruan cohomology of the orbifold
X
which is introduced in Section
5.1
. This provides evidence that isentropicity should be viewed as a geometric-rather than purely analytic-criterion for compatibility with the expected string-theoretic picture.

We further prove that for codimension
m>4
and isotropy
{\mathrm{SU}}(m/2)
, all constructed resolutions are naturally isentropic for large enough
\beta<0
.

In Section
5.2
, we reprove Joyce’s existence result
[
Joy96a
]
for torsion-free
\mathrm{Spin}(7)
-structures, adapted to the setting of orbifold resolutions. Using the results of Section

, we prove that under certain (topological) assumptions
\mathrm{Spin}(7)
-orbifold resolutions exist. In contrast to general existence results for torsion-free
\mathrm{Spin}(7)
-structures, our approach does not require the torsion of the preglued structure to have a small primitive, nor do we rely on the existence of special (Morsian) tubular neighbourhoods
4

A Morsian neighbourhood is one for which

.
of the singular strata, as used in the work of Joyce and Karigiannis
[

]
on

-orbifold resolutions with

-isotropy.

Let
\left|\left|.\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{D}^{1,\alpha}_{\beta;t}}
and
\left|\left|.\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{0,\alpha}_{\beta-1;t}}
denote the adapted Hölder space constructed in Section

. By using the existence of a uniformly bounded right-inverse of the Hodge-de Rham operator, we are able to solve the nonlinear deformation problem of
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structures, by solving a fix-point problem. The condition of being adiabatic torsion-free ensures that the torsion of the preglued

-structures

After having established the existence result, we will be able to construct new examples of
\mathrm{Spin}(7)
-orbifold resolutions, yielding novel compact
\mathrm{Spin}(7)
-manifolds. Furthermore, we will prove that the preglued hyperkähler metric on the Hilbert scheme of two points is relatively close to the genuine hyperkähler metric. Using methods from
G_{2}

[
Nor23
]
, we are able to construct families of compact
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
