The representation-theoretic labelling of the quiver vertices naturally corresponds to the root system of the associated Dynkin diagram. This identification allows the geometry and topology of the resolution to be interpreted in Lie-theoretic terms.

The representations
\varrho
for
\varrho\neq 0
therefore correspond to simple roots
\theta_{\varrho}
. Furthermore, if we declare
\theta_{0}
to be the negative of the highest root, we can express

\displaystyle\theta_{0}=-\sum_{0\neq\varrho\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(\Gamma)}{\mathrm{dim}}_{\mathbb{C}}(\varrho)\theta_{\varrho}.

In particular, the center
\mathfrak{z}^{*}
of
\mathfrak{pu}({\mathrm{R}})
can be identified with the Cartan algebra
\mathfrak{h}_{\Gamma}
corresponding to the Dynkin-diagram associated to
Q_{\Gamma}
.

This connection also extends to the structure of the parameter space. The singular loci in the moduli space correspond to values of the moment map lying on reflection hyperplanes, precisely the walls of the Weyl chamber associated to the Dynkin diagram.

Proposition 4.9
.

[
Kro90
, Prop. 2.8]

The group
{\mathrm{PU}}(\vec{v})
acts on
\mu^{-1}(\zeta)
nonfreely if
\zeta
lies in one of the codimension 3 subspaces of the form
{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{H})^{*}\otimes D_{\theta}
, where
D_{\theta}
is the hyperplane in
\mathfrak{z}^{*}
corresponding to the walls of the Weyl-chamber, i.e.

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}=\bigcup_{1\neq\varrho\in{\mathrm{Irr}}(\Gamma)}{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{H})^{*}\otimes D_{\theta_{\varrho}}

The structure of the exceptional divisors in the resolution mirrors the combinatorics of the Dynkin diagram. This geometric realisation of Lie-theoretic data was one of the central insights of Kronheimer’s work.

Theorem 4.6
.

[
Kro89
, Thm. 1.2, Sec. 4]

The space
\rho_{\zeta}:M_{\zeta}\dashrightarrow\mathbb{H}/\Gamma
is diffeomorphic to a minimal resolution of
\mathbb{C}^{2}/\Gamma
. The exceptional divisor is dual to the Dynkin-diagram, i.e. there exists a basis
C_{i}
of rational curves in
H_{2}(M_{\zeta},\mathbb{Z})
such that the intersection form with respect to this basis is the negative of the Cartan matrix.

This duality between geometry and representation theory is further reflected in the topology of the resolution. Using Poincaré duality, one can identify the second cohomology group of the resolved space with the Cartan subalgebra of the associated Lie algebra.

Corollary 4.5
.

Using Poincarè-duality we deduce that

\displaystyle{\mathrm{H}}^{2}(M_{\zeta})\cong\mathfrak{h}_{\Gamma}

and

\displaystyle{\mathrm{H}}^{2}(M_{\zeta})\cong{\mathrm{H}}^{2}_{c}(M_{\zeta})={\mathrm{H}}^{2}_{c;-}(M_{\zeta})\cong\mathcal{H}^{2}_{{\mathrm{AC}};-}(M_{\zeta})

as
[C]\cdot[C]={\mathrm{PD}}(C)\cup{\mathrm{PD}}(C)<0
. In particular, we identify
{\mathrm{H}}_{2}(M_{\zeta},\mathbb{Z})
with the root lattice of
\mathfrak{h}_{\Gamma}
.

The identification of
{\mathrm{H}}^{2}(M_{\zeta})
with the Cartan subalgebra
\mathfrak{h}_{\Gamma}
endows the moduli space with a rich algebraic structure. The natural symmetries of the root system induce an action of the Weyl group on the space of stability conditions. These symmetries act via reflections across the walls of the Weyl chamber and lift to automorphisms of the universal moduli space, permuting the corresponding birational models of the resolution.

The Weyl group of the Cartan algebra of ADE type

\displaystyle\mathbb{W}={\mathrm{Weyl}}_{0}(Q_{\Gamma})

acts via a reflection

\displaystyle\sigma*\theta_{\zeta}(\varrho)=-\theta_{\zeta}((\sigma\varrho)^{*})

along the hyperplane associated to the root
\sigma
and extends to an action of
15
15

15

Here
{\mathrm{Aut}}_{0}(Q_{\Gamma})
are the automorphisms of the quiver
Q_{\Gamma}
that fix
\varrho_{0}
.

\displaystyle\mathbb{U}={\mathrm{Aut}}_{0}(Q_{\Gamma})\ltimes{\mathrm{Weyl}}_{0}(Q_{\Gamma})

on both
\mathbb{M}
and
\Theta_{{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{H})}
such that
\mu
and
\check{H}
are equivariant;
\underline{\omega}_{\mathbb{M}}
, and
\pi_{\mathfrak{z}}F_{\mathbb{A}}
are invariant under
\mathbb{U}
.

Notice that the action of
{\mathrm{N}}_{{\mathrm{SO}}(\mathbb{H})}(\Gamma)
by conjugation induces an action on the regular representation

\displaystyle\varrho_{{\mathrm{R}}}:{\mathrm{N}}_{{\mathrm{SO}}(\mathbb{H})}(\Gamma)\rightarrow{\mathrm{Sp}}({\mathrm{R}}){\mathrm{Sp}}(1)

and thus on
{\mathrm{Rep}}(Q_{\Gamma},\vec{v})
. Moreover, the hyperkähler moment map

\displaystyle\mu:{\mathrm{Rep}}(Q_{\Gamma},\vec{v})\rightarrow\Theta_{{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{H})}

is equivariant with respect to the action, i.e. for
n\in{\mathrm{N}}_{{\mathrm{SO}}(\mathbb{H})}(\Gamma)

\displaystyle\mu\circ\left(\varrho_{\mathbb{H}}(n)\otimes{\mathrm{Ad}}_{\varrho_{{\mathrm{R}}}(n)}\right)=\left(\varrho_{{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{H})}(n)\otimes{\mathrm{Ad}}^{*}_{\varrho_{{\mathrm{R}}}(n)}\right)\circ\mu.

Here
\varrho_{{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{H})}
acts via the identification
\wedge^{2}_{+}\mathbb{H}^{*}\cong{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{H})
. Consequently, it induces an action of

\displaystyle\varrho_{\mathbb{M}}:{\mathrm{N}}_{{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)}(\Gamma)\twoheadrightarrow{\mathrm{N}}_{{\mathrm{SO}}(\mathbb{H})}(\Gamma)\rightarrow{\mathrm{Diff}}(\mathbb{M})

on
\mathbb{M}
, such that
\kappa\colon\mathbb{M}\rightarrow\Theta_{{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{H})}
and
\check{H}
are
{\mathrm{N}}_{{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)}(\Gamma)
-equivariant, and
\underline{\omega}_{\mathbb{M}}
and
\pi_{\mathfrak{z}}F_{\mathbb{A}}
are
{\mathrm{N}}_{{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)}(\Gamma)
-invariant.

4.4.2.
Parameter Space of Calabi-Yau ALE Six Manifolds

Let in the following
\Gamma\subset{\mathrm{SU}}(3)
. Most of the results of the Kronheimer construction of ALE
\textbf{HK}_{1}
also (conjecturally) descend to ALE
\textbf{CY}_{3}
. For the remaining part this paper we will assume that these Conjectures hold. Let
\Gamma
act freely on
V\backslash\{0\}
.

The case of Calabi–Yau ALE manifolds in real dimension six is a natural generalisation of the hyperkähler ALE spaces studied in four dimensions. However, the theory is significantly more subtle: while the hyperkähler case benefits from the rigidity of quaternionic structures, the Calabi–Yau setting allows for greater geometric flexibility. Despite these differences, many of the constructions, particularly those using moment maps and quiver varieties, continue to apply. We now summarise the results.

Theorem 4.7
.

[
Inf96
, Thm. 5.1]

For each
\zeta\in\Theta^{reg}_{{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{C})}
, the space

\displaystyle M_{\zeta}\coloneqq(\mu^{-1}(\zeta)\cap\mathcal{N}_{0})/{\mathrm{PU}}(\vec{v})\cong\mathcal{N}_{0}\sslash_{\zeta}{\mathrm{PGL}}(\vec{v})

is a ALE-Kähler manifold rate
-4
and crepant resolution of
\mathbb{C}^{3}/\Gamma
.

While Infirri’s result establishes the existence of crepant Kähler ALE resolutions, it does not directly imply the existence of Ricci-flat metrics. Nevertheless, a deep result of Joyce ensures that within each Kähler class on such a resolution, there exists a unique Calabi–Yau metric. This leads to the following corollary.

Corollary 4.6
.

[
Joy00
, Thm. 8.5.1]

There exists a unique Calabi-Yau metric in the same Kähler class as
g_{\zeta}
, i.e. there exits a unique tuple

\displaystyle(\omega_{\zeta,RF},\theta_{\zeta,RF})\in\Omega^{1,1}(M_{\zeta})\oplus\Omega^{3,0}(M_{\zeta})

defining a Calabi-Yau ALE-manifold for each
\zeta\in\Theta^{reg}_{{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{C})}
. Moreover, all these ALE metric are of rate
-6
.

In the spirit of the Kronheimer construction, one might hope that the quotient metric
g_{\zeta}
is already Ricci-flat, so that no further perturbation is required to achieve the Calabi–Yau condition. While this is not yet known in full generality, it is the content of the following conjecture.

Conjecture 4.5
.

[
Inf96
, Con. 3]

The Kähler metric
g_{\zeta}
is of rate
-6
and Ricci-flat. The holomorphic volume form
\theta_{\zeta}\in\Omega^{3,0}(M_{\zeta})
is determined by

\displaystyle\Psi_{\alpha}(\beta,\gamma,\delta)={\mathrm{tr}}(\alpha\beta\gamma)\hskip 28.45274pt\beta,\gamma,\delta\in(\mathcal{H}^{0,1}_{\alpha})^{\Gamma}.

To systematically organise the collection of Calabi–Yau metrics across different stability parameters, we introduce a universal moduli space of Kähler ALE metrics and their associated holomorphic volume forms. This allows us to view the family of Calabi–Yau ALE spaces as a smooth fibration over the space of stability conditions.

Definition 4.9
.

Let
\kappa\colon\mathbb{M}\rightarrow\Theta^{reg}_{{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{C})}
denote the universal Kähler ALE bundle. Define the vertical two form
\omega_{\mathbb{M},RF}\in\Omega^{0,2}(\mathbb{M})

\displaystyle\omega_{\mathbb{M},RF}|_{\zeta}=\omega_{\zeta,RF}

and two vertical
3
-forms
{\mathrm{Re}}({\theta_{\mathbb{M},RF}}),{\mathrm{Im}}({\theta_{\mathbb{M},RF}})\in\Omega^{0,3}(\mathbb{M})

\displaystyle{\mathrm{Re}}(\theta_{\mathbb{M},RF})|_{\zeta}={\mathrm{Re}}(\theta_{RF;\zeta})\hskip 28.45274pt{\mathrm{Im}}(\theta_{\mathbb{M}RF})|_{\zeta}={\mathrm{Im}}(\theta_{RF;\zeta})

where
\{\omega_{\zeta,RF},{\mathrm{e}}^{i\phi}\theta_{RF;\zeta}\}\in\Omega^{1,1}(M_{\zeta})\oplus\Omega^{n,0}(M_{\zeta})
is the unique Calabi-Yau structure up to a phase factor associated to Calabi-Yau metric in the same Kähler class as
\omega_{\zeta}
. Moreover, the universal bundle
\kappa\colon\mathbb{M}\rightarrow\Theta_{{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{C})}
and the connection
\check{H}
are
\mathbb{U}
-equivariant, while the forms
\omega
,
\theta
and
\rho_{\zeta}
are
\mathbb{U}
-invariant.

\lozenge

Remark 24
.

Here we used Joyce’ proof of the ALE-Calabi-conjecture to write
\omega_{\zeta,RF}=\omega_{\zeta}+{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}}^{c}\phi_{\zeta}
for a unique function
\phi_{\zeta}
.

Based on the geometry of the moduli space of ALE hyperkähler spaces, discussed in the previous section, we conjecture the existence of the following structures on
\mathbb{M}
.

Conjecture 4.6
.

There exist
{\Lambda_{\mathbb{M};RF}}\oplus{\Xi_{\mathbb{M};RF}}\in\Omega^{0,2}\oplus\Omega^{0,(n,0)}(\mathbb{M},\mathfrak{z})
such that

\displaystyle{\mathrm{d}}\omega_{\mathbb{M};RF}={\mathrm{d}}^{0,1}\omega_{\mathbb{M};RF}=\left<\kappa^{*}\theta_{\Theta_{{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{C})}}\wedge{\Lambda_{\mathbb{M};RF}}\right>\hskip 28.45274pt\text{and}\hskip 28.45274pt{\Lambda_{\mathbb{M};RF}}\wedge\omega_{\mathbb{M};RF}=0.

and

\displaystyle{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{Re}}({\theta_{\mathbb{M};RF}})=

\displaystyle{\mathrm{d}}^{1,0}{\mathrm{Re}}({\theta_{\mathbb{M};RF}})=\left<\kappa^{*}\theta_{\Theta_{{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{C})}}\wedge{\mathrm{Re}}({\Xi_{\mathbb{M};RF}})\right>

\displaystyle{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{Im}}({\theta_{\mathbb{M};RF}})=

\displaystyle{\mathrm{d}}^{1,0}{\mathrm{Im}}({\theta_{\mathbb{M};RF}})=\left<\kappa^{*}\theta_{\Theta_{{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{C})}}\wedge{\mathrm{Im}}({\Xi_{\mathbb{M};RF}})\right>.

4.4.3.
Parameter Spaces of Hyperkähler- and Calabi-Yau QALE Spaces

In this section, we summarise how the representation-theoretic approach to quotient singularities extends to the construction of families of QALE (quasi-asymptotically locally Euclidean) spaces with special holonomy. These include Calabi-Yau and hyperkähler spaces resolving quotients of the form
V/\Gamma
for
\Gamma\subset\mathrm{SU}(V)
(or
\Gamma\subset\mathrm{Sp}(V)
). Our goal is to describe the parameter spaces of such resolutions in terms of quiver varieties following the work of
[
Nak94
,
Nak99
,
Yam23
,
Yam25
]
.

We now conjecture the global geometry of the moduli space of crepant resolutions as a family over the space of stability parameters.

Conjecture 4.7
.

Let
\Gamma\subset\mathrm{SU}(V)
be a finite subgroup and assume that the singular space
V/\Gamma
admits a crepant resolution. Then there exists a stratified Riemannian fibration

\displaystyle\kappa:\mathbb{M}\rightarrow\Theta_{{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{K})},

which is equivariant under the action of
\mathbb{R}_{>0}\times N_{\mathrm{SO}(V)}(\Gamma)
. This fibration is equipped with an Ehresmann connection
\check{H}
and a vertically closed differential form

\displaystyle(\omega_{\mathbb{M}},\theta_{\mathbb{M}})

\displaystyle\in\Omega^{0,2}(\mathbb{M})\oplus\Omega^{m/2,0}(\mathbb{M})\quad\text{or}

\displaystyle\underline{\omega}_{\mathbb{M}}

\displaystyle\in\Omega^{0,2}(\mathbb{M},\mathrm{Im}(\mathbb{H})),

respectively. The fibres of
\kappa
are special holonomy QALE spaces resolving the singularity
V/\Gamma
, and fit together in a family:

\displaystyle\rho_{\zeta}\colon(M_{\zeta},\omega_{\zeta},\theta_{\zeta})\dashrightarrow(V/\Gamma,\omega_{0},\theta_{0})\hskip 28.45274pt\text{or}\hskip 28.45274pt\rho_{\zeta}\colon(M_{\zeta},\underline{\omega}_{\zeta})\dashrightarrow(V/\Gamma,\underline{\omega}_{0}).

4.5.
Resolutions of Spin(7)-Normal Cones

The constructions developed in this section are inspired by Barbosa’s adiabatic resolution techniques for singular coassociative fibrations
[
Bar19
]
, and generalise them to the setting of
\mathrm{Spin}(7)
-orbifolds. Building on the local normal cone models introduced in Section
4
, we describe a systematic method for resolving singular strata of various codimensions via families of asymptotically conically fibred (ACF)
\mathrm{Spin}(7)
-manifolds.

Our approach constructs local models for adiabatic resolutions using universal families of crepant resolutions parametrized by bundles of stability conditions over the strata. These local resolutions are equipped with natural almost
\mathrm{Spin}(7)
-structures which become torsion-free in the adiabatic limit. Crucially, the resolution depends on a section of a parameter bundle whose image may intersect certain walls in the base. The smooth or stratified structure of the total space reflects the wall-crossing behaviour.

We emphasize that the constructions developed throughout this section are local near the singular strata. In particular, we do not assume that the singular strata are compact or connected.

4.5.1.
Adiabatic Resolutions of Codimension Four Singularities

Let
(X,\Phi)
be a
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-orbifold and
S\subset X
be a singular stratum of codimension four, isotropy
\Gamma\subset{\mathrm{SU}}(2)
. We denote by

\displaystyle\nu_{0}\colon(N_{0},\Phi_{0})\rightarrow(S,g_{S})

its normal cone bundle,
(Y,g_{Y;r})
its CF-link
16
16

16

(CF(Y),g_{{\mathrm{CF}}})\coloneqq(\mathbb{R}_{>0}\times Y,{\mathrm{d}}r^{2}+g_{Y}^{2,0}+r^{2}g_{Y}^{0,2})\cong(N_{0},g_{0})
.
and by
Fr(X/S,\Phi)
the
{\mathrm{N}}_{{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)}(\Gamma)
reduction of principal bundle of
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-frames along
S
. In the following, we will construct ACF-
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structures parametrised by sections parameter bundles, whose fibres are the spaces of stability conditions of ALE-hyperkähler spaces resolving the normal cone fibre
\mathbb{H}/\Gamma
.

In Section
4.4.1
we established that there exist representations of the group
{\mathrm{N}}_{{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)}(\Gamma)

\displaystyle\varrho_{\mathbb{M}}:

\displaystyle{\mathrm{N}}_{{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)}(\Gamma)\rightarrow{\mathrm{Diff}}(\mathbb{M})

\displaystyle\varrho_{{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{H})}:

\displaystyle{\mathrm{N}}_{{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)}(\Gamma)\rightarrow{\mathrm{SO}}({\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{H}))

\displaystyle\varrho_{\mathfrak{z}^{*}}:

\displaystyle{\mathrm{N}}_{{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)}(\Gamma)\rightarrow{\mathrm{GL}}(\mathfrak{z}^{*})

such that
\kappa\colon\mathbb{M}\rightarrow\Theta_{{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{H})}
and
\check{H}
are
{\mathrm{N}}_{{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)}(\Gamma)
-equivariant, and
\underline{\omega}_{\mathbb{M}}
and
{\pi_{\mathfrak{z}}F_{\mathbb{A}}}
are
{\mathrm{N}}_{{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)}(\Gamma)
-invariant. In particular, the action on the central fibre of
\kappa\colon\mathbb{M}\rightarrow\Theta_{{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{H})}
restricts to the action given by

\displaystyle\varrho_{\mathbb{H}/\Gamma}\colon{\mathrm{N}}_{{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)}(\Gamma)\rightarrow{\mathrm{N}}_{{\mathrm{SO}}(\mathbb{H})}(\Gamma)

and the action by
\varrho_{\mathfrak{z}^{*}}
preserves stratified structure of
\mathfrak{z}^{*}
, i.e. it preserves the structure of the Weyl-chambers.

Let

be a finite subgroup. A

-principal bundle

will be referred to as a twisting bundle.

Definition 4.10
.

We define the vector bundle

\displaystyle\mathfrak{P}\coloneqq

\displaystyle(Fr(X/S,\Phi)\times_{S}\mathfrak{C})\times_{{\mathrm{N}}_{{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)}(\Gamma)\times C}{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{H})\otimes\mathfrak{z}^{*}

\displaystyle(Fr(X/S,\Phi)\times_{S}\mathfrak{C})\times_{{\mathrm{N}}_{{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)}(\Gamma)\times C}\mathfrak{z}^{*}.

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}\colon\mathfrak{M}\coloneqq(Fr(X/S,\Phi)\times\mathfrak{C})\times_{{\mathrm{N}}_{{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)}(\Gamma)\times C}\mathbb{M}\rightarrow S.

The

is a fibre bundle map of fibre bundles over

.

the subset of walls in

.

H
on

Remark 27
.

Definition 4.11
.

We equip the bundle

\displaystyle g_{\mathfrak{M}}\coloneqq g_{\mathfrak{M}}^{2,0}+g_{\mathfrak{M}}^{0,2}=\mathfrak{m}^{*}g_{S}+[g_{\mathbb{M}}].

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}^{*}{\mathrm{vol}}_{S}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{6}\left<[\underline{\omega}_{\mathbb{M}}]\wedge[\underline{\omega}_{\mathbb{M}}]\right>,

and a function

\displaystyle w_{{\mathrm{AC}}}=[\mathfrak{w}_{{\mathrm{AC}}}].

The universal resolution map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{\mathbb{M}}:\mathbb{M}}

induces a
universal resolution map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rho_{\mathfrak{M}}}:\mathfrak{M}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{0}}

\lozenge

Notice, that the dilation action
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-action

\displaystyle\delta_{t}\colon\mathfrak{P}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P},\,\zeta\mapsto t^{2}\cdot\zeta

lifts to an action

\displaystyle\delta_{t}\colon\mathfrak{M}\rightarrow\mathfrak{M}

and

\displaystyle\delta_{t}^{*}\Phi_{\mathfrak{M}}=\Phi_{\mathfrak{M}}^{4,0}+t^{2}\cdot\Phi_{\mathfrak{M}}^{2,2}+t^{4}\cdot\Phi_{\mathfrak{M}}^{0,4}.

The following proposition describes ACF-
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structures of rate
-4
asymptotic to
(N_{0},\Phi_{0})
can be constructed via pulling back the universal moduli bundle
(\mathfrak{M},\Phi_{\mathfrak{M}})
via sections of
\mathfrak{P}
.

Proposition 4.10
.

Let
\zeta\in\Gamma(S,\mathfrak{P})\cong\Omega^{2}_{+}(S,\mathfrak{H})
. Then

\displaystyle(N_{\zeta},\Phi_{\zeta})=(\zeta^{*}\mathfrak{M},\zeta^{*}\Phi_{\mathfrak{M}})

is
\nu_{\zeta}:(N_{\zeta},g_{\zeta})\rightarrow(S,g_{S})
a Riemannian fibration, equipped with the
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structure
\Phi_{\zeta}
. Moreover, for
0\in\Omega^{2}_{+}(S,\mathfrak{H})
the bundle coincides with the normal cone bundle

\displaystyle\nu_{0}:(N_{0},\Phi_{0})\rightarrow(S,g_{S})

and
\zeta^{*}\rho_{\mathfrak{M}}=\rho_{\zeta}
equips
(N_{\zeta},\Phi_{\zeta})
with the structure of an ACF-
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structure

\displaystyle\rho_{\zeta}:(N_{\zeta},\Phi_{\zeta})\dashrightarrow(N_{0},\Phi_{0}).

of rate
-4
. Outside an exceptional set

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{\zeta}\coloneqq\rho_{\zeta}^{-1}(0_{S})

of codimension two the space
N_{\zeta}
is a truncated conical fibration over
\vartheta:Y\rightarrow S
.

Proof.

By
[
Joy00
, Thm. 8.2.3]
there exists an one form
\tau\in\Omega^{1}(\mathbb{H}/\Gamma,{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{H}))
such that

\displaystyle(\pi_{\zeta(s)})_{*}\underline{\omega}_{\zeta(s)}-\underline{\omega}_{0}={\mathrm{d}}\underline{\tau}_{\zeta(s)}

and

\displaystyle\nabla^{k}{\mathrm{d}}\underline{\tau}_{\zeta}=\mathcal{O}(r^{-4-k})

we can deduce that

\displaystyle(\rho_{\zeta})_{*}\Phi_{\zeta}-\Phi_{0}=\mathcal{O}(r^{-4})

as

\displaystyle(\rho_{\zeta})_{*}\hat{\Phi}_{\zeta}-\hat{\Phi}_{0}=

\displaystyle-\left<(\kappa_{0})^{*}\left[\theta_{S}\wedge\theta_{S}\right]_{+}\wedge(\rho_{\zeta})_{*}((\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}{\mathrm{d}}^{0,1}\underline{\tau})\right>

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{6}\left<\left(\tilde{0}^{*}\underline{\omega}_{\mathbb{M}}+(\rho_{\zeta})_{*}(\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}\underline{\omega}_{\mathbb{M}}\right)^{0,2,0}\wedge(\rho_{\zeta})_{*}(\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}({\mathrm{d}}^{0,1}\underline{\tau})\right>.

∎

We will now deduce that by scaling
\zeta\mapsto t^{2}\cdot\zeta
we obtain a family of ACF-
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structures, that converge in the Gromov-Hausdorff topology to the normal cone structure.

Proposition 4.11
.

The family of
(N_{t^{2}\cdot\zeta},\Phi_{t^{2}\cdot\zeta})\xrightarrow[GH]{t\to 0}(N_{0},\Phi_{0})
converges in the Gromov-Hausdorff topology.

Proof.

Notice that by our construction,
(N_{t^{2}\cdot\zeta},\Phi_{t^{2}\cdot\zeta})
is naturally a family of submanifolds in
\mathfrak{M}
. In particular, the Gromov-Hausdorff distance of
(N_{t^{2}\cdot\zeta},\Phi_{t^{2}\cdot\zeta})
to
(N_{0},\Phi_{0})
is proportional to

\displaystyle{\mathrm{d}}_{GH}\left\{(N_{t^{2}\cdot\zeta},\Phi_{t^{2}\cdot\zeta}),(N_{0},\Phi_{0})\right\}\sim\left|\left|t^{2}\cdot\zeta\right|\right|_{C^{0}}\lesssim t^{2}\to 0.

∎

Remark 28
.

The Ehresmann connections induced by
g_{\zeta}
and by
\zeta^{*}H=H_{\zeta}
coincide, and their curvature is given by

\displaystyle F_{H_{\zeta}}=\zeta^{*}F_{H}.

The above construction produces ACF fibrations via the pullback of sections of the parameter bundle
\mathfrak{P}
. The stratified nature of
\kappa:\mathfrak{M}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}
causes
(N_{\zeta},\Phi_{\zeta})
to be potentially singular. The following proposition describes how the smooth (stratified) structure
(N_{\zeta},\Phi_{\zeta})
dependence on the intersection properties of
{\mathrm{im}}(\zeta)
and
\mathfrak{W}\subset\mathfrak{P}
.

Proposition 4.12

(Wall-Crossing Formulae
m=4
)
.

The space
N_{\zeta}
carries a smooth structure depending on the intersection of the image of
\zeta
and the wall
\mathfrak{W}\subset\mathfrak{P}

(i)

Let us now assume that
{\mathrm{im}}(\zeta)\subset\mathfrak{P}\backslash\mathfrak{W}
. Then the space
(N_{\zeta},g_{\zeta})
is a smooth Riemannian manifold.

(ii)

If
{\mathrm{im}}(\zeta)
intersects
\mathfrak{W}
transversely,
(N_{\zeta},g_{\zeta})
is a stratified Riemannian space.

Proof.

The spaces
\mathfrak{M}
and
\mathfrak{P}
are stratified spaces. If the image of
\zeta
intersects the strata of
\mathfrak{P}
transversally, i.e. for all strata
\mathfrak{W}^{i}\subset\mathfrak{P}
, the section
\zeta:\zeta^{-1}(\mathfrak{W}^{i})\rightarrow\mathfrak{W}^{i}
is transverse, we can define a stratification on
S
by declaring
\zeta
to be a map of stratified spaces. Now,
\mathfrak{k}:\mathfrak{M}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}
is a map of stratified spaces and hence, the pullback
\zeta^{*}\mathfrak{M}
is a stratified space.
∎

Recall that
\mathfrak{W}\subset\mathfrak{P}
is a stratum of codimension three. As such the smooth structure of
(N_{\zeta},\Phi_{\zeta})
does not depend on the section
\zeta\in\Gamma(S,\mathfrak{P})
as long as
{\mathrm{im}}(\zeta)\subset\mathfrak{P}\backslash\mathfrak{W}
.

Lemma 4.14
.

If
\zeta,\zeta^{\prime}\in\Gamma(S,\mathfrak{P})
such that
N_{\zeta}
and
N_{\zeta^{\prime}}
are smooth, then
N_{\zeta}\cong N_{\zeta`}
.

Proof.

Notice that the fibres of
\mathbb{M}|_{\Theta_{{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{H})\backslash{\mathcal{W}}}}
are all diffeomorphic. As further

\displaystyle\pi_{0}\left([S,\mathfrak{P}\backslash\mathfrak{W}]_{\mathfrak{P}}\right)=1

we deduce the statement.
∎

Remark 29
.

Notice that the Levi-Civita connection on
S
induces a torsion-free connection
\varphi
on the
{\mathrm{N}}_{{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)}(\Gamma)\times C
-principal bundle
Fr(X/S,\Phi)\times\mathfrak{C}
.

The following theorem establishes the precise relationship between the harmonicity of a section

\displaystyle\zeta\in\Gamma(S,\mathfrak{P})\cong\Omega^{2}_{+}(S,\mathfrak{H})

and the adiabatic torsion-freeness of the asymptotically conically fibred
\mathrm{Spin}(7)
-structure
(N_{\zeta},\Phi_{\zeta})
.

Theorem 4.8
.

The torsion of the
\mathrm{Spin}(7)
-structure
\Phi_{\zeta}
on
N_{\zeta}
satisfies

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\Phi_{\zeta}={\mathrm{d}}^{1,0}\Phi^{0,4}_{\zeta}+{\mathrm{d}}^{1,0}\Phi_{\zeta}+{\mathrm{d}}^{2,-1}\Phi_{\zeta}^{2,2}+\mathrm{d}^{2,-1}\Phi_{\zeta}^{0,4}.

If
\mathrm{d}_{\varphi}\zeta=0
, i.e. harmonic then

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\Phi_{\zeta}=\mathrm{d}^{2,-1}\Phi_{\zeta}.

Remark 30
.

For all
\zeta
,
{\mathrm{d}}^{1,0}\Phi^{0,4}_{\zeta}=k_{\zeta}\wedge\Phi^{0,4}_{\zeta}
and hence,
\zeta
being harmonic implies that the fibres of
\nu_{\zeta}
are minimal submanifolds. If in addition
\nabla^{\varphi}\zeta=0
, then the fibres of the fibration
\nu_{\zeta}:(N_{\zeta},g_{\zeta})\to(S,g_{S})
are totally geodesic.

Before we prove this statement, we will first point out the following corollary of Theorem
4.8
.

As mentioned at the beginning of this chapter, the notion of adiabatic torsion-free
\mathrm{Spin}(7)
-structures is closely related to the adiabatic kernel of the Hodge-de Rham operator associated to
(N_{\zeta},\Phi_{\zeta})
. In particular, Theorem
4.8
implies the following corollary.

Corollary 4.7
.

If
\zeta\in\Gamma(S,\mathfrak{P})
satisfies

\displaystyle{\mathrm{d}}_{\varphi}\zeta=0

then
\Phi_{\zeta}
defines a nowhere vanishing element in the adiabatic harmonic with respect to itself, i.e.

\displaystyle\Phi_{\zeta}\in\mathfrak{Ker}_{{\mathrm{ACF}};0}\left(\widehat{D}^{0}_{\zeta}\right).

We will now proceed to prove Theorem
4.8
.

Proof.

(Theorem
4.8
)
We define
\overline{\zeta}\in\Omega^{0}(Fr(X/S,\Phi)\times_{S}\mathfrak{C},\Theta_{{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{H})})^{{\mathrm{N}}_{{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)}(\Gamma)\times C}

\displaystyle\zeta=\left<\overline{\zeta},\left[\theta_{S}\wedge\theta_{S}\right]_{+}\right>

.

We will lift the forms from

Now, consider the pullback of

{\mathrm{d}}=\begin{cases}{\mathrm{d}}^{1,0}&={\mathrm{d}}^{1,0,0}+{\mathrm{d}}^{0,1,0}+{\mathrm{d}}^{2,-1,0}\\

We identify

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{6}\left<((\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}\underline{\omega}_{\mathbb{M}})^{0,2,0}\wedge((\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}\underline{\omega}_{\mathbb{M}})^{0,2,0}\right>.

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0

To see that the harmonicity of

\displaystyle{\mathrm{d}}_{\varphi}\zeta=

\displaystyle{\mathrm{d}}^{1,0}\left<\left[\theta_{S}\wedge\theta_{S}\right]_{+},\overline{\zeta}\right>=\left<\left[\theta_{S}\wedge\theta_{S}\right]_{+}\wedge(\overline{\zeta}^{*}\theta_{\Theta_{{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{H})}})^{1,0}\right>.

where

\displaystyle{\mathrm{d}}^{1,0,0}((\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}\underline{\omega}_{\mathbb{M}})^{0,2,0}=

\displaystyle({\mathrm{d}}(\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}\underline{\omega}_{\mathbb{M}})^{1,2,0}-{\mathrm{d}}^{0,1,0}((\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}\underline{\omega}_{\mathbb{M}})^{1,1,0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left((\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}\left<\kappa^{*}\theta_{\Theta_{{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{H})}}\wedge\pi_{\mathfrak{z}}F_{\mathbb{A}}\right>\right)^{1,2,0}-{\mathrm{d}}^{0,1,0}((\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}\underline{\omega}_{\mathbb{M}})^{1,1,0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left<\overline{\zeta}^{*}(\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}\theta_{\Theta_{{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{H})}}\wedge(\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}\pi_{\mathfrak{z}}F_{\mathbb{A}}\right>^{1,2,0}-{\mathrm{d}}^{0,1,0}((\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}\underline{\omega}_{\mathbb{M}})^{1,1,0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left<(\overline{\zeta}^{*}(\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}\theta_{\Theta_{{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{H})}})^{1,0,0}\wedge((\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}\pi_{\mathfrak{z}}F_{\mathbb{A}})^{0,2,0}\right>

\displaystyle-\left<(\overline{\zeta}^{*}(\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}\theta_{\Theta_{{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{H})}})^{0,1,0}\wedge((\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}\pi_{\mathfrak{z}}F_{\mathbb{A}})^{1,1,0}\right>

\displaystyle-{\mathrm{d}}^{0,1,0}((\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}\underline{\omega}_{\mathbb{M}})^{1,1,0}

whereby

\displaystyle{\mathrm{d}}^{0,1,0}((\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}\underline{\omega}_{\mathbb{M}})^{1,1,0}=-\left<(\overline{\zeta}^{*}(\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}\theta_{\Theta_{{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{H})}})^{0,1,0}\wedge((\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}\pi_{\mathfrak{z}}F_{\mathbb{A}})^{1,1,0}\right>

and

\displaystyle{\mathrm{d}}^{0,1,0}((\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}\underline{\omega}_{\mathbb{M}})^{0,2,0}=

\displaystyle({\mathrm{d}}(\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}\underline{\omega}_{\mathbb{M}})^{0,3,0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left<(\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}\left(\kappa^{*}\theta_{\Theta_{{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{H})}}\wedge\pi_{\mathfrak{z}}F_{\mathbb{A}}\right)\right>^{0,3,0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left<(\overline{\zeta}^{*}(\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}\theta_{\Theta_{{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{H})}})^{0,1,0}\wedge((\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}\pi_{\mathfrak{z}}F_{\mathbb{A}})^{0,2,0}\right>.

Hence, we deduce that

\displaystyle({\mathrm{d}}^{1,0,0}+{\mathrm{d}}^{0,1,0})((\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}\underline{\omega}_{\mathbb{M}})^{0,2,0}=

\displaystyle\left<(\overline{\zeta}^{*}\kappa)^{*}(\overline{\zeta}^{*}\theta_{\Theta_{{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{H})}})^{1,0}\wedge((\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}\pi_{\mathfrak{z}}F_{\mathbb{A}})^{0,2,0}\right>.

Further, as the pull back of the Soldering form satisfies

\displaystyle{\mathrm{d}}(\overline{\zeta}^{*}\kappa)^{*}\left[\theta_{S}\wedge\theta_{S}\right]_{+}=

\displaystyle(\overline{\zeta}^{*}\kappa)^{*}{\mathrm{d}}\left[\theta_{S}\wedge\theta_{S}\right]_{+}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\overline{\zeta}^{*}\kappa)^{*}[\varphi\overset{\circ}{\wedge}\left[\theta_{S}\wedge\theta_{S}\right]_{+}]\in\Omega^{2,1}(\overline{\zeta}^{*}\mathbb{M},{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{H}))^{{\mathrm{N}}_{{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)}(\Gamma)}

we deduce, that if
\zeta
is harmonic

\displaystyle\psi_{\zeta}^{*}{\mathrm{d}}\Phi^{2,2}_{\zeta}=

\displaystyle-\left<(\overline{\zeta}^{*}\kappa)^{*}\left[\theta_{S}\wedge\theta_{S}\right]_{+}\wedge\left<(\overline{\zeta}^{*}\kappa)^{*}(\overline{\zeta}^{*}\theta_{\Theta_{{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{H})}})^{1,0}\wedge((\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}\pi_{\mathfrak{z}}F_{\mathbb{A}})^{0,2,0}\right>\right>

\displaystyle+\psi^{*}_{\zeta}{\mathrm{d}}^{2,-1}\Phi^{2,2}_{\zeta}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left<(\overline{\zeta}^{*}\kappa)^{*}\underbrace{\left<\left[\theta_{S}\wedge\theta_{S}\right]_{+}\wedge(\overline{\zeta}^{*}\theta_{\Theta_{{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{H})}})^{1,0}\right>}_{{\mathrm{d}}_{\varphi}\zeta=0}\wedge((\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}\pi_{\mathfrak{z}}F_{\mathbb{A}})^{0,2,0}\right>+\psi^{*}_{\zeta}{\mathrm{d}}^{2,-1}\Phi^{2,2}_{\zeta}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\psi^{*}_{\zeta}{\mathrm{d}}^{2,-1}\Phi^{2,2}_{\zeta}

Further,

\displaystyle\psi_{\zeta}^{*}{\mathrm{d}}^{1,0}\Phi_{\zeta}^{0,4}=

\displaystyle{\mathrm{d}}^{1,0,0}\hat{\Phi}_{\zeta}^{0,4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{6}{\mathrm{d}}^{1,0,0}\left<((\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}\underline{\omega}_{\mathbb{M}})^{0,2,0}\wedge((\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}\underline{\omega}_{\mathbb{M}})^{0,2,0}\right>

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}\left<{\mathrm{d}}^{1,0,0}((\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}\underline{\omega}_{\mathbb{M}})^{0,2,0}\wedge((\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}\underline{\omega}_{\mathbb{M}})^{0,2,0}\right>

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{3}\left<\left<(\overline{\zeta}^{*}\kappa)^{*}(\overline{\zeta}^{*}\theta_{\Theta_{{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{H})}})^{1,0}\wedge((\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}\pi_{\mathfrak{z}}F_{\mathbb{A}})^{0,2,0}\right>\wedge((\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}\underline{\omega}_{\mathbb{M}})^{0,2,0}\right>

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{3}\left<(\overline{\zeta}^{*}\kappa)^{*}(\overline{\zeta}^{*}\theta_{\Theta_{{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{H})}})^{1,0}\wedge\left<((\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}\pi_{\mathfrak{z}}F_{\mathbb{A}})^{0,2,0}\wedge((\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}\underline{\omega}_{\mathbb{M}})^{0,2,0}\right>\right>

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{3}\left<(\overline{\zeta}^{*}\kappa)^{*}(\overline{\zeta}^{*}\theta_{\Theta_{{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{H})}})^{1,0}\wedge\left<((\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}(\underbrace{\pi_{\mathfrak{z}}F_{\mathbb{A}}\wedge\underline{\omega}_{\mathbb{M}}}_{=0}))^{0,4,0}\right>\right>

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0

vanishes as a consequence of
F_{\mathbb{A}}
being fibrewise ASD.
∎

Remark 31
.

The remaining terms of the torsion of the
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structure
\Phi_{\zeta}
are

\displaystyle\psi_{\zeta}^{*}{\mathrm{d}}^{2,-1}\Phi^{2,2}_{\zeta}=

\displaystyle{\mathrm{d}}^{2,-1,0}\hat{\Phi}^{2,2}_{\zeta}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\iota_{\psi_{\zeta}^{*}F_{H_{\zeta}}}\left<(\overline{\zeta}^{*}\kappa)^{*}\left[\theta_{S}\wedge\theta_{S}\right]_{+}\wedge((\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}\underline{\omega}_{\mathbb{M}})^{0,2,0}\right>

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left<(\overline{\zeta}^{*}\kappa)^{*}\left[\theta_{S}\wedge\theta_{S}\right]_{+}\wedge(\iota_{\psi_{\zeta}^{*}F_{H_{\zeta}}}(\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}\underline{\omega}_{\mathbb{M}})^{0,2,0}\right>

and

\displaystyle\psi_{\zeta}^{*}{\mathrm{d}}^{2,-1}\Phi^{0,4}_{\zeta}=

\displaystyle{\mathrm{d}}^{2,-1,0}\hat{\Phi}^{0,4}_{\zeta}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\iota_{\psi_{\zeta}^{*}F_{H_{\zeta}}}\frac{1}{6}\left<((\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}\underline{\omega}_{\mathbb{M}})^{0,2,0}\wedge((\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}\underline{\omega}_{\mathbb{M}})^{0,2,0}\right>

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}\left<\iota_{\psi_{\zeta}^{*}F_{H_{\zeta}}}(\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}\underline{\omega}_{\mathbb{M}}\wedge((\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}\underline{\omega}_{\mathbb{M}})^{0,2,0}\right>.

Corollary 4.8
.

Notice that whenever
\zeta
satisfies
{\mathrm{d}}_{\varphi}\zeta=0
, then so does
t^{2}\cdot\zeta
. In the following we will write

\displaystyle\Phi^{t}_{\zeta}:=\delta^{*}_{t}\Phi_{t^{2}\cdot\zeta}=\Phi^{4,0}_{\zeta}+t^{2}\cdot\Phi^{2,2}_{\zeta}+t^{4}\cdot\Phi^{0,4}_{\zeta}.

This adiabatic torsion-free
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structure is ACF to
(N_{0},\delta_{t}^{*}\Phi_{0})
.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(N_{\zeta},\Phi^{t}_{\zeta})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(N_{t^{2}\cdot\zeta},\Phi_{t^{2}\cdot\zeta})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(N_{0},\Phi^{t}_{0})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(N_{0},\Phi_{0})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{t}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{\zeta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{t^{2}\cdot\zeta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{t}}

Consequently,
\Phi_{\zeta}
is adiabatic torsion-free, i.e.

\displaystyle{\mathrm{d}}\Phi^{t}_{\zeta}={\mathrm{d}}^{2,-1}\left(t^{4}\cdot\Phi^{2,2}_{\zeta}+t^{4}\cdot\Phi^{0,4}_{\zeta}\right)=(\delta_{t})^{*}{\mathrm{d}}\Phi_{t^{2}\cdot\zeta}

and if
{\mathrm{d}}_{\varphi}\zeta=0
, then

\displaystyle|{\mathrm{d}}\Phi^{t}_{\zeta}|_{g^{t}_{\zeta}}=t\cdot|{\mathrm{d}}^{2,-1}\Phi^{2,2}_{\zeta}|_{g_{\zeta}}\xrightarrow[t\to 0]{}0

Finally, we use the Lerray-Serre spectral sequence to compute the cohomology of the space
N_{\zeta}
.

Proposition 4.13
.

The cohomology of
N_{\zeta}
is given by

\displaystyle{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(N_{\zeta})=

\displaystyle{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}({\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(S,\underline{\mathbb{R}}[0]\oplus\mathcal{H}^{2}_{-}(N_{\zeta}/S),{\mathrm{d}}^{3,-2}).

Moreover, if the differential
{\mathrm{d}}^{3,-2}
in the Leray-Serre spectral sequence vanishes then

\displaystyle{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(N_{\zeta})={\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(S)\oplus{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet-2}(S,\mathcal{H}^{2}_{-}(N_{\zeta}/S)).

4.5.2.
Adiabatic Resolutions of Codimension Six Singularities

Let
S\subset X
be a singular stratum of codimension six and isotropy
\Gamma\subset{\mathrm{SU}}(3)
and let

\displaystyle\nu_{0}\colon(N_{0},\Phi_{0})\rightarrow(S,g_{S})

denote its normal cone bundle,
(Y,g_{Y;r})
its CF-link and let
Fr(X/S,\Phi)
denote its
{\mathrm{N}}_{{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)}(\Gamma)
-reduction of the
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-principal bundle of frames to
S
. In the following we will construct a family of fibrations over Riemann surface admitting an adiabatic
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structure, such that the fibres are ALE-Calabi-Yau
3
-folds. We assume all Conjecture made in Section
4.4.2
.

In Section
4.4.2
we established that there exist representations of the group
{\mathrm{N}}_{{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)}(\Gamma)

\displaystyle\varrho_{\mathbb{M}}:

\displaystyle{\mathrm{N}}_{{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)}(\Gamma)\rightarrow{\mathrm{Diff}}(\mathbb{M})

\displaystyle\varrho_{\Theta_{{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{C})}}:

\displaystyle{\mathrm{N}}_{{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)}(\Gamma)\rightarrow{\mathrm{GL}}(\Theta_{{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{C})})

such that
\kappa\colon\mathbb{M}\rightarrow\Theta_{{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{C})}
and
\check{H}
are
{\mathrm{N}}_{{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)}(\Gamma)
-equivariant and
\omega
and
{\pi_{\mathfrak{z}}F_{\mathbb{A}}}
are
{\mathrm{N}}_{{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)}(\Gamma)
-invariant. In particular, the action on the central fibre of
\kappa\colon\mathbb{M}\rightarrow\Theta_{{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{C})}
restricts to the action given by

\displaystyle\varrho_{\mathbb{C}^{3}/\Gamma}:{\mathrm{N}}_{{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)}(\Gamma)\rightarrow{\mathrm{N}}_{{\mathrm{SO}}(\mathbb{C}^{3})}(\Gamma)

and the action by
\varrho_{\Theta_{{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{C})}}
preserves stratified structure of
\Theta_{{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{C})}
, i.e. it preserves the structure of the chambers.

Let
C={\mathrm{C}}_{\mathbb{W}}({\mathrm{im}}(\varrho_{\Theta_{{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{C})}}))\subset\mathbb{W}
and
c\colon\mathfrak{C}\rightarrow S
a
C
-principal bundle.

Definition 4.12
.

We define the vector bundle

\displaystyle\mathfrak{P}\coloneqq

\displaystyle(Fr(X/S,\Phi)\times_{S}\mathfrak{C})\times_{{\mathrm{N}}_{{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)}(\Gamma)\times C}\Theta_{{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{C})}

We further define the fibre bundle

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}\colon\mathfrak{M}\coloneqq(Fr(X/S,\Phi)\times\mathfrak{C})\times_{{\mathrm{N}}_{{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)}(\Gamma)\times C}\mathbb{M}\rightarrow S.

The
{\mathrm{N}}_{{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)}(\Gamma)\times C
-equivariant map

induces the fibre bundle map

\displaystyle\mathfrak{k}\colon\mathfrak{M}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}

with the structure of a fibre bundle, i.e.
\mathfrak{k}
is a fibre bundle map of fibre bundles over
S
.

\lozenge

Remark 32
.

There exists stratified subspace

\displaystyle\underline{0}\subset\mathfrak{W}\coloneqq(Fr(X/S,\Phi)\times_{S}\mathfrak{C})\times_{{\mathrm{N}}_{{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)}(\Gamma)\times C}\mathcal{W}\subset\mathfrak{P}.

Remark 33
.

The
{\mathrm{N}}_{{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)}(\Gamma)\times C
-invariant Ehresmann connection

on

induces an Ehresmann connection
H
on

\displaystyle\mathfrak{k}\colon\mathfrak{M}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}.

Definition 4.13
.

We equip the bundle
\mathfrak{k}\colon\mathfrak{M}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}
with the structure of a Riemannian fibration by

\displaystyle g_{\mathfrak{M}}\coloneqq g_{\mathfrak{M}}^{2,0}+g_{\mathfrak{M}}^{0,2}=\mathfrak{m}^{*}g_{S}+[g_{\mathbb{M}}].

Further, there exists a four form

\displaystyle\Phi_{\mathfrak{M}}\coloneqq\Phi_{\mathfrak{M}}^{2,2}+\Phi_{\mathfrak{M}}^{1,3}+\Phi_{\mathfrak{M}}^{0,4}

given by

\displaystyle\Phi_{\mathfrak{M}}^{2,2}\coloneqq

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}^{*}(\theta_{S}\wedge\overline{\theta}_{S})\wedge[\omega_{\mathbb{M};RF}]

\displaystyle\Phi_{\mathfrak{M}}^{1,3}\coloneqq

\displaystyle\Phi_{\mathfrak{M}}^{0,4}\coloneqq

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\cdot[\omega_{\mathbb{M};RF}]\wedge[\omega_{\mathbb{M};RF}],

and a function

\displaystyle w_{{\mathrm{AC}}}=[\mathfrak{w}_{{\mathrm{AC}}}].

The universal resolution map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{\mathbb{M}}:\mathbb{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}^{3}/\Gamma}

induces a
universal resolution map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{\mathfrak{M}}:\mathfrak{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{0}}

\lozenge

Notice, that the dilation action
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-action

\displaystyle\delta_{t}\colon\mathfrak{P}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P},\,\zeta\mapsto t^{2}\cdot\zeta

lifts to an action

\displaystyle\delta_{t}\colon\mathfrak{M}\rightarrow\mathfrak{M}

and

\displaystyle\delta_{t}^{*}\Phi_{\mathfrak{M}}=t^{2}\Phi_{\mathfrak{M}}^{2,2}+t^{3}\cdot\Phi_{\mathfrak{M}}^{1,3}+t^{4}\cdot\Phi_{\mathfrak{M}}^{0,4}.

The following proposition describes how ACF-
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structures of rate
-6
asymptotic to
(N_{0},\Phi_{0})
can be constructed via pulling back the universal moduli bundle
(\mathfrak{M},\Phi_{\mathfrak{M}})

.

Proposition 4.14
.

Let
\zeta\in\Gamma(S,\mathfrak{P})
. Then

\displaystyle(N_{\zeta},\Phi_{\zeta})=(\zeta^{*}\mathfrak{M},\zeta^{*}\Phi_{\mathfrak{M}})

is
\nu_{\zeta}:(N_{\zeta},g_{\zeta})\rightarrow(S,g_{S})
a Riemannian fibration, equipped with the
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structure
\Phi_{\zeta}
. Moreover, for
0\in\Gamma(S,\mathfrak{P})
the bundle coincides with the normal cone bundle

\displaystyle\nu_{0}:(N_{0},\Phi_{0})\rightarrow(S,g_{S})

and
\zeta^{*}\rho_{\mathfrak{M}}=\rho_{\zeta}
equips
(N_{\zeta},\Phi_{\zeta})
with the structure of an ACF-
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structure

\displaystyle\rho_{\zeta}:(N_{\zeta},\Phi_{\zeta})\dashrightarrow(N_{0},\Phi_{0}).

of rate
-6
. Outside an exceptional set

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{\zeta}\coloneqq\rho_{\zeta}^{-1}(0_{S})

of codimension two, the space

is a truncated cone fibration over
\vartheta:Y\rightarrow S
.

We will now deduce that by scaling
\zeta\mapsto t^{2}\cdot\zeta
we obtain a family of ACF-
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structures, that converge in the Gromov-Hausdorff topology to the normal cone structure.

Proposition 4.15
.

The family
(N_{t^{2}\cdot\zeta},\Phi_{t^{2}\cdot\zeta})\xrightarrow[GH]{t\to 0}(N_{0},\Phi_{0})
converges in the Gromov-Hausdorff topology.

Remark 34
.

The Ehresmann connections induced by
g_{\zeta}
and by
\zeta^{*}H=H_{\zeta}
coincide and their curvature is given by

\displaystyle F_{H_{\zeta}}=\zeta^{*}F_{H}.

The above construction produces ACF fibrations via the pullback of sections of the parameter bundle
\mathfrak{P}
. The stratified nature of
\kappa:\mathfrak{M}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}
causes
(N_{\zeta},\Phi_{\zeta})
to be potentially singular. The following proposition describes how the smooth (stratified) structure
(N_{\zeta},\Phi_{\zeta})
dependence on the intersection properties of
{\mathrm{im}}(\zeta)
and
\mathfrak{W}\subset\mathfrak{P}
.

Proposition 4.16

(Wall-Crossing Formulae
m=6
)
.

The space
N_{\zeta}
carries a smooth structure depending on the intersection of the image of
\zeta
and the wall
\mathfrak{W}\subset\mathfrak{P}

(i)

Let us assume that
{\mathrm{im}}(\zeta)\subset\mathfrak{P}\backslash\mathfrak{W}
. Then the space
(N_{\zeta},g_{\zeta})
is a smooth Riemannian manifold.

(ii)

If
{\mathrm{im}}(\zeta)
intersects
\mathfrak{W}
transversely,
(N_{\zeta},g_{\zeta})
is a stratified Riemannian space.

The following theorem establishes the precise relationship between the section

\displaystyle\zeta\in\Gamma(S,\mathfrak{P})

being parallel and the adiabatic torsion-freeness of the asymptotically conically fibred
\mathrm{Spin}(7)
-structure
(N_{\zeta},\Phi_{\zeta})
.

Theorem 4.9
.

The torsion of the
\mathrm{Spin}(7)
-structure
\Phi_{\zeta}
satisfies

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\Phi_{\zeta}=\mathrm{d}^{1,0}\Phi_{\zeta}^{1,3}+\mathrm{d}^{1,0}\Phi_{\zeta}^{0,4}+\mathrm{d}^{2,-1}\Phi_{\zeta}^{0,4}.

If the section
\zeta
satisfies
\nabla_{\varphi}\zeta=0
, then

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}^{1,0}\Phi_{\zeta}^{1,3}=0\hskip 28.45274pt\text{and}\hskip 28.45274pt\mathrm{d}^{1,0}\Phi_{\zeta}^{0,4}=0.

Remark 35
.

Since
{\mathrm{d}}^{1,0,0}((\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta}^{*}\omega_{\mathbb{M};RF})^{0,2,0}=0
holds if
\nabla_{\varphi}\zeta=0
, we deduce that the fibres of
\nu_{\zeta}:(N_{\zeta},g_{\zeta})\rightarrow(S,g_{S})
are totally geodesic.

Similar to the Corollary
4.7
we deduce that
\nabla_{\varphi}\zeta=0
implies that
\Phi_{\zeta}
is adiabatic harmonic with respect to itself.

Corollary 4.9
.

If
\zeta\in\Gamma(S,\mathfrak{P})
satisfies

\displaystyle\nabla_{\varphi}\zeta=0

then
\Phi_{\zeta}
defines a nowhere vanishing element in

\displaystyle\Phi_{\zeta}\in\mathfrak{Ker}_{{\mathrm{ACF}};0}\left(\widehat{D}^{0}_{\Phi_{\zeta}}\right).

In order to prove this Proposition, we will lift forms from
N_{\zeta}
to the bundle
\overline{\zeta}^{*}\mathbb{M}
, where
\overline{\zeta}
denotes the
{\mathrm{N}}_{{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)}(\Gamma)\times C
-equivariant lift of
\zeta
.

There are at most
\pi_{0}([S,\mathfrak{P}\backslash{W}])

{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structure
