We will now proceed to compute the torsion of
\Phi_{\zeta}
and prove that it satisfies
{\mathrm{d}}\Phi_{\zeta}={\mathrm{d}}^{2,-1}\Phi^{0,4}_{\zeta}
whenever
\nabla_{\varphi}\zeta=0
. We compute

\displaystyle{\mathrm{d}}_{\varphi}\zeta=

\displaystyle{\mathrm{d}}^{1,0}\overline{\zeta}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\overline{\zeta}^{*}\theta_{\Theta_{{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{C})}})^{1,0}

and deduce that

\displaystyle{\mathrm{d}}^{1,0,0}((\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}\omega_{\mathbb{M};RF})^{0,2,0}=

\displaystyle((\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}{\mathrm{d}}\omega_{\mathbb{M};RF})^{1,2,0}-{\mathrm{d}}^{0,1,0}((\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}\omega_{\mathbb{M};RF})^{1,1,0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left<(\kappa^{*}_{\zeta}\overline{\zeta}^{*}\theta_{\Theta_{{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{C})}})^{1,0,0}\wedge({\Lambda_{\mathbb{M};RF}})^{0,2,0}\right>,

\displaystyle{\mathrm{d}}^{0,1,0}((\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}\omega_{\mathbb{M};RF})^{0,2,0}=

\displaystyle((\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}{\mathrm{d}}\omega_{\mathbb{M};RF})^{0,3,0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left<(\kappa^{*}_{\zeta}\overline{\zeta}^{*}\theta_{\Theta_{{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{C})}})^{0,1,0}\wedge({\Lambda_{\mathbb{M};RF}})^{0,2,0}\right>,

\displaystyle{\mathrm{d}}^{1,0,0}(\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}{\theta_{\mathbb{M};RF}}=

\displaystyle({\mathrm{d}}(\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}{\theta_{\mathbb{M};RF}})^{1,3,0}-{\mathrm{d}}^{0,1,0}((\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}{\theta_{\mathbb{M};RF}})^{1,2,0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left<(\kappa^{*}_{\zeta}\overline{\zeta}^{*}\theta_{\Theta_{{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{C})}})^{1,0,0}\wedge({\Xi_{\mathbb{M};RF}})^{0,3,0}\right>,

and

\displaystyle{\mathrm{d}}^{0,1,0}(\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}{\theta_{\mathbb{M};RF}}=

\displaystyle({\mathrm{d}}(\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}{\theta_{\mathbb{M};RF}})^{0,4,0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left<(\kappa^{*}_{\zeta}\overline{\zeta}^{*}\theta_{\Theta_{{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{C})}})^{0,1,0}\wedge({\Xi_{\mathbb{M};RF}})^{0,3,0}\right>.

Hence, if
{\mathrm{d}}^{1,0}\overline{\zeta}=0
we deduce that

\displaystyle\psi_{\zeta}^{*}{\mathrm{d}}\Phi^{2,2}_{\zeta}=

\displaystyle(\overline{\zeta}^{*}\kappa)^{*}(\theta_{S}\wedge\overline{\theta}_{S})\wedge\left<\underbrace{\kappa^{*}_{\zeta}(\overline{\zeta}^{*}\theta_{\Theta_{{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{C})}})^{1,0}}_{=0}\wedge({\Lambda_{\mathbb{M};RF}})^{0,2,0}\right>

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0

and

\displaystyle\psi_{\zeta}^{*}{\mathrm{d}}\Phi^{1,3}_{\zeta}=

\displaystyle{\mathrm{Re}}((\overline{\zeta}^{*}\kappa)^{*}\theta_{S}\wedge\left<\underbrace{\kappa^{*}_{\zeta}(\overline{\zeta}^{*}\theta_{\Theta_{{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{C})}})^{1,0}}_{=0}\wedge({\Xi_{\mathbb{M};RF}})^{0,3,0}\right>

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0

and

\displaystyle\psi_{\zeta}^{*}{\mathrm{d}}\Phi^{0,4}_{\zeta}=

\displaystyle\left<(\overline{\zeta}^{*}\kappa)^{*}(\overline{\zeta}^{*}\theta_{\Theta_{{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{C})}})^{1,0}\wedge\left<((\kappa^{*}\overline{\zeta})^{*}(\underbrace{\Lambda_{\mathbb{M};RF}\wedge\omega_{\mathbb{M};RF}}_{=0})^{0,4,0}\right>\right>+{\mathrm{d}}^{2,-1}\Phi^{0,4}_{\zeta}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathrm{d}}^{2,-1}\Phi^{0,4}_{\zeta}

∎

Remark 36
.

The remaining terms of the torsion of the adiabatic torsion-free
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structure are

\displaystyle{\mathrm{d}}\Phi_{\zeta}={\mathrm{d}}^{2,-1}\Phi_{\zeta}

Corollary 4.10
.

Notice that whenever
\zeta
satisfies
{\mathrm{d}}_{\varphi}\zeta=0
then so does
t\cdot\zeta
. In the following we will write

\displaystyle\Phi^{t}_{\zeta}:=\Phi_{t\cdot\zeta}=t^{2}\Phi^{2,2}_{\zeta}+t^{3}\cdot\Phi^{1,3}_{\zeta}+t^{4}\cdot\Phi^{0,4}_{\zeta}.

This adiabatic torsion-free
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structure is ACF to
(N_{0},\delta_{t}^{*}\Phi_{0})
.

Finally, we use the Lerray-Serre spectral sequence to compute the cohomology of the space
N_{\zeta}
.

Proposition 4.17
.

The Lerray-Serre spectral sequence collapses on the second page. Consequently, the cohomology of
N_{\zeta}
is given by

\displaystyle{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(N_{\zeta})=

\displaystyle{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(S,\underline{\mathbb{R}}[0]\oplus\mathcal{H}^{2}_{-}(N_{\zeta}/S)).

Proof.

The differential
{\mathrm{d}}^{2,-1}
on the second page vanishes as
{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(N_{\zeta}/S)
is only supported in even degrees. The higher differentials
{\mathrm{d}}^{n,1-n}
vanish because
n>{\mathrm{dim}}(S)
.
∎

4.5.3.
Adiabatic Resolutions of Codimension Seven Singularities

Let us assume that
S
is a connected singular strata of codimension seven, i.e.
S
is a copy of
\mathbb{S}^{1}
in
X
. Consequently, the bundle
NS
is trivialisable.

Proposition 4.18
.

We fix a trivialisation
NS\cong\mathbb{S}^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{7}
. Let us assume that there exists a space of ALE space
\pi:Y\dashrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{7}/\Gamma
that carries a closed
{\mathrm{G}}_{2}
-structure
\varphi
. Then the

\displaystyle\Phi^{t}=t^{3}\cdot{\mathrm{d}}\theta\wedge\varphi+t^{4}*_{g_{\varphi}}\varphi

is adiabatic torsion-free
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structure.

4.5.4.
Adiabatic Resolutions of Codimension Eight Singularities

Let us assume that
S
is a connected singular strata of codimension eight, i.e.
S
consists of a single point and
X
is conically singular at
S\in X
.

Corollary 4.11
.

An adiabatic torsion-free
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structure over a type A singular
\mathbb{R}^{8}/\Gamma
is a
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-ALE space
(N_{\zeta};t^{4}\cdot\Phi_{\zeta})
.

In the following we will show how to resolve various simple types of codimension eight singularities. These singularities can be resolved by special QALE spaces, which are of product type. Assume that the local model of
X
around the singular strata
S\cong\{pt\}
is given by
W/\Gamma=\mathbb{H}/\Gamma_{1}\oplus\mathbb{H}/\Gamma_{2}
.

Lemma 4.16
.

Let
(\zeta_{1}\oplus\zeta_{2})\in\Theta_{1;{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{H})}^{reg}\oplus\Theta_{2;{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{H})}^{reg}
. Then there exists a resolution

\displaystyle\pi:M_{\zeta_{1}}\times M_{\zeta_{2}}\dashrightarrow W/\Gamma

equipped with a torsion-free QALE
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structure

\displaystyle\Phi^{t}_{\zeta_{12}}=t^{4}\cdot\left({\mathrm{vol}}_{\zeta_{1}}+{\mathrm{vol}}_{\zeta_{1}}-{\mathrm{tr}}_{+}\left(\underline{\omega}_{\zeta_{1}}\wedge\underline{\omega}_{\zeta_{1}}\right)\right).

In the following we will describe three further examples, discussed by Joyce in
[
Joy99
, Chap. 15]
. These will be
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-ALE spaces coming from lifted actions on Calabi-Yau ALE four folds. These groups are:

1.

Identify
\mathbb{R}^{8}
with
\mathbb{C}^{4}
by
(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3},x_{4},x_{5},x_{6},x_{7},x_{8})=(x_{1}+{\mathrm{i}}x_{2},x_{3}+{\mathrm{i}}x_{4},x_{5}+{\mathrm{i}}x_{6},x_{7}+{\mathrm{i}}x_{8})
. Define

\displaystyle\alpha:

\displaystyle(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3},z_{4})\mapsto({\mathrm{i}}z_{1},{\mathrm{i}}z_{2},{\mathrm{i}}z_{3},{\mathrm{i}}z_{4})

\displaystyle\beta:

\displaystyle(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3},z_{4})\mapsto(\overline{z_{2}},-\overline{z_{1}},\overline{z_{4}},-\overline{z_{3}}).

Then
\alpha^{4}=\beta^{4}=1
,
\alpha\beta=\beta^{3}\alpha
and the group
\Gamma=\left<\alpha,\beta\right>
is a finite subgroup of
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
acting freely on
\mathbb{R}^{8}\backslash\{0\}
.
18
18

18

The group
\Gamma\nsubseteq{\mathrm{SU}}(4)
.
There exists a
\beta
-equivariant, crepant resolution of
\mathbb{C}^{4}/\alpha
such that
\beta
defines an anti-holomorphic involution. Moreover,
\beta
acts freely, such that the quotient is a
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-ALE manifold.

2.

Identify
\mathbb{R}^{8}
with
\mathbb{C}^{4}
by
(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3},x_{4},x_{5},x_{6},x_{7},x_{8})=(-x_{1}+{\mathrm{i}}x_{3},x_{2}+{\mathrm{i}}x_{4},-x_{5}+{\mathrm{i}}x_{7},x_{6}+{\mathrm{i}}x_{8})
. Define

\displaystyle\beta:

\displaystyle(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3},z_{4})\mapsto({\mathrm{i}}z_{1},{\mathrm{i}}z_{2},{\mathrm{i}}z_{3},{\mathrm{i}}z_{4})

\displaystyle\alpha:

\displaystyle(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3},z_{4})\mapsto(\overline{z_{2}},-\overline{z_{1}},\overline{z_{4}},-\overline{z_{3}}).

We resolve the space analogously to the first case to obtain a
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-ALE space
(\hat{X},\hat{\Phi})
which, as a
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-manifold, is isomorphic to
(X,\Phi)
but not isomorphic as an ALE space.

3.

Identify
\mathbb{R}^{8}
with
\mathbb{C}^{4}
by
(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3},x_{4},x_{5},x_{6},x_{7},x_{8})=(x_{1}+{\mathrm{i}}x_{2},x_{3}+{\mathrm{i}}x_{4},x_{5}+{\mathrm{i}}x_{6},x_{7}+{\mathrm{i}}x_{8})
. Let
n\in 2\mathbb{N}+1
and define

\displaystyle\alpha:

\displaystyle(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3},z_{4})\mapsto(e^{{\mathrm{i}}2\pi/n}z_{1},e^{-{\mathrm{i}}2\pi/n}z_{2},e^{{\mathrm{i}}2\pi/n}z_{3},e^{-{\mathrm{i}}2\pi/n}z_{4})

\displaystyle\beta:

\displaystyle(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3},z_{4})\mapsto({\mathrm{i}}z_{1},...,{\mathrm{i}}z_{4})

\displaystyle\gamma:

\displaystyle(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3},z_{4})\mapsto(\overline{z_{2}},-\overline{z_{1}},\overline{z_{4}},-\overline{z_{3}}).

Notice that for
n=1
the group
\Gamma^{n}=\left<\alpha,\beta,\gamma\right>
coincides with the group from the previous two examples. In general,
\Gamma^{n}
is a group of order
8n
acting freely on
\mathbb{R}^{8}\backslash\{0\}
. The quotient
\mathbb{C}^{4}/\left<\alpha,\beta\right>
admits a
\gamma
-equivariant, crepant resolution
19
19

19

This space can be described using toric geometry.
such that
\gamma
acts freely via an anti-holomorphic involution. The resulting quotient admits a
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-ALE structure.

4.6.
Universal Families of Resolutions

As established in the previous section, there exists a correspondence

\displaystyle\zeta\in\Gamma(S,\mathfrak{P})\rightsquigarrow\rho_{\zeta}:(N_{\zeta},\Phi_{\zeta})\dashrightarrow(N_{0},\Phi_{0}).

and hence, there exists a universal moduli space

\displaystyle N_{.}\colon\mathcal{N}\rightarrow\Gamma(S,\mathfrak{P})^{stra}

whose fibre at a point
\zeta
is
(N_{\zeta},\Phi_{\zeta})
.
20
20

20

Here
\Gamma(S,\mathfrak{P})^{stra}
denotes the subspace section defining a stratification on
S
via the pullback.
Even though this space is an infinitive dimensional (singular) Fréchet-manifold, the data of resolutions of
N_{0}
is “stored” in a simpler way as subspaces of the moduli bundle
\mathfrak{M}
. Hence, there exists an identification

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}\cong{\mathrm{ev}}^{*}_{\mathfrak{P}}\mathfrak{M}\hookrightarrow\overline{\mathcal{S}_{8}(\mathfrak{M})}

where
\mathcal{S}_{8}(\mathfrak{M})
denotes the set of
8
-dimensional stratified subspaces of
\mathfrak{M}
. In particular, we notice that the ACF
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structure on
N_{\zeta}
is induced by a universal four form
\Phi_{\mathfrak{M}}\in\Omega^{4}(\mathfrak{M})
. Hence, we can think of
\mathfrak{M}
as universal family of resolutions of
(N_{0},\Phi_{0})
.

Remark 37
.

A similar identification holds in the local model where
S
is a point and

\displaystyle(N_{0},\omega_{0})\coloneqq(\mathbb{C}^{m/2}/\Gamma,\omega_{\mathrm{st}}).

In this case, the universal family of Kähler (or hyperkähler) ALE resolutions is encoded in a moduli space
\mathbb{M}
.

Remark 38
.

The advantage of considering universal families of resolutions lies in their strong convergence properties: Gromov–Hausdorff convergence of individual resolutions lifts to convergence of smooth currents within
\mathfrak{M}
. Moreover, since the ACF
\mathrm{Spin}(7)
-structure is induced from a global ambient form
\Phi_{\mathfrak{M}}
, we can interpret certain singular ACF spaces as limits of smooth families within this universal geometry.

We wish to extend this construction to resolutions of
X
. In the same way as above, after choosing tubular neighbourhoods and cut-off-functions, we obtain a correspondence

\displaystyle\zeta\in\Gamma(S,\mathfrak{P})\rightsquigarrow\rho_{\zeta}:(X_{\zeta},\Phi^{pre}_{\zeta})\dashrightarrow(X,\Phi).

Notice, that there exists a infinite dimensional stratified Fréchet space

\displaystyle X_{.}\colon\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Gamma(S,\mathfrak{P})^{stra}

whose fibres at a point
\zeta
coincide with
(X_{\zeta},\Phi^{pre}_{\zeta})
. This space naturally admits a covering by

\displaystyle\rho_{\mathcal{N}}^{-1}({\mathrm{Tub}}_{5\epsilon}(S))\sqcup\Gamma(S,\mathfrak{P})\times X\backslash S.

Notice further that there exists a universal pre-glued vertical four form
\Phi^{pre}\in\Omega^{0,4}(\mathcal{X})
that restricts to the pre-glued
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structure
\Phi^{pre}_{\zeta}
.

Remark 39
.

Technically the universal pre-glued vertical four-form exists only on the ball in
\Gamma(S,\mathfrak{P})

\displaystyle\left|\left|\zeta\right|\right|_{C^{0}}\leq C(\epsilon,\rho)

where
\rho
is the width of
\Theta\colon{\mathrm{Tub}}_{\rho}(Cay_{+}(X))\rightarrow Cay_{+}(X)
.

Theorem 4.10

(Existence of Universal Family of Orbifold Resolutions)
.

There exists a universal family
\rho_{\mathfrak{X}}:(\mathfrak{X},\Phi^{pre}_{\mathfrak{X}})\rightarrow(X,\Phi)
, if and only if there exists a Hermitian extension of
\mathfrak{P}
to
X
.

Proof.

Let
(\mathfrak{Q},h_{\mathfrak{Q}},\nabla^{h_{\mathfrak{Q}}})\rightarrow X
be a vector bundle that restricts to the parameter bundle
(\mathfrak{P},h_{\mathfrak{P}},\nabla^{h_{\mathfrak{P}}})\rightarrow S
and let

\displaystyle\tilde{j}:(\mathfrak{P}_{0},h_{\mathfrak{P}_{0}})\subset(\nu_{0}^{*}\mathfrak{P},\nu_{0}^{*}h_{\mathfrak{P}})\hookrightarrow(\mathfrak{Q},h_{\mathfrak{Q}})

be a lift of the tubular neighbourhood
j\colon{\mathrm{Tub}}_{5\epsilon}(S)\hookrightarrow X
. Recall, that there exists a universal resolution map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rho_{\mathfrak{M}}}:(\mathfrak{M},\Phi_{\mathfrak{M}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(N_{0},\Phi_{0})}}

that lifts to a map

\displaystyle\rho^{-1}_{\mathfrak{M}}({\mathrm{Tub}}_{5\epsilon}(S)).

We define the space

\displaystyle\mathfrak{X}\eqqcolon(\rho^{-1}_{\mathfrak{M}}({\mathrm{Tub}}_{5\epsilon}(S))\cup\mathfrak{Q}\backslash\mathfrak{P})/\sim,

where
m_{\zeta_{s}}\sim q_{x}
if
\tilde{j}(\rho(m_{\zeta_{s}}))=q_{j(s)}
. Further, we define

\displaystyle\Phi^{pre}_{\mathfrak{X}}=\Theta((1-\chi_{3})\Phi_{\mathfrak{M}}+\chi_{3}\mathfrak{q}^{*}\Phi_{0}+\mathfrak{q}^{*}\Phi_{hot}).

Here
\Theta
can be lifted, as it is a pointwise operation and hence, lifts from
X\backslash S
using the connection
H_{\mathfrak{Q}}
, i.e. the horizontal subspace in
T\mathfrak{Q}
. In the same way, we are able to construct a universal Riemannian structure

\displaystyle g^{pre}_{\mathfrak{X}}=\gamma(\Theta((1-\chi_{3})\Phi_{\mathfrak{M}}+\chi_{3}\mathfrak{q}^{*}\Phi_{0}+\mathfrak{q}^{*}\Phi_{hot}))

which completes one direction of the proof.

For the converse direction, assume that there exists such a universal family of resolutions of
(X,\Phi)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rho_{\mathfrak{X}}}:(\mathfrak{X},\Phi^{pre}_{\mathfrak{X}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(X,\Phi)}}

Then there exists (singular) submanifold
[\zeta:X_{\zeta}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{X}]
for each
\zeta\in\Gamma(S,\mathfrak{P})
by definition. In particular, the short exact sequence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TX_{\zeta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\zeta^{*}T\mathfrak{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N\zeta.}

implies an inclusion at
\zeta=0
(the central fibre
X_{0}=X
),

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TX_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0^{*}T\mathfrak{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N0}

and realise that
N0|_{S}=\mathfrak{P}
and
N0=\mathfrak{Q}
, which completes the proof.
∎

Remark 40
.

For codimension-four strata in
G_{2}
-orbifolds with isotropy group
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
, the above construction always applies, since the parameter bundle
\mathfrak{P}
is trivial in this case.

5.
Existence of Torsion-Free
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
Structures on Orbifold Resolutions

In the following section we will solve the fixpoint problem
9
to deform the pre-glued
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structures on
X_{\zeta;t}
constructed in Section
4.5.1
-
4.5.4
to a torsion-free structure. In contrast to Joyce’ general existence result of torsion-free
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
structures deforming those whose torsion has a small potential, we will use the adapted norms constructed in
[
Maj25b
]
to prove an existence result for resolutions of
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-orbifolds. This existence result will only depend on the smallness of the torsion of the pre-glued
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structure
\Phi^{pre}_{\zeta;t}
.

5.1.
The Vanishing of the Obstruction Map and Orbifold String Cohomology

In complex geometry, the “Cohomological Crepant Resolution Conjecture”
[
Rua01
]
states that the Chen-Ruan cohomology ring of a Gorenstein orbifold coincides, after quantum corrections with the cohomology ring of a crepant resolution.
21
21

21

In the realm of hyperkähler geometry, this conjecture is expected to hold without quantum corrections.
This conjecture connects string-theoretic predictions with deep geometric structures and has inspired significant developments in the study of orbifold cohomology. We will show that the obstruction map
{\mathrm{ob}}_{\beta;t}
arising in the construction of
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-orbifold resolutions can be interpreted as a
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-analogue of this conjecture. This supports the view that isentropicity should be regarded as the geometric condition familiar from the Calabi-Yau case. In the following section we will briefly recall the construction of the Chen-Ruan cohomology and refer to
[
ALR07
, Sec. 4]
for a more in-depth construction.

Definition 5.1
.

Let
g\in G
be an element and let
\{e^{2\pi{\mathrm{i}}\theta_{1}},...,e^{2\pi{\mathrm{i}}\theta_{n}}\}
be its eigenvalues with
\theta_{i}\in[0,1)
. We define the
age
of
g
as

\displaystyle{\mathrm{age}}(g)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\theta_{i}\in\mathbb{R}.

As
g\in{\mathrm{SO}}(n)
all non-zero eigenvalues come in pairs
\{e^{2\pi{\mathrm{i}}\theta},1-e^{2\pi{\mathrm{i}}\theta_{1}}\}
and hence,

\displaystyle{\mathrm{age}}(g)\in\left\{0,...,\left\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\right\rfloor\right\}.

\lozenge

Remark 41
.

The age only depends on the conjugacy class of an element and hence, descends to a function

\displaystyle{\mathrm{age}}:{\mathrm{Con}}(G)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}.

We will follow
[
ALR07
, Sec. 4]
and
[
Moe02
, Chap. 6.4]
to define the first and second intertia stack of an orbifold
X
.

Definition 5.2
.

Let
X
be an orbifold and
X^{\bullet}
a proper, foliating Lie groupoid representing it. We define the
inertia Lie groupoid

\mathcal{I}X^{\bullet}

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}X^{0}\coloneqq

\displaystyle\{\phi\in X^{1}|s(\phi)=t(\phi)\}

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}X^{1}\coloneqq

\displaystyle\{(\kappa,\phi)\in X^{1}\times\mathcal{I}X^{0}|s(\phi)=s(\kappa),\kappa\phi\kappa^{-1}\in\mathcal{I}X^{0}\}

with

\displaystyle s(\kappa,\phi)\coloneqq\phi\hskip 28.45274pt\text{and}\hskip 28.45274ptt(\kappa,\phi)\coloneqq\kappa\phi\kappa^{-1}.

\lozenge

Remark 42
.

The inertia groupoid
\mathcal{I}X^{\bullet}
represents an orbifold called the inertia orbifold
\mathcal{I}X
corresponding to
X
.

Remark 43
.

Let
X^{\bullet}
be a presenting Lie groupoid of an orbifold
X
. We define the fibre product

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Lambda(X^{1})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X^{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X^{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X^{0}\times X^{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\times t)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta}

The inertia groupoid of
X^{\bullet}
can be identified with
\mathcal{I}X^{\bullet}=X^{\bullet}\ltimes\Lambda(X^{1})
, where
X^{1}
acts via conjugation.

Example 2
.

[
ALR07
, Ex. 2.5]

Assume that
X^{\bullet}=\Gamma\ltimes X
. Then

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}X^{\bullet}\cong\Gamma\ltimes\left(\bigsqcup_{g\in\Gamma}{\mathrm{Fix}}(g)\right),

where
\Gamma
acts via
h\colon{\mathrm{Fix}}(g)\rightarrow{\mathrm{Fix}}(hgh^{-1}),x\mapsto h.x
. In particular, the inertia groupoid admits a decomposition into

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}X^{\bullet}\cong\bigsqcup_{(h)\in{\mathrm{Con}}(\Gamma)}\mathcal{I}(X_{(h)})^{\bullet},

where
\mathcal{I}(X_{(h)})^{\bullet}\coloneqq\Gamma\ltimes\left(\bigsqcup_{g\in(h)}{\mathrm{Fix}}(g)\right)
.

\clubsuit

Example 3
.

Let
X
be an orbifold and
S
a single singular stratum of isotropy
\Gamma
. Let
NS
be a vector bundle on
S
such that
\pi:B(NS)\rightarrow B(NS)/\Gamma
and
j:{\mathrm{Tub}}(S)\cong B(NS)/\Gamma\hookrightarrow X
.
Then

\displaystyle X^{0}=X\backslash S\sqcup B(NS)\hskip 28.45274pt\text{and}\hskip 28.45274pt{\mathrm{Hom}}_{X^{\bullet}}(x,y)\coloneqq\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1_{x}&,x=y\in X\backslash Tub(S)\\
j\circ\pi&,x\in B(NS),y\in X\backslash S\\
g\in\Gamma&,y=gx\in B(NS)\end{array}\right.

defines a presenting Lie groupoid. The associated inertia Lie groupoid is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}^{\bullet}X\cong(X\backslash S)\sqcup\left(\Gamma\ltimes\bigsqcup_{g\in\Gamma}Fix(g,B(NS))\right)\cong X\sqcup\bigsqcup_{(g)\in{\mathrm{Con}}(\Gamma)}B(NS)_{(g)}/C(g),

where
NS^{(g)}\coloneqq\Gamma\ltimes\sqcup_{h\in(g)}Fix(g)
and
\Gamma
acts via
h\colon{\mathrm{Fix}}(g)\rightarrow{\mathrm{Fix}}(hgh^{-1})

If
NS\cong Fr_{G}(X/S)\times_{{\mathrm{N}}_{G}(\Gamma)}\mathbb{R}^{m}
for some
{\mathrm{N}}_{G}(\Gamma)
-reduction
F_{{\mathrm{N}}_{G}(\Gamma)}(S)\hookrightarrow Fr(X,G)|_{S}
of a
G
-structure on
X
, the inertia bundle can be expressed as

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}^{\bullet}X\cong X\sqcup F_{{\mathrm{N}}_{G}(\Gamma)}(S)\times_{{\mathrm{N}}_{G}(\Gamma)}\bigsqcup_{(g)\in{\mathrm{Con}}(\Gamma)}(\mathbb{R}^{m})_{(g)}/C(g).

In particular, if
S
is of type A then

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}X\cong X\sqcup F_{{\mathrm{N}}_{G}(\Gamma)}(S)\times_{{\mathrm{N}}_{G}(\Gamma)}\bigsqcup_{(g)\in{\mathrm{Con}}(\Gamma)}[*/C(g)].

\clubsuit

Using the definition of the first and second inertia stack, we will follow
[
ALR07
, Sec. 4]
to define the Chen-Ruan cohomology.

Definition 5.3
.

Let
X
be an
n
-dimensional orbifold such that all isotropy groups are conjugated to subgroups of
G\subset{\mathrm{SO}}(n)
. We define the
Chen-Ruan cohomology
as

\displaystyle{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}_{{\mathrm{CR}}}(X,R)\coloneqq\bigoplus_{[g]\in{\mathrm{Con}}(G)}{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet-2{\mathrm{age}}(g)}\left(\mathcal{I}X_{(g)},R\right)

where
\mathcal{I}X_{(g)}
are the strata of the inertia orbifold and
{\mathrm{Con}}(G)
denotes the set of conjugacy classes of elements of the group
G
.

\lozenge

We now provide evidence that the isentropicity of a resolution should be regarded as a geometric condition, rather than a purely analytic one. Specifically, we prove that the resolution is isentropic if and only if the graded vector space structure of the singular cohomology of the resolution agrees with the Chen-Ruan orbifold cohomology of the original orbifold
X
. This compatibility reflects the expected string-theoretic picture, where the cohomology of a smooth resolution captures the contributions from twisted sectors in the orbifold theory.

Theorem 5.1
.

Let
(X,\Phi)
be a compact
\mathrm{Spin}(7)
-orbifold, and let
S\subset X
be a single singular stratum of codimension
m=4
with isotropy group
\Gamma\subset\mathrm{SU}(2)
. Let
\zeta\in\Omega^{2}_{+}(S,\mathfrak{H}_{S})
be harmonic. Then the resolution

\displaystyle\rho_{\zeta;t}:(X_{\zeta;t},\Phi_{\zeta;t})\dashrightarrow(X,\Phi)

is isentropic if and only if the cohomology of the resolution satisfies

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{\bullet}(X_{\zeta;t},\mathbb{R})\cong\mathrm{H}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{{\mathrm{CR}}}}(X,\mathbb{R})

as graded vector spaces.

Proof.

As discussed in Example
3
, the inertia groupoid of
X
decomposes as

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}X\cong X\sqcup\mathrm{Fr}_{\mathrm{Spin}(7)}(X/S)\times_{N_{\mathrm{Spin}(7)}(\Gamma)}\mathrm{Con}(\Gamma),

where
\mathrm{Con}(\Gamma)
denotes the set of nontrivial conjugacy classes in
\Gamma
.

By the McKay correspondence (Theorem
4.5
), the conjugacy classes of
\Gamma
correspond to the roots in a root system of type ADE, encoded in a lattice

\displaystyle\Lambda_{S}\cong\mathrm{Fr}_{\mathrm{Spin}(7)}(X/S)\times_{N_{\mathrm{Spin}(7)}(\Gamma)}\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Con}(\Gamma)},

which gives rise to a local system

\displaystyle\mathfrak{H}_{S}:=\Lambda_{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{R}.

Now, since
\Gamma\subset\mathrm{SU}(2)
, each nontrivial element
g\in\Gamma
has age one, and hence, the Chen-Ruan cohomology of
X
is given by

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{{\mathrm{CR}}}}(X,\mathbb{R})\cong\mathrm{H}^{\bullet}(X,\mathbb{R})\oplus\mathrm{H}^{\bullet-2}(S,\mathfrak{H}_{S}).

On the other hand, by Lemma
3.4
, Lemma
3.5
, and Corollary
4.5
, the cohomology of the fibration satisfies

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{\bullet}(N_{\zeta}/S)\cong\underline{\mathbb{R}}[0]\oplus\mathfrak{H}_{S}.

Now, assume that the resolution is isentropic, i.e. the analytic obstruction map
\mathrm{ob}_{\beta;t}=0
for sufficiently large
\beta<0
. Then by Proposition
3.4
and Corollary
3.4
, the cohomology of the total space satisfies

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{\bullet}(X_{\zeta;t},\mathbb{R})

\displaystyle\cong\mathrm{xker}_{\beta}(D^{\mathrm{pre}}_{\zeta;t})

\displaystyle\cong\mathrm{H}^{\bullet}(X,\mathbb{R})\oplus\mathrm{Tot}^{\bullet}\mathrm{H}^{\bullet}\left(S,\mathcal{H}^{\bullet}(N_{\zeta}/S)\right)

\displaystyle\cong\mathrm{H}^{\bullet}(X,\mathbb{R})\oplus\mathrm{H}^{\bullet-2}(S,\mathfrak{H}_{S}).

This proves that
\mathrm{H}^{\bullet}(X_{\zeta;t},\mathbb{R})\cong\mathrm{H}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{{\mathrm{CR}}}}(X,\mathbb{R})
as graded vector spaces if and only if the resolution is isentropic.
∎

It is important to note, however, that the identification of cohomology groups as graded vector spaces does not guarantee an isomorphism of ring structures. In the Calabi-Yau case, “quantum corrections” arising from contributions of worldsheet instantons or, more generally, from Gromov-Witten theory, may alter the classical cup product structure in the Chen–Ruan cohomology. Similarly, for
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-spaces, one expects that analogous corrections could affect the ring structure. Therefore, even in the isentropic case, additional geometric or algebraic data may be necessary to fully match the Chen-Ruan ring structure with the classical cohomology ring of the resolution.

Remark 44
.

The ring structure on
{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(X_{\zeta;t})
can be computed via computing the
\cup
-product on
{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(X)
and on
{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(N_{\zeta})/{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(S)
. Let in the following
\iota:S\hookrightarrow X
denote the inclusion. We compute

\displaystyle{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(X)\otimes{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(X)\rightarrow{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(X),

\displaystyle\,\alpha\otimes\beta\mapsto\alpha\cup\beta

\displaystyle{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(X)\otimes{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(N_{\zeta})/{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(S)\rightarrow{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(X),

\displaystyle\,\alpha\otimes\beta\mapsto[\nu_{\zeta}^{*}\iota^{*}\alpha\cup\beta]=0

The
\cup
-product on
(N_{\zeta},g_{\zeta})
can be identified with

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left({\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(S)\oplus{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet-2}(S,\mathfrak{H})\right)\otimes\left({\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(S)\oplus{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet-2}(S,\mathfrak{H})\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet+\bullet}(S)\oplus{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet+\bullet-2}(S,\mathfrak{H})\oplus{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet+\bullet-4}(S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\alpha+\omega,\beta+\eta)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\alpha\cup\beta\oplus(\alpha\cup\eta+(-1)^{|\beta|}\beta\cup\omega)\oplus\omega\cup_{C}\eta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cup}

where the last component is the intersection product on
S
twisted by the quadratic form on the local system given by the vertical intersection, i.e. the Cartan matrix.
The first component of
{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(S)
is in the kernel of the quotient
{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(N_{\zeta})/{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(S)
and the second copy of
{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet-4}(S)
is mapped into
{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(X)
via the Thom isomorphism.
[
BT+82
, Prop. 6.24, Prop. 6.25]
.

Theorem 5.2
.

Let
(X,\Phi)
be a
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-orbifold and
S\subset X
a compact singular stratum of isotropy
\Gamma\subset{\mathrm{SU}}(m/2)
for
m>2
. Then

\displaystyle{\mathrm{ob}}_{\beta;t}=0

for
\beta<0
large enough.

Proof.

The vertical cohomology of
\nu_{\zeta}:(N_{\zeta},\Phi_{\zeta})\rightarrow(S,g_{S})
is only supported in even degree. Consequently, the second page of the Leray-Serre spectral sequence collapses as

\displaystyle{\mathrm{d}}^{2,-1}=0

and
{\mathrm{d}}^{n,1-n}=0
vanishes as
S
is of dimension
{\mathrm{dim}}(S)=8-m<3
.
∎

5.2.
Deformation to Torsion-Free
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
Structures on
X_{\zeta;t}

This section will be devoted to proving the main theorem of this paper. Before we start proving the existence result, we will first prove an a priori bound on the torsion of the pre-glued
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structure. We will use this “a priori bound” to show that the only obstruction to deform the pre-glued structure to a torsion-free structure is
{\mathrm{ob}}_{\beta;t}=0
.

Assumption 7
.

In the following we will assume that
(X,\Phi)
is a
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-orbifold and

\displaystyle\rho_{\zeta;t}:(X_{\zeta;t},\Phi^{pre}_{\zeta;t})\dashrightarrow(X,\Phi)

is a complete adiabatic torsion-free
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-orbifold resolution.

Notation
.

Let
\vartheta
be a positive real number, satisfying the following

\displaystyle\vartheta<\min\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\lambda(2-\beta),\\
-\lambda\beta+(1-\lambda)m,\\
\frac{m}{2}-\kappa+\lambda,\\
\frac{m}{2}-\kappa+(1-\lambda)m-\lambda.\end{array}\right.

Proposition 5.1

(A Priori Bound)
.

Assume that Assumption
7
holds. Let
\Phi^{pre}_{\zeta;t}
be the
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structure on
X_{\zeta;t}
obtained by interpolating between the adiabatic torsion-free
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structure constructed in Section
4.5.1
-
4.5.4
and the torsion-free structure
\Phi
on
X
. We obtain the a priori bound for the torsion of
\Phi^{pre}_{\zeta;t}

(32)

\displaystyle\left|\left|{\mathrm{d}}\Phi^{pre}_{\zeta;t}\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{0,\alpha}_{\beta-1;t}}\lesssim

\displaystyle t^{\vartheta}

Remark 45
.

We will establish this a priori bound in a general form, so that the optimal bound in specific examples can be easily determined.

Proof.

We begin by bounding the ACF-part of the torsion of
\Phi^{pre}_{\zeta;t}
. Recall,

\displaystyle\pi_{\tau;\Phi}=\pi_{1\oplus 7\oplus 35;\Phi}

is the projection onto the components that are tangential at
\Phi
to the bundle of
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
structures and

\displaystyle\pi_{\nu;\Phi}=1-\pi_{\tau;\Phi}

denotes the projection on to the normal components.

We begin by bounding the ACF-part of the torsion of
\Phi^{pre}_{\zeta;t}
. Notice, that we can expand
\Phi^{pre}_{\zeta;t}
by

\displaystyle\Theta(\widetilde{\Phi}^{pre;t}_{\zeta})=

\displaystyle\Theta(\Phi^{t}_{\zeta}+\chi^{t}_{3}(\Phi^{t}_{0}-\Phi^{t}_{\zeta})+\Phi^{t}_{hot})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Phi^{t}_{\zeta}+\pi_{\tau;\Phi^{t}_{\zeta}}\left(\chi^{t}_{3}(\Phi^{t}_{0}-\Phi^{t}_{\zeta})+\Phi^{t}_{hot}\right)+Q_{\Phi^{t}_{\zeta}}\left(\chi^{t}_{3}(\Phi^{t}_{0}-\Phi^{t}_{\zeta})+\Phi^{t}_{hot}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Phi}^{pre;t}_{\zeta}+\pi_{\nu;\Phi^{t}_{\zeta}}\left(\chi^{t}_{3}(\Phi^{t}_{0}-\Phi^{t}_{\zeta})+\Phi^{t}_{hot}\right)+Q_{\Phi^{t}_{\zeta}}\left(\chi^{t}_{3}(\Phi^{t}_{0}-\Phi^{t}_{\zeta})+\Phi^{t}_{hot}\right)

where by
\widetilde{\Phi}^{pre;t}_{\zeta}=\Phi^{t}_{\zeta}+\chi^{t}_{3}(\Phi^{t}_{0}-\Phi^{t}_{\zeta})+\Phi^{t}_{hot}=\Phi+(1-\chi_{3})(\Phi_{t^{2}\cdot\zeta}-\Phi_{0})
.

Hence, we bound the ACF-part of the torsion of
\Phi^{pre}_{\zeta;t}
by

\displaystyle\left|\left|(1-\chi^{t}_{4}){\mathrm{d}}\Phi^{pre;t}_{\zeta}\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{0,\alpha}_{{\mathrm{ACF}};\beta-1;t}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\underbrace{\left|\left|(1-\chi^{t}_{4}){\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{\Phi}^{pre}_{\zeta;t}\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{0,\alpha}_{{\mathrm{ACF}};\beta-1;t}}}_{(I)}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\left|\left|(1-\chi^{t}_{4}){\mathrm{d}}\left(\pi_{\nu;\Phi^{t}_{\zeta}}\left(\chi^{t}_{3}(\Phi^{t}_{0}-\Phi^{t}_{\zeta})+\Phi^{t}_{hot}\right)\right)\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{0,\alpha}_{{\mathrm{ACF}};\beta-1;t}}}_{(II)}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\left|\left|(1-\chi^{t}_{4}){\mathrm{d}}\left(Q_{\Phi^{t}_{\zeta}}\left(\chi^{t}_{3}(\Phi^{t}_{0}-\Phi^{t}_{\zeta})+\Phi^{t}_{hot}\right)\right)\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{0,\alpha}_{{\mathrm{ACF}};\beta-1;t}}}_{(III)}.

Recall that by Lemma
4.3
, we know that

\displaystyle{\mathrm{d}}\Phi_{hot}=-{\mathrm{d}}^{2,-1}\Phi_{0}\hskip 28.45274pt\text{and}\hskip 28.45274pt\pi_{\tau;\Phi_{0}}\Phi_{1}=\Phi_{1}.

By using the first identity, we deduce that

\displaystyle{\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{\Phi}^{pre;t}_{\zeta}=

\displaystyle{\mathrm{d}}\chi^{t}_{3}\wedge(\Phi^{t}_{0}-\Phi^{t}_{\zeta})+(1-\chi^{t}_{3})({\mathrm{d}}\Phi^{t}_{\zeta}-{\mathrm{d}}\Phi^{t}_{0})

which is independent of higher-order terms. Consequently, we are able to bound

\displaystyle(I)=

\displaystyle\left|\left|(1-\chi^{t}_{4}){\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{\Phi}^{pre;t}_{\zeta}\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{0,\alpha}_{{\mathrm{ACF}};\beta-1;t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\max\left\{t^{-\lambda\beta+(1-\lambda)m},t^{m/2-\kappa-\lambda+(1-\lambda)m},\max\left\{t^{1+\lambda(1-\beta)},t^{1+m/2-\kappa}\right\}\right\}.

We further bound

\displaystyle(II)\lesssim

\displaystyle\underbrace{\left|\left|{\mathrm{d}}(1-\chi^{t}_{4})\left(\pi_{\nu;\Phi^{t}_{\zeta}}\left(\chi^{t}_{3}(\Phi^{t}_{0}-\Phi^{t}_{\zeta})+\Phi^{t}_{hot}\right)\right)\right|\right|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{{\mathrm{ACF}};\beta-1;t}}}_{(i)}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\left|\left|{\mathrm{d}}\chi^{t}_{4}\wedge\left(\pi_{\nu;\Phi^{t}_{\zeta}}\left(\chi^{t}_{3}(\Phi^{t}_{0}-\Phi^{t}_{\zeta})+\Phi^{t}_{hot}\right)\right)\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{0,\alpha}_{{\mathrm{ACF}};\beta-1;t}}}_{(ii)}

whereby

\displaystyle(i)\lesssim

\displaystyle\underbrace{\left|\left|(1-\chi^{t}_{4}){\mathrm{d}}\left(\pi_{\nu;\Phi^{t}_{\zeta}}\left(\chi^{t}_{3}(\Phi^{t}_{0}-\Phi^{t}_{\zeta})+\Phi^{t}_{hot}\right)\right)\right|\right|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{{\mathrm{ACF}};\beta-1;t}}}_{\lesssim t^{\lambda(1-\beta)}\max\{t^{\lambda},t^{(1-\lambda)m}\}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\left|\left|{\mathrm{d}}\chi^{t}_{4}\wedge\left(\pi_{\nu;\Phi^{t}_{\zeta}}\left(\chi^{t}_{3}(\Phi^{t}_{0}-\Phi^{t}_{\zeta})+\Phi^{t}_{hot}\right)\right)\right|\right|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{{\mathrm{ACF}};\beta-1;t}}}_{\lesssim t^{-\lambda\beta}\max\{t^{2\lambda},t^{(1-\lambda)m}\}},

where we used that

\displaystyle\pi_{\nu;\Phi^{t}_{\zeta}}\Phi^{t}_{hot}=

\displaystyle\pi_{\nu;\Phi^{t}_{0}}\Phi^{t}_{hot}+(\pi_{\nu;\Phi^{t}_{\zeta}}-\pi_{\nu;\Phi^{t}_{0}})\Phi^{t}_{hot}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi_{\nu;\Phi^{t}_{0}}\Phi^{t}_{2\leq}+(\pi_{\nu;\Phi^{t}_{\zeta}}-\pi_{\nu;\Phi^{t}_{0}})\Phi^{t}_{hot}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(t^{2}r^{2})+\mathcal{O}(r^{-m})

and hence

\displaystyle(ii)\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|\left|\pi_{\mathcal{I};\beta-1}\left({\mathrm{d}}\chi^{t}_{4}\wedge\left(\pi_{\nu;\Phi^{t}_{\zeta}}\left(\chi^{t}_{3}(\Phi^{t}_{0}-\Phi^{t}_{\zeta})+\Phi^{t}_{hot}\right)\right)\right)\right|\right|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{{\mathrm{ACF}};\beta-1;t}}

\displaystyle+t^{-\kappa}\left|\left|\pi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta-1}\left({\mathrm{d}}\chi^{t}_{4}\wedge\left(\pi_{\nu;\Phi^{t}_{\zeta}}\left(\chi^{t}_{3}(\Phi^{t}_{0}-\Phi^{t}_{\zeta})+\Phi^{t}_{hot}\right)\right)\right)\right|\right|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\max\left\{t^{-\lambda\beta}\max\{t^{2\lambda},t^{(1-\lambda)m}\},t^{m/2-\kappa}\max\{t^{\lambda},t^{(1-\lambda)m-\lambda}\}\right\}

Finally, we bound

\displaystyle(III)\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|\left|(1-\chi^{t}_{4}){\mathrm{d}}\left(Q_{\Phi^{t}_{\zeta}}\left(\chi^{t}_{3}(\Phi^{t}_{0}-\Phi^{t}_{\zeta})+\Phi^{t}_{hot}\right)\right)\right|\right|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{{\mathrm{ACF}};\beta-1;t}}

\displaystyle+\left|\left|{\mathrm{d}}\chi^{t}_{4}\wedge\left(Q_{\Phi^{t}_{\zeta}}\left(\chi^{t}_{3}(\Phi^{t}_{0}-\Phi^{t}_{\zeta})+\Phi^{t}_{hot}\right)\right)\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{0,\alpha}_{{\mathrm{ACF}};\beta-1;t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\max\left\{\left|\left|{\mathrm{d}}\Phi^{t}_{\zeta}\right|\right|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{{\mathrm{ACF}};\beta-1;t}},\left|\left|1\right|\right|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{{\mathrm{ACF}};\beta-1;t}}\right\}\left|\left|\chi^{t}_{3}(\Phi^{t}_{0}-\Phi^{t}_{\zeta})+\Phi^{t}_{hot}\right|\right|_{C^{1,\alpha}_{{\mathrm{ACF}};0;t}}^{2}

\displaystyle+t^{-\kappa+m/2-\lambda}\left|\left|\chi^{t}_{3}(\Phi^{t}_{0}-\Phi^{t}_{\zeta})+\Phi^{t}_{hot}\right|\right|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{{\mathrm{ACF}};0;t}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\max\left\{t^{\lambda(1-\beta)}\max\{t^{2(1-\lambda)m},t^{2\lambda}\},t^{m/2-\kappa-\lambda}\max\{t^{(1-\lambda)m},t^{\lambda}\}\right\}

Combing these estimates, we deduce that

\displaystyle\left|\left|(1-\chi^{t}_{4}){\mathrm{d}}\Phi^{pre}_{\zeta;t}\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{0,\alpha}_{{\mathrm{ACF}};\beta-1;t}}\lesssim t^{\vartheta}.

We will continue with the CFS-part of the a priori estimate on the torsion of the pre-glued
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structure
\Phi^{pre}_{\zeta;t}
. We expand

\displaystyle\Phi^{pre}_{\zeta;t}=

\displaystyle\Theta(\widetilde{\Phi}^{pre}_{\zeta;t})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Theta(\Phi+(1-\chi_{3})(\Phi_{t^{2}\cdot\zeta}-\Phi_{0}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Phi+\pi_{\tau;\Phi}\left((1-\chi_{3})(\Phi_{t^{2}\cdot\zeta}-\Phi_{0})\right)+Q_{\Phi}\left((1-\chi_{3})(\Phi_{t^{2}\cdot\zeta}-\Phi_{0})\right)

and deduce that

\displaystyle{\mathrm{d}}\Phi^{pre}_{\zeta;t}={\mathrm{d}}\pi_{\tau;\Phi}\left((1-\chi_{3})(\Phi_{t^{2}\cdot\zeta}-\Phi_{0})\right)+{\mathrm{d}}Q_{\Phi}\left((1-\chi_{3})(\Phi_{t^{2}\cdot\zeta}-\Phi_{0})\right).

\displaystyle\left|\left|\chi_{2}{\mathrm{d}}\Phi^{pre}_{\zeta;t}\right|\right|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{{\mathrm{CFS}};\beta-1;t}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|\left|\chi_{2}{\mathrm{d}}\pi_{\tau;\Phi}\left((1-\chi_{3})(\Phi_{t^{2}\cdot\zeta}-\Phi_{0})\right)\right|\right|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{{\mathrm{CFS}};\beta-1;t}}

\displaystyle+\left|\left|\chi_{2}{\mathrm{d}}Q_{\Phi}\left((1-\chi_{3})(\Phi_{t^{2}\cdot\zeta}-\Phi_{0})\right)\right|\right|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{{\mathrm{CFS}};\beta-1;t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\max\left\{t^{\lambda(2-\beta)},t^{\lambda(2-\beta)+(1-\lambda)m}\right\}\lesssim t^{\lambda(2-\beta)}

where we used Lemma
4.3
to expand

\displaystyle\pi_{\tau;\Phi}=\pi_{\tau;\Phi_{0}}+\left(\underbrace{\pi_{\tau;\Phi}-\pi_{\tau;\Phi_{0}}}_{\mathcal{O}(r^{2})}\right).

Combining the ACF and CFS-part of the estimate we deduce the a priori bound

\displaystyle\left|\left|{\mathrm{d}}\Phi^{pre}_{\zeta;t}\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{0,\alpha}_{\beta-1;t}}\lesssim

\displaystyle t^{\vartheta}

on the torsion of the preglued
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structure
\Phi^{pre}_{\zeta;t}
.
∎

Assumption 8
.

We assume that the resolution
\rho_{\zeta;t}:(X_{\zeta;t},\Phi^{pre}_{\zeta;t})\dashrightarrow(X,\Phi)
is an isentropic resolution.

Following this we will now use the derived results to proof the following main theorem.

Theorem 5.3

(Existence of Torsion-Free
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)

.
