4.5
, such that

(33)

\displaystyle\left|\left|{\mathrm{d}}\Phi^{pre}_{\zeta;t}\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{0,\alpha}_{\beta-1;t}}\lesssim t^{\vartheta}

where
\vartheta
satisfies (
Notation
). If

\displaystyle\rho_{\zeta;t}:(X_{\zeta;t},\Phi_{\zeta;t})\dashrightarrow(X,\Phi)

is an isentropic resolution, there exists a torsion-free
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-orbifold resolution

\displaystyle[\rho_{\zeta;t}:(X_{\zeta;t},\Phi_{\zeta;t})\dashrightarrow(X,\Phi)]\in\textbf{GHRes}(X,\Phi)

satisfying

(34)

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi_{\zeta;t}-\Phi^{pre}_{\zeta;t}\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{D}^{1,\alpha}_{\beta;t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim t^{\vartheta}

Here
\Phi_{\zeta;t}=\Theta(\Phi^{pre}_{\zeta;t}+\eta_{\zeta;t})
where
\eta_{\zeta;t}
is a unique solution to the fixpoint problem (
9
).

As discussed in Section
2.6
we will essentially use the contraction mapping principal in a suitable function space to obtain a solution and then argue that this solution is already a smooth one.

Proof of Theorem
5.3
.

In order to improve the readability of the following prove, we will drop the subscript
\zeta
everywhere.

In the following we will show that the map

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{t}\colon\mathfrak{C}^{0,\alpha}_{\beta-1;t}(X_{t},\wedge^{\bullet}T^{*}X_{t})\rightarrow\mathfrak{C}^{0,\alpha}_{\beta-1;t}(X_{t},\wedge^{\bullet}T^{*}X_{t})

\displaystyle\eta\mapsto-D^{op}_{\Phi^{pre}_{t}}\Phi^{pre}_{t}-D_{\Phi_{t}^{pre}}\pi_{-;\Phi^{pre}_{t}}\left\{Q_{\Phi_{t}^{pre}}(R_{\Phi^{pre}_{t}}\eta)\right\}-D^{op}_{\Phi_{t}^{pre}}\pi_{+;\Phi^{pre}_{t}}\left\{Q_{\Phi_{t}^{pre}}(R_{\Phi^{pre}_{t}}\eta)\right\}

restricted to a small enough neighbourhood of
0\in\mathfrak{C}^{0,\alpha}_{\beta-1;t}
, defines a contractive self-mapping. By using the contraction mapping principal and the ellipticity, we will deduce the existence and smoothness of the fixpoint.

Firstly, by Proposition
5.1
we know that

\displaystyle\left|\left|\mathcal{C}_{t}(0)\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{0,\alpha}_{\beta-1;t}}=\left|\left|D^{op}_{\Phi_{t}^{pre}}\Phi^{pre}_{t}\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{0,\alpha}_{\beta-1;t}}\asymp\left|\left|{\mathrm{d}}\Phi^{pre}_{t}\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{0,\alpha}_{\beta-1;t}}\lesssim e(t)=t^{\vartheta}.

Secondly, we need to show that
\mathcal{C}_{t}
is a contraction. Hence, we bound

\displaystyle\left|\left|\mathcal{C}_{t}(\eta_{1})-\mathcal{C}_{t}(\eta_{2})\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{0,\alpha}_{\beta-1;t}}\lesssim(I)+(II)

for

\displaystyle(I)=

\displaystyle\left|\left|D_{\Phi^{pre}_{t}}\pi_{-;\Phi^{pre}_{t}}\left\{Q_{\Phi_{t}^{pre}}(R_{\Phi^{pre}_{t}}\eta_{1})-Q_{\Phi_{t}^{pre}}(R_{\Phi^{pre;t}}\eta_{2})\right\}\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{0,\alpha}_{\beta-1;t}}

\displaystyle(II)=

\displaystyle\left|\left|D^{op}_{\Phi_{t}^{pre}}\pi_{+;\Phi^{pre}_{t}}\left\{Q_{\Phi_{t}^{pre}}(R_{\Phi^{pre}_{t}}\eta_{1})-Q_{\Phi_{t}^{pre}}(R_{\Phi^{pre}_{t}}\eta_{2})\right\}\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{0,\alpha}_{\beta-1;t}}.

These terms, will be bounded individually by

\displaystyle(I)=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\left|\left|\chi_{2}D_{\Phi^{pre}_{t}}\pi_{-;\Phi^{pre}_{t}}\left\{Q_{\Phi_{t}^{pre}}(R_{\Phi^{pre}_{t}}\eta_{1})-Q_{\Phi_{t}^{pre}}(R_{\Phi^{pre;t}}\eta_{2})\right\}\right|\right|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{{\mathrm{CFS}};\beta-1;t}}}_{(i)}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\left|\left|(1-\chi^{t}_{4})D_{\Phi^{pre;t}}\pi_{-;\Phi^{pre;t}}\left\{Q_{\Phi^{pre;t}}(R_{\Phi^{pre;t}}\delta_{t}^{*}\eta_{1})-Q_{\Phi^{pre;t}}(R_{\Phi^{pre;t}}\delta_{t}^{*}\eta_{2})\right\}\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{0,\alpha}_{{\mathrm{ACF}};\beta-1;t}}}_{(ii)}

whereby we bound

\displaystyle(i)\lesssim

\displaystyle\max\left\{\left|\left|{\mathrm{d}}\Phi^{pre;t}_{\zeta}\right|\right|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{{\mathrm{CFS}};\beta-1;t}}\left|\left|1\right|\right|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{{\mathrm{CFS}};-2\beta;t}},\left|\left|1\right|\right|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{{\mathrm{CFS}};-\beta;t}}\right\}

\displaystyle\cdot\left|\left|\chi_{1}R_{\Phi^{pre}_{t}}(\eta_{1}-\eta_{2})\right|\right|_{C^{1,\alpha}_{{\mathrm{CFS}};\beta;t}}\left(\left|\left|\chi_{1}R_{\Phi^{pre}_{t}}\eta_{1}\right|\right|_{C^{1,\alpha}_{{\mathrm{CFS}};\beta;t}}+\left|\left|\chi_{1}R_{\Phi^{pre;t}}\eta_{2}\right|\right|_{C^{1,\alpha}_{{\mathrm{CFS}};\beta;t}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle q(t)\cdot\left|\left|\eta_{1}-\eta_{2}\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{0,\alpha}_{\beta-1;t}}\left(\left|\left|\eta_{1}\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{0,\alpha}_{\beta-1;t}}+\left|\left|\eta_{2}\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{0,\alpha}_{\beta-1;t}}\right)

\displaystyle(ii)=

\displaystyle\left|\left|\pi_{\mathcal{I};\beta-1}(1-\chi^{t}_{4})D_{\Phi^{pre;t}}\pi_{-;\Phi^{pre;t}}\left\{Q_{\Phi^{pre;t}}(R_{\Phi^{pre;t}}\delta_{t}^{*}\eta_{1})-Q_{\Phi^{pre;t}}(R_{\Phi^{pre;t}}\delta_{t}^{*}\eta_{2})\right\}\right|\right|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{{\mathrm{ACF}};\beta-1;t}}

\displaystyle+t^{-\kappa}\left|\left|\varpi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta-1}(1-\chi^{t}_{4})D_{\Phi^{pre;t}}\pi_{-;\Phi^{pre;t}}\left\{Q_{\Phi^{pre;t}}(R_{\Phi^{pre;t}}\delta_{t}^{*}\eta_{1})-Q_{\Phi^{pre;t}}(R_{\Phi^{pre}_{t}}\delta_{t}^{*}\eta_{2})\right\}\right|\right|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\max\{1,t^{\kappa-\beta-m/2-\alpha}\}\max\left\{\left|\left|{\mathrm{d}}\Phi^{pre;t}_{\zeta}\right|\right|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{{\mathrm{ACF}};\beta-1;t}}\left|\left|1\right|\right|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{{\mathrm{ACF}};-2\beta;t}},\left|\left|1\right|\right|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{{\mathrm{ACF}};-\beta;t}}\right\}

\displaystyle\cdot\left|\left|(1-\chi^{t}_{5})R_{\Phi^{pre;t}}(\delta_{t}^{*}\eta_{1}-\delta_{t}^{*}\eta_{2})\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{1,\alpha}_{{\mathrm{ACF}};\beta;t}}

\displaystyle\cdot\left(\left|\left|(1-\chi^{t}_{5})R_{\Phi^{pre;t}}\delta_{t}^{*}\eta_{1}\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{1,\alpha}_{{\mathrm{ACF}};\beta;t}}+\left|\left|(1-\chi^{t}_{5})R_{\Phi^{pre;t}}\delta_{t}^{*}\eta_{2}\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{1,\alpha}_{{\mathrm{ACF}};\beta;t}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle q(t)\cdot\left|\left|\eta_{1}-\eta_{2}\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{0,\alpha}_{\beta-1;t}}\left(\left|\left|\eta_{1}\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{0,\alpha}_{\beta-1;t}}+\left|\left|\eta_{2}\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{0,\alpha}_{\beta-1;t}}\right)

where we used Lemma
4.7
, which ensures that
D^{pre}_{t}
and
D_{\Phi^{pre}_{t}}
are close and that

\displaystyle\left|\left|\varpi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta-1}(1-\chi^{t}_{4})D^{pre;t}_{\zeta}\eta\right|\right|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{t}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|\left|\varpi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta-1}\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;H}(1-\chi^{t}_{4})\eta\right|\right|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{t}}

\displaystyle+\left|\left|\varpi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta-1}(D^{pre;t}_{\zeta}-\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;H})(1-\chi^{t}_{4})\eta\right|\right|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{t}}

\displaystyle+\left|\left|\varpi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta-1}{\mathrm{cl}}_{g^{pre;t}_{\zeta}}({\mathrm{d}}\chi^{t}_{4})\eta\right|\right|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle t^{m/2-\beta-\alpha}\left|\left|(1-\chi^{t}_{5})\eta\right|\right|_{C^{1,\alpha}_{{\mathrm{ACF}};\beta;t}}

The bounds for
(II)
are analogous and thus we deduce that

\displaystyle(I)+(II)\lesssim

\displaystyle q(t)\cdot\left|\left|\eta_{1}-\eta_{2}\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{0,\alpha}_{\beta-1;t}}\left(\left|\left|\eta_{1}\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{0,\alpha}_{\beta-1;t}}+\left|\left|\eta_{2}\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{0,\alpha}_{\beta-1;t}}\right)

where

\displaystyle q(t)=\min\left\{\lambda\beta,\kappa-m/2-\alpha\right\}

Now, for
\eta\in\mathfrak{C}^{0,\alpha}_{\beta-1;t}
satisfying

\displaystyle\left|\left|\eta\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{0,\alpha}_{\beta-1;t}}<\varrho(t)

where
\varrho(t)
satisfies

\displaystyle t^{\vartheta}<\varrho(t)<q(t)^{-1}\hskip 28.45274pt\text{and}\hskip 28.45274ptq(t)\rho(t)^{2}<e(t)

\mathcal{C}_{t}
defines a self-mapping and by the contraction mapping principal, we obtain a fixpoint
\eta_{t}
of
\mathcal{C}_{t}
. Consequently, the torsion-free
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structure
\Phi_{t}=\Theta(\Phi^{pre}_{t}+\eta_{t})
on
X_{t}
satisfies

(35)

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi_{t}-\Phi^{pre}_{t}\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{D}^{1,\alpha}_{\beta;t}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|\left|D_{\Phi^{pre}_{t}}\left(\eta_{t}+Q_{\Phi_{t}^{pre}}(\eta_{t})\right)\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{0,\alpha}_{\beta-1;t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|\left|*_{\Phi^{pre}_{t}}{\mathrm{d}}\pi_{+;\Phi^{pre}_{t}}Q_{\Phi_{t}^{pre}}(\eta_{t})\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{0,\alpha}_{\beta-1;t}}+\left|\left|{\mathrm{d}}\Phi^{pre}_{t}\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{0,\alpha}_{\beta-1;t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle q(t)\rho(t)^{2}+e(t)\lesssim t^{\vartheta}

Finally, we will prove the smoothness of
\Phi_{t}
. We will prove this by using an elliptic bootstrap to show that the solution
\eta_{t}
is smooth. First notice that a solution to

\displaystyle-D_{\Phi_{t}^{pre}}\eta_{t}=D^{op}_{\Phi_{t}^{pre}}\Phi^{pre}_{t}+D_{\Phi_{t}^{pre}}\pi_{-;\Phi^{pre}_{t}}\left\{Q_{\Phi_{t}^{pre}}(\eta_{t})\right\}+D^{op}_{\Phi_{t}^{pre}}\pi_{+;\Phi^{pre}_{t}}\left\{Q_{\Phi_{t}^{pre}}(\eta_{t})\right\}

can be seen as a solution to

\displaystyle\left[D_{\Phi_{t}^{pre}}\pi_{-;\Phi^{pre}_{t}}+\left\{D_{\Phi_{t}^{pre}}\pi_{-;\Phi^{pre}_{t}}+D^{op}_{\Phi_{t}^{pre}}\pi_{+;\Phi^{pre}_{t}}\right\}Q_{\Phi_{t}^{pre}}\circ\right]\eta_{t}=-D^{op}_{\Phi_{t}^{pre}}\Phi^{pre}_{t}

where we can see
\left[D_{\Phi_{t}^{pre}}\pi_{-;\Phi^{pre}_{t}}+\left\{D_{\Phi_{t}^{pre}}\pi_{-;\Phi^{pre}_{t}}+D^{op}_{\Phi_{t}^{pre}}\pi_{+;\Phi^{pre}_{t}}\right\}Q_{\Phi_{t}^{pre}}\circ\right]
as a nonlinear, first order differential operator that depends smoothly on the 1-jet of
\Phi^{pre}_{t}
. Further notice, that

\displaystyle\left\{D_{\Phi_{t}^{pre}}\pi_{-;\Phi^{pre}_{t}}+D^{op}_{\Phi_{t}^{pre}}\pi_{+;\Phi^{pre}_{t}}\right\}Q_{\Phi_{t}^{pre}}(0)=0

and hence, we can deduce that
\left[D_{\Phi_{t}^{pre}}\pi_{-;\Phi^{pre}_{t}}+\left\{D_{\Phi_{t}^{pre}}\pi_{-;\Phi^{pre}_{t}}+D^{op}_{\Phi_{t}^{pre}}\pi_{+;\Phi^{pre}_{t}}\right\}Q_{\Phi_{t}^{pre}}\circ\right]
is elliptic
22
22

22

Its linearisation is an elliptic first order differential operator.
for
C^{0}
-small enough
\eta
, as ellipticity is an open condition. For
\eta\in C^{0,\alpha}
the right hand side of the above equation is also in
C^{0,\alpha}
, but as the right hand side is smooth,
[
ADN64
, Thm. 10.7]
ensures that
\eta
lies in
C^{1,\alpha}
. Elliptic bootstrapping, yields the desired smoothness.
∎

Remark 46
.

A concrete choice of parameters that satisfies all the assumptions made in the proof is

\displaystyle\lambda\approx 0.65,\quad\alpha\ll 1,\quad\beta\ll 0,\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\kappa=\frac{m}{2}+\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}.

With this choice, the exponent
\vartheta
appearing in the a priori estimate satisfies

\displaystyle\vartheta\approx 2.6,

ensuring that the torsion is sufficiently small to apply the contraction mapping argument and complete the gluing construction.

5.2.1.
Deformation to Torsion-Free
G_{2}
-Structures

Let
(Y,\varphi)
be a
{\mathrm{G}}_{2}
orbifold, whose singular strata of codimension
m
is given by
P\subset Y
. In the following we will resolve the
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-orbifold

\displaystyle(X,\Phi)=(\mathbb{T}^{1}\times Y,{\mathrm{d}}\theta\wedge\varphi+*_{\varphi}\varphi)

in an
\mathbb{T}^{1}
-equivariant way. Such a
\mathbb{T}^{1}
-equivariant torsion-free
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structure
\Phi_{\zeta;t}
on
X_{\zeta;t}=\mathbb{T}^{1}\times Y_{\zeta;t}
yields a torsion-free
{\mathrm{G}}_{2}
on
Y_{\zeta;t}
. This construction thus generalises and extends the work of Joyce and Karigiannis
[
JK21
]
.

In order to apply Theorem
5.3
, we need to verify that the operator
D^{pre}_{\zeta;t}
is isentropic, i.e.
{\mathrm{ob}}_{\beta;t}=0
. By Proposition
3.4
and Corollary
3.4
it suffices to compute the total cohomology of
Y_{\zeta;t}
and compare its dimension with the one of
{\mathrm{xker}}_{\beta}(D^{pre}_{\zeta;t})
.

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
P\subset Y
be a singular stratum of codimension
m=4
. The cohomology of
Y_{\zeta;t}
is given by

\displaystyle{\mathrm{H}}^{k}(Y_{\zeta;t})={\mathrm{H}}^{k-2}(P,\mathcal{H}^{2}(\zeta^{*}\mathfrak{M}/P))\otimes{\mathrm{H}}^{k}(Y)

Furthermore, the cohomology of
Y_{\zeta;t}
is given by

\displaystyle{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(Y_{\zeta;t})={\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(Y)\oplus{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(Q_{\zeta})/{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(P).

By working in a
\mathbb{T}^{1}
-equivariant pregluing datum, we deduce the following corollary.

Corollary 5.1

(Existence of Torsion-Free
{\mathrm{G}}_{2}
-Orbifold Resolutions)
.

Let
(Y_{\zeta;t},\varphi_{\zeta;t})
be a resolution of the
{\mathrm{G}}_{2}
-orbifold
(Y,\varphi)
, such that
\mathbb{T}^{1}\times Y_{\zeta;t},{\mathrm{d}}s\wedge\varphi^{pre}_{\zeta;t}+*_{g_{\varphi^{pre}_{\zeta;t}}}\varphi^{pre}_{\zeta;t})
is a
\mathbb{T}^{1}
-equivariant, isentropic, adiabatic
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-resolution of the orbifold
(\mathbb{T}^{1}\times Y,{\mathrm{d}}s\wedge\varphi+*_{g_{\varphi}}\varphi)
. Then there exists a torsion-free
{\mathrm{G}}_{2}
-structure
\varphi_{\zeta;t}
on
Y_{\zeta;t}
satisfying

(36)

\displaystyle\left|\left|\varphi_{\zeta;t}-\varphi^{pre}_{\zeta;t}\right|\right|_{X_{\zeta;t}}\lesssim t^{\vartheta}.

Again,
\vartheta
satisfies (
Notation
) and depends on the choices of
\alpha
,
\beta
,
\lambda
and
\kappa
as well as the pregluing error.

5.3.
The Holonomy of the Resolved
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-Structure

In the following section we will discuss how the holonomy of the resolved metric can be determined. This will be done by computing the
\hat{A}
-genus of
X_{\zeta;t}
.

The
\hat{A}
-genus of the resolution can be expressed in terms of the Betti-numbers of
X
and the
L^{2}
-Betti numbers of
N_{\zeta}
. In particular,

(37)

\displaystyle\hat{A}(X_{\zeta;t})=1+b^{7}_{2}(X_{\zeta;t})-b_{1}(X_{\zeta;t})

holds for
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-manifolds. By
[
Joy00
, Thm. 10.6.1]
the holonomy of a compact
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-manifold is the full group
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
, if the
\hat{A}
-genus is equal to 1. Moreover, in latter case both

\displaystyle b^{7}_{2}(X_{\zeta;t})=b_{1}(X_{\zeta;t})=0.

vanish. As we have already seen in Section
3.2
and under the Assumption
8
, we are able to proof the following statement.

Notation
.

We will denote by

\displaystyle\beta_{i}(N_{\zeta})\coloneqq\sum_{p+q=i}{\mathrm{dim}}(E^{p,q}_{2}(N_{\zeta}))-b_{i}(S).

Proposition 5.2
.

Let us assume that Assumption
8
holds. Then the
\hat{A}(X_{\zeta;t})
is given by

\displaystyle\hat{A}(X_{\zeta;t})=1+b^{7}_{2}(X)-b_{1}(X)+\beta^{7}_{2}(N_{\zeta})-\beta_{1}(N_{\zeta}).

where
\beta^{j}_{i}
are the refined harmonic Betti numbers of
N_{\zeta}
. Let
S\subset X
be a singular strata of type A of codimension four, such that
(X,\Phi)
admits a resolution
(X_{\zeta;t},\Phi_{\zeta;t})
. Furthermore, in this case

\displaystyle\beta^{7}_{2}(N_{\zeta})=\beta_{1}(N_{\zeta})=0

and thus,
{\mathrm{Hol}}(g_{\Phi_{\zeta;t}})={\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
if and only if
{\mathrm{Hol}}(g_{\Phi})={\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
.

Proof.

First notice, that the
\wedge^{\bullet}T^{*}N_{\zeta}
decomposes in the presence of the Ehresmann connection on
N_{\zeta}
into irreducible
{\mathrm{Sp}}(1){\mathrm{Sp}}(1){\mathrm{Sp}}(1)\subset{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-representations. As we have assumed that Assumption
8
holds, we know that

\displaystyle{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(N_{\zeta})/{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(S)=\underset{}{{\mathrm{Tot}}^{1}}\left({\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(S,\mathcal{H}^{\bullet}(N_{\zeta}/S))\right)=0

and we deduce that

\displaystyle\beta_{1}(N_{\zeta})=0

as
\mathcal{H}^{0}(N_{\zeta}/S)\cong\mathcal{H}^{1}(N_{\zeta}/S)=0
. The composition into irreducible
{\mathrm{Sp}}(1){\mathrm{Sp}}(1){\mathrm{Sp}}(1)
-subbundles induces a decomposition of

\displaystyle{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(N_{\zeta})/{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(S)

on the second page of the Leray-Serre spectral sequence as
\Phi_{\zeta}
satisfies
({\mathrm{d}}^{1,0}+{\mathrm{d}}^{0,1})\Phi_{\zeta}=0
. In the case
m=4
the stabilizer of the fibred
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
is
H\subset{\mathrm{Sp}}(1){\mathrm{Sp}}(1){\mathrm{Sp}}(1)
and

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{2}_{7}(N_{\zeta})\cong{\mathrm{H}}^{0}(S,\mathcal{H}^{2}_{+}(N_{\zeta}/S))\oplus{\mathrm{H}}^{2}_{+}(S,\mathcal{H}^{0}(N_{\zeta}/S))\oplus{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{\Phi_{\zeta}}(S,\mathcal{H}^{1}(N_{\zeta}/S))

and since
\mathcal{H}^{2}(N_{\zeta}/S)=\mathcal{H}^{2}_{-}(N_{\zeta}/S)

\displaystyle\beta^{7}_{2}(N_{\zeta})=0

we conclude the proof.
23
23

23

Here
{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{\Phi}(S,\mathcal{H}^{1}(N_{\zeta}/S))
is a subspace of
{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(S,\mathcal{H}^{1}(N_{\zeta}/S))
satisfying some algebraic condition. But as the latter vanishes,
{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{\Phi}(S,\mathcal{H}^{1}(N_{\zeta}/S))
vanishes identically.

∎

6.
Examples of Compact
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-Manifolds

In this section, we will illustrate the application of the existence result established in the previous section by discussing concrete examples of compact
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-manifolds. The construction of such manifolds typically proceeds in three main steps:

1.

Identify a suitable compact
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-orbifold.

2.

Verify Assumption
7
for the constructed resolution.

3.

Verify Assumption
8
for the constructed resolution.

We begin by addressing the first and most crucial step: How does one find compact
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-orbifolds that serve as candidates for our construction? Several classes of examples are known or can be systematically produced, which we briefly summarize below.

Notation
.

In the following we will denote by
\textbf{CY}_{m}
] the category of
2m
real dimensional Calabi-Yau manifolds,
\textbf{HK}_{k}
the category of
4k
real dimensional Hyperkähler manifolds,
\textbf{G}_{2}
for the category of
G_{2}
-manifolds and
Spin(7)
for the category of
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-manifolds.
24
24

24

Morphisms are structure preserving smooth structure.

•

Flat compact
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-orbifolds:
These take the form
\mathbb{O}/G
, where
G\subset{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)\ltimes\mathbb{O}
is a crystallographic group. Such orbifolds are classified in low dimensions and offer a tractable source of examples.

•

Compact
\textbf{CY}_{4}
with antiholomorphic involutions:
Given a compact Calabi-Yau 4-fold
(Y,\omega,\Omega)
equipped with an antiholomorphic involution
\sigma
, one can often construct a
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structure on the quotient
Y/\langle\sigma\rangle
, possibly after resolving singularities.

•

Lifted group actions on lower-dimensional spaces:
This includes actions on products such as
(\textbf{HK}_{1}\times\textbf{HK}_{1})/G
,
(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\textbf{CY}_{3})/G
, or
\mathbb{T}^{1}\times\textbf{G}_{2}
, where the symmetry group
G
can be chosen to preserve an induced
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structure.

Notation
.

In the following we will denote by
\textbf{CY}_{m}
] the category of
2m
real dimensional Calabi-Yau manifolds,
\textbf{HK}_{k}
the category of
4k
real dimensional Hyperkähler manifolds,
\textbf{G}_{2}
for the category of
G_{2}
-manifolds and
Spin(7)
for the category of
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-manifolds.
25
25

25

Morphisms are structure preserving smooth structure.

6.1.
Generalised Kummer Construction

The first set of example of compact
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-manifolds which we will discuss are the ones first considered by Joyce
[
Joy96a
]
. The idea of how to construct these examples is by generalising the Kummer construction of K3 surfaces to the
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-setting. In the following we will discuss these examples in a more general context and show that the estimates on the distance of the torsion-free to the pre-glued structure can be improved. This will extend the work of Platt
[
Pla20
]
in the
{\mathrm{G}}_{2}
-setting.

Let
(\mathbb{O},\Phi_{0},g_{0})
be the standard flat
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structure on
\mathbb{O}
and let
\Gamma\subset{\mathrm{Iso}}(\mathbb{O},\Phi_{0})
be discrete and co-compact subgroup of
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-isometries. In particular, we have a lattice
\Lambda
and a subgroup
H\subset{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
such that

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H}

is short exact.

Definition 6.1
.

A resolution of
\mathbb{O}/G
is called a
generalised Kummer
-
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
manifold or a Joyce manifold and is a compact torsion-free
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-manifold.

\lozenge

Proposition 6.1
.

The singular strata of the orbifolds
\mathbb{O}/G
can always be covered by intersecting affine tori of codimensions four, six, seven and eight. In particular, if the singular strata is of type A and the singular strata is of codimension four, the resolution data exists, if the flat bundle
\mathfrak{H}
admits a parallel section avoiding the walls.

Proof.

Notice, that the image of the fix point set of an element in
g=(w,A)\in H\ltimes\Lambda=G
in
\mathbb{O}/G
is given by an affine torus

\displaystyle Fix(g)=\left\{x_{0}+{\mathrm{ker}}(A-1)\right\}/\left\{\Lambda\cap{\mathrm{ker}}(A-1)\right\}.

Consequently, the singular locus is given by the intersection of such affine tori. As the ambient metric is flat, harmonic self-dual two forms are parallel and hence non-vanishing.
∎

Remark 47
.

Furthermore, if
S
is of type A, the normal bundle
NS
can be covered by the trivial bundle
\mathbb{R}^{m}\times\mathbb{T}^{s}
. As a consequence, Assumption
8
is easily verifiable.

6.1.1.
Improved Estimates for Joyce-Manifolds

The first examples of compact
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-manifolds were constructed by Joyce in
[
Joy96a
]
by resolving the orbifold singularities of
\mathbb{T}^{8}/\mathbb{Z}^{4}_{2}
and the ones of certain weighted projective spaces. Using Theorem
5.3
we are able to deduce improved estimates for the torsion-free
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structure constructed via the generalised Kummer construction. Notice, that
\Phi_{hot}=0
and consequently, via are able to improve on
\vartheta
.

Theorem 6.1
.

Let
G=\Gamma\ltimes\mathbb{Z}^{8}
and
(X_{\zeta;t},\Phi_{\zeta;t})\dashrightarrow(\mathbb{R}^{8}/G,\Phi_{st})
be one of the Joyce manifolds constructed in
[
Joy96a
]
and
\Phi_{\zeta;t}
its torsion-free
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structure. Let
\Phi^{pre}_{\zeta;t}
be the Cayley form on
X_{\zeta;t}
constructed from the pregluing process. Then

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi_{\zeta;t}-\Phi^{pre}_{\zeta;t}\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{D}^{1,\alpha}_{\beta;t}}\lesssim t^{\approx 4}.

6.2.
Resolution of
Spin(7)
with
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-Involutions

In this subsection we will discuss how to construct a family of compact
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-manifolds, starting from a
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-manifold with involutions.

Lemma 6.1
.

[
Joy00
, Prop. 10.8.6]

Let
(\hat{X},\hat{\Phi})
be a
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-manifold with an involution

\displaystyle\tau:(\hat{X},\hat{\Phi})\rightarrow(\hat{X},\hat{\Phi}).

The fixpoint-locus of
\tau
consists of the disjoint union of points and a (non) connected Cayley submanifold. In particular, the quotient
(X,\Phi)\coloneqq(\hat{X},\hat{\Phi})/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
is a
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-orbifold with codimension four and eight, isotropy
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
strata.

6.2.1.
Resolution of
\textbf{CY}_{4}
with Antiholomorphic Involutions

In the following subsection we will construct examples of
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-orbifolds using real homogenous polynomials.

Definition 6.2
.

A
complete intersection

Z
of dimension
n
is the intersection
H_{1}\cap...\cap H_{k}\subset\mathbb{CP}^{m}
of
k
-hypersurfaces that intersect transversely so that
n=m-k
.

\lozenge

By the adjunction formula we know that the canonical bundle of
Z
is trivial, if

\displaystyle d_{1}+...+d_{k}=m+1

where
d_{i}
denotes the degree of
H_{i}
. By Yau’s proof of the Calabi-conjecture
[
Yau18
]
a complete intersection carries a Calabi-Yau metric. In a similar manner we can use complete intersections in weighted projective spaces to construct Calabi-Yau orbifolds.

The complex conjugation defines an anti-holomorphic involution of
\mathbb{CP}^{m}
. If all hypersurfaces are cut-out by equations in real coefficients, this antiholomorphic involution restricts to one on
Z
. An equivariant version of the existence result of the Calabi-Yau metric on
Z
shows that the Calabi-Yau metric is
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-invariant. In the following, we will denote this structure by
(Z,\omega,\theta)
.

If the real locus
{\mathrm{Re}}(Z)
is nonempty, it is a totally geodesic special Lagrangian with respect to the Calabi-Yau structure
(Z,\omega,\theta)
.

Proposition 6.2
.

Let
(Z,\omega,\theta)
be a
\textbf{CY}_{4}
given as a complete intersection. Assume that the real locus
{\mathrm{Re}}(Z)
of
Z
is non empty and smooth. Let
\mathfrak{Z}_{2}\rightarrow{\mathrm{Re}}(Z)
be a twofold, unramnified cover of
{\mathrm{Re}}(Z)
. If there exists a harmonic, self-dual,
\mathfrak{Z}_{2}
-twisted, nonvanishing two form
\zeta
on
{\mathrm{Re}}(Z)
, then the quotient
Z/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
admits a resolution

\displaystyle\rho_{\zeta;t}:X_{\zeta;t}\dashrightarrow Z/\mathbb{Z}_{2}.

Moreover, the resolution carries a family of torsion-free
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structure
\Phi_{\zeta;t}
resolving the natural one on
(Z/\mathbb{Z}_{2},\Phi)
induced by the Calabi-Yau structure.

Remark 48
.

It remains an open question if Assumption
8
holds in general.

Remark 49
.

Let
(\omega_{FS},\theta_{Z})
denote the
{\mathrm{SU}}(4)
-structure induced by the Fubini-Study metric on
Z
. Assume that
(\omega,\theta_{Z})
is a Calabi-Yau structure close to
(\omega_{FS},\theta_{Z})
. If
|\zeta_{FS}|>c
for sufficiently large
c
, then
\zeta
might be non-vanishing.

Example 4
.

Let
Z
denote the complete intersection given by

\displaystyle\{[z_{0}:...:z_{5}]\in\mathbb{CP}^{5}|z_{0}^{6}+z_{1}^{6}+z_{2}^{6}-z_{3}^{6}-z_{5}^{6}-z_{6}^{6}=0\}.

The real locus
{\mathrm{Re}}(Z)
is given by

\displaystyle\{[x_{0}:...:x_{5}]\in\mathbb{RP}^{5}|x_{0}^{6}+x_{1}^{6}+x_{2}^{6}-x_{3}^{6}-x_{5}^{6}-x_{6}^{6}=0\}.

which is diffeomorphic to
(\mathbb{S}^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2})/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
. In particular,
{\mathrm{H}}^{2}({\mathrm{Re}}(Z),\mathbb{R})=0
but

\displaystyle{\mathrm{H}}^{2}({\mathrm{Re}}(Z),\widetilde{{\mathrm{Re}}(Z)}\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\mathbb{R})={\mathrm{H}}^{2}(\widetilde{{\mathrm{Re}}(Z)},\mathbb{R})=\mathbb{R}^{2}.

In particular,

\displaystyle b_{2}^{+}(\widetilde{{\mathrm{Re}}(Z)})=\frac{1}{2}(b_{2}(\widetilde{{\mathrm{Re}}(Z)})-\sigma(\widetilde{{\mathrm{Re}}(Z))})=1.

The harmonic representative of
{\mathrm{H}}^{2}_{+}(\widetilde{{\mathrm{Re}}(Z)},\mathbb{R})
with respect to the metric on
{\mathrm{Re}}(Z)
, induced by the Fubini-Study metric, the sum of two volume-forms hence, non-vanishing.

\clubsuit

6.2.2.
Conjectural Examples in
\textbf{SYZ}_{4}

The SYZ-conjecture is a conjectural relation between mirror symmetry and T-duaily in String Theory. Formulated by Ströminger, Yau and Zaslow in the category
\textbf{CY}_{3}
the conjecture extends to special holonomy spaces.

Conjecture 6.1
.

[
SYZ96
]

Let
(X,\Phi)\in\textbf{Spin(7)}
. There exists a compact Riemannian four manifold
S
and a singular fibration

\displaystyle\pi:X\rightarrow S

by toric Cayleys. Moreover, the mirror-dual
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-manifold is given by

\displaystyle\overline{X}\coloneqq{\mathrm{Hom}}(\pi_{1}(\pi^{-1}),{\mathrm{U}}(1)).

Remark 50
.

In the subcategory
\textbf{CY}_{4}
the fibration is a fibration by special Lagrangian tori.

Let
(\hat{X},\hat{\Phi})
be a SYZ-fibred
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-manifold. We define an involution

\displaystyle\tau:(\hat{X},\hat{\Phi})\rightarrow(\hat{X},\hat{\Phi})

by fibrewise multiplication by
-1
. The singular strata of
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-orbifold
(X,\Phi)/\tau
are sixteen copies of
S
with isotropy
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
.

Conjecture 6.2
.

Let us assume that there exists non-vanishing, harmonic
\zeta\in\Omega^{2}_{+}(S)
. Assume that we can resolve
(X,\Phi)/\tau
by a family

\displaystyle\rho_{\zeta;t}:(X_{\zeta;t},\Phi_{\zeta;t})\dashrightarrow(X,\Phi)/\tau.

Then
(\overline{X},\overline{\Phi})/\overline{\tau}
can be resolved by a family

\displaystyle\rho_{\zeta^{*};t}:(\overline{X}_{\zeta^{*};t},\overline{\Phi}_{\zeta^{*};t})\dashrightarrow(\overline{X},\overline{\Phi})/\overline{\tau}

and both
(X_{\zeta;t},\Phi_{\zeta;t})
and
(\overline{X}_{\zeta^{*};t},\overline{\Phi}_{\zeta^{*};t})
are mirror dual.

6.3.
Resolutions of
(\textbf{HK}_{1}\times\textbf{HK}_{1})/G

In the following subsection, we will focus on a select set of examples of compact
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-manifolds. These will be constructed using finite group actions on products of compact hyperkähler four-manifolds
\textbf{HK}_{1}
.

Given two hyperkähler four-manifolds
(M_{i},\underline{\omega}_{i},g_{i})\in\textbf{HK}_{1}
, we define the natural Cayley form on their product as

\displaystyle\hat{\Phi}={\mathrm{vol}}_{1}-{\mathrm{tr}}(\underline{\omega}_{1}\wedge\underline{\omega}_{2})+{\mathrm{vol}}_{2},

where
\underline{\omega}_{i}
denotes the hyperkähler triple on
M_{i}
, and
{\mathrm{vol}}_{i}
represents the associated volume forms.

Group actions by isometries on
\textbf{HK}_{1}\times\textbf{HK}_{1}
can be divided into two types:

•

Product-type actions:
finite groups acting diagonally on
(M_{1},g_{1})\times(M_{2},g_{2})
.

•

Swapping-type actions:
involutions swapping
M_{1}
and
M_{2}
via an isometry
M_{1}\cong M_{2}
.

For the first type of group actions, a finite group
G
that preserves the Cayley form
\hat{\Phi}
must act by orientation-preserving isometries and induce a
G
-equivariant isomorphism between the maximal positive-definite subspaces

\displaystyle P_{1}\subset{\mathrm{H}}^{2}(M_{1},\mathbb{R})\hskip 28.45274pt\text{and}\hskip 28.45274ptP_{2}\subset{\mathrm{H}}^{2}(M_{2},\mathbb{R}),

with the identification
{\mathrm{H}}^{2}(M_{2},\mathbb{R})^{*}\cong{\mathrm{H}}^{2}(M_{2},\mathbb{R})
from Poincaré duality. We will discuss the details of these conditions further in Subsection
6.3.1
.

For the second type of group actions, the involution swapping
M_{1}
and
M_{2}
preserves the Cayley form
\hat{\Phi}
if and only if it is a hyperkähler involution. In this case, the orbifold quotient is given by

\displaystyle(M_{1}\times M_{1})/\mathbb{Z}_{2}={\mathrm{Sym}}^{2}(M_{1}),

the symmetric product of
M_{1}
. We will explain how resolutions of these symmetric products are related to the Hilbert schemes of two points. The
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-orbifold resolutions obtained this way will carry hyperkähler structures. Using the results from Section
5
, the pre-glued hyperkähler metrics will approximate those constructed via Yau’s solution to the Calabi conjecture. These constructions and their properties will be further explored in Subsection
6.3.3
.

6.3.1.
Actions on
\textbf{HK}_{1}

A vast class of examples of compact
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-orbifolds are constructed by quotients of products of hyperkähler lines, i.e.
\mathbb{H}/\Lambda\times\mathbb{H}/\Lambda^{\prime}
,
\mathbb{H}/\Lambda\times K3
and
K3\times K3^{\prime}
. The first example are flat
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-orbifolds (Bieberbach orbifolds) and their resolutions have been extensively studied by Joyce in
[
Joy00
]
. From now on we will assume that
M_{i}\in\textbf{HK}_{1}
and that at most one of them is flat.

Given a
M\in\textbf{HK}_{1}
the second cohomology
{\mathrm{H}}^{2}(M,\mathbb{R})
is a real vector space. The intersection form defines a definite bilinear form of signature

\displaystyle(3,3)\hskip 28.45274pt\text{or}\hskip 28.45274pt(3,19)

In the following we will denote by
P\subset{\mathrm{H}}^{2}(M,\mathbb{R})
the maximal positive subspace, corresponding to the span of the hyperkähler structure. The
{\mathrm{H}}^{2}(M,\mathbb{R})
contains a canonical lattice given by

\displaystyle{\mathrm{H}}^{2}(M,\mathbb{Z})\subset{\mathrm{H}}^{2}(M,\mathbb{R}).

Let
G\subset{\mathrm{Isom}}(M,g)
denote a finite group acting via isometries. This action induces a representation

\displaystyle\varrho_{G}:G\rightarrow{\mathrm{Aut}}({\mathrm{H}}^{2}(M,\mathbb{Z}))\subset{\mathrm{Aut}}({\mathrm{H}}^{2}(M,\mathbb{R})).

A particularly fruitful approach to constructing group actions on a K3 surface
M\in\textbf{HK}_{1}
involves in the first step studying the induced action on its second integral cohomology group
{\mathrm{H}}^{2}(M,\mathbb{Z})
, which carries the structure of an even, unimodular lattice of signature
(3,19)
, known as the K3 lattice. Automorphisms of
M
that preserve the hyperkähler structure induce lattice automorphisms preserving the intersection form, and thus correspond to elements in
{\mathrm{O}}({\mathrm{H}}^{2}(M,\mathbb{Z}))
.

The construction of such actions can be approached lattice-theoretically. Given a finite group
G
, one seeks a faithful representation

\displaystyle\varrho_{G}:G\to{\mathrm{O}}(\Lambda_{{\mathrm{K3}}})

that satisfies certain geometric constraints. In particular, to lift such a representation to an actual geometric action on a K3 surface, it is necessary that
\varrho_{G}
preserves a positive-definite 3-plane
P\subset{\mathrm{H}}^{2}(M,\mathbb{R})
, corresponding to the span of the hyperkähler structure
[\underline{\omega}]
. By the global Torelli theorem
[
Huy16
, Thm 2.4]
for K3 surfaces, such a lattice automorphism lifts to an actual isometry of the surface if and only if it preserves this period plane and satisfies appropriate monodromy conditions.

Following the work of Nikulin
[
Nik79
,
VGS07
]
, this can be formalized by analysing primitive embeddings of the invariant sublattice
\Lambda^{G}\subset\Lambda_{{\mathrm{K3}}}
, ensuring that its orthogonal complement
(\Lambda^{G})^{\perp}
satisfies suitable lattice-theoretic conditions (e.g., absence of roots or obstructions to ampleness). Such data determines whether the action lifts to a holomorphic, anti-holomorphic, or non-symplectic automorphism.

This method is used to construct suitable actions on
M_{1}\times M_{2}
from diagonal actions
G\subset{\mathrm{Isom}}(M_{1})\times{\mathrm{Isom}}(M_{2})
by identifying the positive planes
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
via a
G
-equivariant isometry

\displaystyle\hat{\Phi}^{2,2}:P_{1}\xrightarrow{\cong}P_{2},

which ensures that the Cayley form
\hat{\Phi}
descends to the quotient
(M_{1}\times M_{2})/G
, yielding a torsion-free
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structure.

Definition 6.3
.

An action of a group
G
is holomorphic if
P_{i}\cong\mathbb{R}\oplus\mathbb{C}
as a
G
-space, and the action is given by
{\mathrm{id}}\oplus\chi
for a character
\chi:G\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times}
.

\lozenge

Corollary 6.1
.

Let
G
act holomorphically on
(M_{1},\underline{\omega}_{1})
and
(M_{2},\underline{\omega}_{2})
. Then
G
preserves
\hat{\Phi}^{2,2}
if and only if
\chi_{1}=\overline{\chi}_{2}
.

Remark 51
.

Using the Nikulin-classification
[
Nik79
,
VGS07
,
Huy16
]
of (anti-)holo-morphic and (anti-symplectic)
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-actions, it is possible to construct a vast number of new examples of compact
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-manifolds. Using these actions by
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{d}
we are able to construct
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-orbifolds with singular strata being diffeomorphic to the
K3
or products of two tori and Riemannian surfaces. These orbifolds can always be resolved.

Remark 52
.

If the singular stratum
S=S_{1}\times S_{2}
is smooth, of codimension four, isotropy type
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
and of type
(2,2)
in
\textbf{HK}_{1}\times\textbf{HK}_{1}
, then there exists canonical, non-vanishing self-dual two form on
S
given by

\displaystyle\zeta={\mathrm{vol}}_{S_{1}}+{\mathrm{vol}}_{S_{2}}.

6.3.2.
Examples of Resolutions of
(HK1\times HK1)/G

In the following we will construct a new example of a
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-manifold with holonomy equal
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
.

Example 5
.

We will follow the idea in
[
JK21
, Chap. 7.3]
and pick a K3 surface
\Sigma
that admits a branched double cover

\displaystyle\Sigma\rightarrow\mathbb{CP}^{2}

whose branching set is given by a sixtic
C
of genus
10
. We define an involution

\displaystyle\alpha_{\Sigma}:\Sigma\rightarrow\Sigma

by swapping the sheets of the branched covering and consequently
{\mathrm{Fix}}(\alpha_{\Sigma})=C
.

Furthermore, the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
action on
\mathbb{CP}^{2}
by complex conjugation can be lifted to an action

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{CP}^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{CP}^{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta_{\Sigma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{.}}

for which
{\mathrm{Fix}}(\beta_{\Sigma})=\mathbb{S}^{2}
and
{\mathrm{Fix}}(\alpha_{\Sigma}\beta_{\Sigma})=\emptyset
. Now, set

\displaystyle\mathbb{T}^{1}=[0,4]/\sim

an define the
\mathbb{Z}^{3}_{2}
-action

\displaystyle\alpha_{\mathbb{T}^{4}}

\displaystyle:(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3},x_{4})\mapsto(-x_{1},2-x_{2},x_{3},x_{4})

\displaystyle\beta_{\mathbb{T}^{4}}

\displaystyle:(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3},x_{4})\mapsto(2-x_{1},x_{2},-x_{3},x_{4})

\displaystyle\gamma_{\mathbb{T}^{4}}

\displaystyle:(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3},x_{4})\mapsto(-x_{1},-x_{2},2-x_{3},2-x_{4})

on the four torus
\mathbb{T}^{4}
, with

\displaystyle{\mathrm{Fix}}(\alpha_{\mathbb{T}^{4}})=

\displaystyle\left\{{0\choose 2},{1\choose 3},\mathbb{T}^{1},\mathbb{T}^{1}\right\}

\displaystyle{\mathrm{Fix}}(\beta_{\mathbb{T}^{4}})=

\displaystyle\left\{{1\choose 3},\mathbb{T}^{1},{0\choose 2},\mathbb{T}^{1}\right\}

\displaystyle{\mathrm{Fix}}(\gamma_{\mathbb{T}^{4}})=

\displaystyle\left\{{0\choose 2},{0\choose 2},{1\choose 3},{1\choose 3}\right\}.

We can lift the action of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{3}
to
\mathbb{T}^{4}\times\Sigma
via
\alpha=\alpha_{\mathbb{T}^{4}}\times\alpha_{\Sigma}
,
\beta=\beta_{\mathbb{T}^{4}}\times\beta_{\Sigma}
and
\gamma=\gamma_{\mathbb{T}^{4}}\times{\mathrm{id}}_{\Sigma}
whose fixpoint sets are given by

\displaystyle{\mathrm{Fix}}(\alpha)=

\displaystyle\left\{{0\choose 2},{1\choose 3},\mathbb{T}^{1},\mathbb{T}^{1}\right\}\times C

\displaystyle{\mathrm{Fix}}(\beta)=

\displaystyle\left\{{1\choose 3},\mathbb{T}^{1},{0\choose 2},\mathbb{T}^{1}\right\}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}

\displaystyle{\mathrm{Fix}}(\gamma)=

\displaystyle\left\{{0\choose 2},{1\choose 3},{0\choose 2},{1\choose 3}\right\}\times\Sigma.

and the words

\displaystyle\alpha\beta,\alpha\gamma,\beta\gamma,\alpha\beta\gamma

act freely on
\mathbb{T}^{4}\times\Sigma
.

Clearly, this
\mathbb{Z}^{3}_{2}
action preserves the torsion-free
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structure

\displaystyle\hat{\Phi}={\mathrm{vol}}_{\mathbb{T}^{4}}+{\mathrm{vol}}_{\Sigma}-{\mathrm{tr}}\left(\underline{\omega}_{\mathbb{T}^{4}}\wedge\underline{\omega}_{\Sigma}\right)

on
\mathbb{T}^{4}\times\Sigma
and hence, the torsion-free structure
\hat{\Phi}
descends to a torsion-free
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structure
\Phi
on the orbifold

\displaystyle(\mathbb{T}^{4}\times\Sigma)/\mathbb{Z}^{3}_{2}.

The singular strata in
(\mathbb{T}^{4}\times\Sigma)/\mathbb{Z}^{3}_{2}
are nonintersecting and given by two copies of
\mathbb{T}^{2}\times C
, two copies of
\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}
and four copies of
\Sigma
.

We can pick the resolution data on
\mathbb{T}^{2}\times C
to be determined by
\zeta={\mathrm{vol}}_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}+{\mathrm{vol}}_{C}
, the resolution data on
\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}
by
\zeta={\mathrm{vol}}_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}+{\mathrm{vol}}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}
and on the
\Sigma
we can freely pick any combination of the hyperkähler triple as
\zeta
.

In order to verify Assumption
8
we compute the Lerray-Serre spectral sequence of
N_{\zeta}\rightarrow S
. The spectral sequence collapse on the second page, i.e. the maps

\displaystyle{\mathrm{d}}^{3,-2}\colon

\displaystyle{\mathrm{H}}^{0}(S,\mathbb{R})\rightarrow{\mathrm{H}}^{3}(S)

\displaystyle{\mathrm{d}}^{3,-2}\colon

\displaystyle{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(S,\mathbb{R})\rightarrow{\mathrm{H}}^{4}(S)

as
\Sigma
is simply connected, and via a degree argument on the product strata
\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}
and
\mathbb{T}^{2}\times C
.
26
26

26

In the latter case we use that
N_{\zeta}\rightarrow S\rightarrow\mathbb{T}^{2}
is a sequence of fibre bundles and hence
{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(S,{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(N_{\zeta}/S))\cong{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(\mathbb{T}^{2},{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(S/\mathbb{T}^{2},{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(N_{\zeta}/S)))\cong{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(\mathbb{T}^{2},{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(N_{\zeta}/\mathbb{T}^{2}))\cong{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(N_{\zeta})
.

\clubsuit

Lemma 6.2
.

The Assumption
8
holds on
X_{\zeta;t}
and we thus obtain a
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structure
\Phi_{\zeta;t}
satisfying

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi_{\zeta;t}-\Phi^{pre}_{\zeta;t}\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{D}^{1,\alpha}_{\beta;t}}\lesssim t^{\vartheta}.

Moreover, as
b_{1}(X_{\zeta;t})=b^{7}_{2}(X_{\zeta;t})=0
the resolved
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-manifold has full holonomy.

Example 6
.

Let
i\in\{1,2\}
and in the following
g_{i}(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3})\in\mathbb{R}[z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}]_{4}
be two homogeneous degree four polynomial with no real solution, e.g. the
g_{i}(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3})=z_{1}^{4}+z_{2}^{4}+z_{3}^{4}
. Let

\displaystyle f(z_{0},z_{1},z_{2},z_{3})=z_{0}^{4}+g_{i}(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}).

The hypersurface
M_{i}
corresponding to the vanishing locus of
f
in
\mathbb{CP}^{3}
is a K3 surface. Define an action of
D_{4}=\left<\tau,g|\tau^{2}=g^{4}=1,\tau g\tau g=1\right>
on
M
by

\displaystyle g_{i}:[z_{0}:z_{1}:z_{2}:z_{3}]\mapsto[{\mathrm{i}}z_{0}:z_{1}:z_{2}:z_{3}]

and

\displaystyle\tau:[z_{0}:...:z_{3}]\mapsto[\overline{z}_{0}\colon\overline{z}_{1}\colon\overline{z}_{2}\colon\overline{z}_{3}].

Further, we have

\displaystyle Fix(g_{i})=\left\{[z_{1}:z_{2}:z_{3}]\in\mathbb{CP}^{2}|g_{i}(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3})=0\right\}

and
Fix(\tau)=\emptyset
. Moreover,
g_{i}^{*}\omega_{i}=\omega_{i}
and
g_{i}^{*}\theta_{i}={\mathrm{i}}\theta_{i}
, and
\tau^{*}\omega_{i}=-\omega_{i}
and
\tau^{*}\theta_{i}=\overline{\theta}_{i}
.

The
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structure

\displaystyle\hat{\Phi}={\mathrm{vol}}_{1}-\omega_{1}\wedge\omega_{2}-{\mathrm{Re}}(\theta_{1}\wedge\overline{\theta_{2}})+{\mathrm{vol}}_{2}

is
D_{4}
-invariant, and hence, the
(X,\Phi)\coloneqq(M_{1}\times M_{2},\hat{\Phi})/D_{4}
is a
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-orbifold, with singular stratum of codimension four and isotropy
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
, given by

\displaystyle S\coloneqq

\displaystyle\left\{([z_{1}:...:z_{2}],[w_{1}:...:w_{2}])\in\mathbb{CP}^{2}\times\mathbb{CP}^{2}|\right.

\displaystyle\left.g_{1}(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3})=g_{2}(w_{1},w_{2},w_{3})=0\right\}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}.

Notice, that the bundle
Fr(X/S,\Phi)
reduces further to the strata being a two-fold cover of a product. In particular, the frame bundle of
S
reduces to
Fr(S)=(Fr(S_{1})\times Fr(S_{2}))/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
, which is a
{\mathrm{U}}(1)^{2}
-bundle on
S
. We twisted the parameter bundle by
\mathbb{Z}_{2}\subset Weyl(A_{3})
and pick

\displaystyle\zeta\coloneqq{\mathrm{vol}}_{S_{1}}+{\mathrm{vol}}_{S_{2}}\in\Omega^{0}(S,\mathfrak{P}).

\clubsuit

Lemma 6.3
.

There exists family
\rho_{\zeta;t}:(X_{\zeta;t},\Phi_{\zeta;t})\dashrightarrow(X,\Phi)
torsion-free
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-orbifold resolution such that

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi_{\zeta;t}-\Phi^{pre}_{\zeta;t}\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{D}^{1,\alpha}_{\beta;t}}\lesssim t^{\vartheta}.

6.3.3.
The Hilbert Scheme
{\mathrm{Hilb}}^{2}(\textbf{HK}_{1})

Using Yau’s proof of the Calabi-conjecture, it was shown that the Hilbert scheme of two points
{\mathrm{Hilb}}^{2}(\textbf{HK}_{1})
of a
\textbf{HK}_{1}
, i.e. a crepant resolution of
{\mathrm{Sym}}^{2}(\textbf{HK}_{1})
carries a hyperkähler metric.

Using Theorem
5.3
we will construct a family of torsion-free
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structures on
{\mathrm{Hilb}}^{2}(\textbf{HK}_{1})
whose holonomy reduces to a subgroup of
{\mathrm{Sp}}(2)
. As such, we prove that the hyperkähler metric on
{\mathrm{Hilb}}^{2}(\textbf{HK}_{1})
is “close” to the pre-glued metric.

Let
(\Sigma,\underline{\omega}_{\Sigma})\in\textbf{HK}_{1}
denote a hyperkähler structure. The second symmetric product
{\mathrm{Sym}}^{2}(\textbf{HK}_{1})=(\textbf{HK}_{1}\times\textbf{HK}_{1})/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
is a
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-orbifold whose torsion-free
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structure is induced by the product structure

\displaystyle\hat{\Phi}={\mathrm{vol}}_{1}+{\mathrm{vol}}_{2}-{\mathrm{tr}}(\underline{\omega}_{1}\wedge\underline{\omega}_{2}).

The singular strata of
{\mathrm{Sym}}^{2}(\Sigma)
is the diagonal
\Sigma
and its normal bundle can be identified with its tangent bundle, i.e.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\Sigma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T{\mathrm{Sym}}^{2}(\Sigma)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N\Sigma}

splits by
N\Sigma\cong T\Sigma\ni v\mapsto(\pm v\oplus\mp v)
. There exists a two sphere of nonvanishing, harmonic, self-dual two forms corresponding to resolutions

\displaystyle N_{\omega_{I}}\dashrightarrow N\Sigma/\mathbb{Z}_{2}.

The spaces
N_{\omega_{I}}
are biholomorphic to
{\mathrm{Bl}}_{I}(0:\Sigma\hookrightarrow N\Sigma/\mathbb{Z}_{2})
and the resolved space is biholomorphic to

\displaystyle{\mathrm{Bl}}_{I}(\Delta:\Sigma\hookrightarrow{\mathrm{Sym}}^{2}(\Sigma))\cong{\mathrm{Hilb}}^{2}(\Sigma).

The pre-gluing metric
g^{pre}_{I;t}
interpolates between the metric on the symmetric product and the metric on the ACF space
N_{\omega_{I}}
.

Proposition 6.3
.

[
JK21
, Cor. 3.9]

There exists a Morsian tubular neighbourhood of the diagonal
\Sigma
in
{\mathrm{Sym}}^{2}(\Sigma)
, i.e.

\displaystyle{\mathrm{exp}}_{g_{\Phi}}^{*}\Phi=\Phi+\Phi_{hot}\hskip 28.45274pt\text{and}\hskip 28.45274pt|\Phi_{hot}|=\mathcal{O}(r^{2})

Proof.

A geodesic neighbourhood of the diagonal in
K3\times K3
is invariant under the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-action. By Taylor expanding the Cayley form and using the arguments
[
JK21
, Chap. 3.4]
we can eliminate the linear order term in the expansion.
∎

Proposition 6.4
.

There exists an
B^{3}_{T}(0)\backslash\{0\}\subset{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathbb{H})
-family of hyperkähler metrics
g_{I;t}
on
{\mathrm{Hilb}}^{2}(\Sigma)
satisfying

\displaystyle\left|\left|g_{I;t}-g^{pre}_{I;t}\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{D}^{1,\alpha}_{\beta;t}}\lesssim t^{\vartheta}.

6.4.
Resolutions of
(\textbf{CY}_{3}\times\mathbb{T}^{2})/G

Another source of examples of
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-orbifolds are quotients of a
(Z,\omega,\theta)\in\textbf{CY}_{3}
and a complex torus. We define the
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-manifold

\displaystyle(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times Z,{\mathrm{d}}z\wedge{\mathrm{d}}\overline{z}\wedge\omega+{\mathrm{Re}}({\mathrm{d}}z\wedge\theta)).

We can construct a
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-orbifold by matching actions on
Z
by an action on
\mathbb{T}^{2}
to construct
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-orbifolds. These orbifolds will usually not be simply connected and hence, are not suitable to construct new examples of full holonomy
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-manifolds.

Examples with isotropy
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
and codimension four singular strata can be constructed analogously to
[
JK21
, Chap. 7.4]
.

6.5.
Resolutions of
(\textbf{G}_{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1})/\mathbb{Z}_{2}

In the following section we will discuss how to construct compact
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-manifolds using the extra twisted connected sum of
G_{2}
-manifolds
[
Nor23
]
. This example produces a family of
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-manifolds with a neck stretching phenomena. Using methods from geometric measure theory, we are able to construct ACyl
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-manifolds. The author is very grateful for the help of Johannes Nordström in the construction of this example.

Lemma 6.4
.

[
Joy00
, Prop. 10.8.1]

Let
(Y,\varphi)
be
G_{2}
-manifold with an involution

\displaystyle\tau:(Y,\varphi)\mapsto(Y,-\varphi).

If the fixpoint-locus is non-empty, then
Fix(\tau)\subset Y
is a coassociative.

Given such a tuple
(Y,\varphi,\tau)
, we construct the
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-orbifold

\displaystyle(X,\Phi)\coloneqq(\mathbb{S}^{1}\times Y,\theta\wedge\varphi+*_{\varphi}\varphi)/\tau

where
\tau
acts on
\mathbb{S}^{1}\mapsto\mathbb{S}^{1},\theta\mapsto\overline{\theta}
.

Lemma 6.5
.

Assume that the coassociative
{\mathrm{Fix}}(\tau)
admits a non-vanishing, harmonic, self-dual,
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-twisted two form

\displaystyle\zeta\in{\mathrm{H}}^{2}_{+}(Fix(\tau),\mathfrak{C}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\mathbb{R}),

then there exists a family of torsion-free
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-orbifold resolutions

\displaystyle\rho_{\zeta;t}:(X_{\zeta;t},\Phi_{\zeta;t})\dashrightarrow(X,\Phi),

such that

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi_{t;\zeta}-\Phi^{pre}_{\zeta;t}\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{D}^{1,\alpha}_{\beta;t}}\lesssim t^{\vartheta}.

A large class of existing examples of
\textbf{G}_{2}
manifolds are constructed by gluing two
\textbf{ACylG}_{2}
. Often this construction is described by the extra twisted connected sum
[
Nor23
]
. In this gluing construction, two
\mathbb{T}^{1}\times\textbf{ACylCY}_{3}
are joint by twisting the exterior
\mathbb{T}^{1}
with the one in the cross section of the
\textbf{ACylCY}_{3}
. A large class of
\textbf{ACylCY}_{3}
were constructed in
[
CHNP15a
,
CHNP15b
]
using (weak) Fano threefolds.

Definition 6.4
.

Let
(Z_{\pm},\omega_{\pm},\theta_{\pm})\in\textbf{ACyl}\textbf{CY}_{3}
with cross-section
\mathbb{T}^{1}\times\Sigma
where
\Sigma\in\textbf{HK}_{1}
. Assume that there exists an isometry

\displaystyle\mathfrak{r}:(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\Sigma,g_{\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\Sigma})\rightarrow(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\Sigma,g_{\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\Sigma})

such that
(-{\mathrm{id}}_{\mathbb{R}}\times\mathfrak{r})
is a translational invariant automorphism of

(Cyl(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\Sigma),\varphi_{Cyl})

where

\displaystyle\varphi_{Cyl}\coloneqq{\mathrm{d}}s\wedge{\mathrm{d}}\theta_{1}\wedge{\mathrm{d}}\theta_{2}+{\mathrm{d}}s\wedge\omega_{I}+{\mathrm{d}}\theta_{1}\wedge\omega_{J}+{\mathrm{d}}\theta_{2}\wedge\omega_{K},

i.e.
[\mathfrak{r},\tau_{s}]={\mathrm{id}}_{Cyl(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\Sigma)}
. The
extra twisted connected sum
of the building blocks
(\mathbb{T}^{1}\times Z_{i},\varphi_{i}\coloneqq{\mathrm{d}}\theta_{1}\wedge\omega_{i}+{\mathrm{Re}}(\theta_{i}))
by
\mathfrak{r}
is the unique, gauge fixed, torsion-free
G_{2}
-structure close to the preglued
G_{2}
-structure obtained gluing along
\mathfrak{r}
.

\lozenge

The hyperkähler-twist
\mathfrak{r}
restricts to an isometry of
\Sigma
and acts on a basis of the maximally positive subspace

by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{r}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\cos(\theta)&-\sin(\theta)&0\\
\sin(\theta)&\cos(\theta)&0\\
0&0&-1\end{array}\right).

Let

Notice, that an isometry of an ACyl spaces is asymptotic to an isometry of the form

In the following, we will construct two

.

\displaystyle\sigma_{\Sigma}^{*}(\omega_{I},\omega_{J},\omega_{K})=

6.6.
Resolutions of Weighted Projective Spaces

In
[
Joy99
]
, Joyce constructed
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-orbifold resolutions of weighted projective spaces by gluing asymptotically conical
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-manifolds into the isolated conical singularities of
\mathbb{CP}^{[a_{0},\dots,a_{4}]}
.

Using the adapted function spaces developed in this paper, we are able to refine Joyce’s analysis and improve the estimates for the distance between the torsion-free
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structure and the preglued structure.

Theorem 6.2
.

Let
X_{\zeta;t}\dashrightarrow\mathbb{CP}^{[a_{0},...,a_{4}]}
be one of the Joyce manifolds constructed in
[
Joy99
]
and
\Phi_{\zeta;t}
its torsion-free
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-structure. Let
\Phi^{pre}_{\zeta;t}
be the Cayley form on
X_{\zeta;t}
constructed from the pregluing process. Then

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi_{\zeta;t}-\Phi^{pre}_{\zeta;t}\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{D}^{1,\alpha}_{\beta;t}}\lesssim t^{\approx 4}.

Appendix A
Geometric Structures on Orbifolds

In the following section we will briefly discuss Riemannian orbifolds by following the work of Moerdijk in
[
Moe02
]
. In this approach, the category of orbifolds is associated with the category of proper foliation groupoids up to Morita equivalence. By representing an orbifold by a Lie groupoid, the notions of tangent bundle and normal bundle can be understood in a more natural way than by the usual chart based approach.

We will furthermore introduce the Chen-Ruan cohomology of Riemannian orbifolds. This cohomology theory was motivated by predictions from string theory and plays an important role in the study of orbifolds in both mathematics and physics. The Chen–Ruan cohomology refines the usual singular cohomology by incorporating information from the twisted sectors, which account for the orbifold’s local isotropy groups. This yields a richer algebraic structure that captures the contribution of singularities to the topology of the orbifold. In the context of this paper, an analytical obstructions that arise in the construction and deformation theory of
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
orbifold resolutions, can be expressed in terms of the Chen-Ruan cohomology of the underlying
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-orbifold, encoding how singular strata contribute to the moduli of smooth structures.

In this section we recall the general framework of

G
-structures on orbifolds and fix the notation used throughout this paper. We describe how reductions of the frame bundle define geometric structures compatible with the orbifold stratification. Building on this, we introduce Hermitian Dirac bundles on orbifolds, which play a central role in this paper.

A.1.
Orbifolds

In this section, we recall the groupoid-theoretic definition of orbifolds and their morphisms, following the modern approach via proper étale Lie groupoids and Morita equivalence.

Definition A.1
.

Let
X^{\bullet}
be a Lie groupoid, i.e. a groupoid object
28
28

28

A category in which every morphism is an isomorphism.
in
\textbf{C}^{\infty}\textbf{Mfd}
. We will denote by

\displaystyle X^{0}\coloneqq{\mathrm{Obj}}(X^{\bullet})\hskip 28.45274pt\text{and}\hskip 28.45274ptX^{1}\coloneqq{\mathrm{Mor}}(X^{\bullet}).

•

X^{\bullet}
is called a
proper Lie groupoid
if
(s,t):X^{1}\rightarrow X^{0}\times X^{0}
is a proper map. In particular, every isotropy group is a compact Lie group.

•

X^{\bullet}
is called a
foliation groupoid
if every isotropy group is discrete.

•

X^{\bullet}
is called
étale
if
s
and
t
are local diffeomorphisms.

Let
X^{\bullet}
be an étale Lie groupoid. Then every arrow
\phi:x\to y
in
X^{\bullet}
induces a germ of a diffeomorphism
\tilde{\phi}:(U_{x},x)\rightarrow(V_{y},y)
as
\tilde{\phi}=t\circ\hat{\phi}
, where
\hat{\phi}:U_{X}\rightarrow X^{1}
is an
s:X^{1}\rightarrow X^{0}
section, defined on a sufficiently small neighbourhood
U_{x}
of
x
.

•

X^{\bullet}
is called
faithfull
, if for each point
x\in X^{0}
the homomorphism
G_{x}\rightarrow{\mathrm{Diff}}_{x}(X^{0})
is injective.

\lozenge

Definition A.2
.

Let
X^{\bullet}
be a Lie groupoid. A
left (right)
X^{\bullet}
-space
is a smooth manifold
E
equipped with an action of
X^{\bullet}
. Such an action is given by the smooth morphisms

\displaystyle\pi:E\rightarrow X^{0}\hskip 28.45274pt\mu_{L}:X^{1}\times_{s,\pi}E\rightarrow E\hskip 28.45274pt(\mu_{R}:E\times_{\pi;t}X^{0}\rightarrow E)

satisfying
\pi(\phi.e)=s(\phi)
,
1_{x}.e=e
and
\eta.(\phi.e)=(\eta\phi).e
. For each
X^{\bullet}
space
E
, we define the translation groupoid
(X\ltimes E)^{\bullet}
whose objects are points in
E
and whose morphisms
\phi:e\rightarrow e^{\prime}
are morphisms
\phi:\pi(e)\rightarrow\pi(e^{\prime})
in
X^{1}
with
\phi.e=e^{\prime}
. There exists a homomorphism
\pi_{E}:(X\ltimes E)^{\bullet}\rightarrow X^{\bullet}
of orbifolds.

\lozenge

Remark 53
.

By replacing the left by right actions in the above definition we can define right
X^{\bullet}
-spaces.

Remark 54
.

Notice that on the level of groupoids, the fibre over
x\in X^{0}
is
\pi^{-1}(x)
; at the level of orbit spaces
|X\ltimes E|\rightarrow|X|
the fibres are
\pi^{-1}(x)/{\mathrm{Isot}}_{x}
.

Definition A.3
.

A
right/left-
X^{\bullet}
-
Y^{\bullet}
-bibundle

Z
is given by smooth maps
\alpha_{X}:Z\rightarrow X^{0}
and
\alpha_{Y}:Z\rightarrow Y^{0}
called the moment maps such that both
\alpha_{X}
is a right/left
X^{\bullet}
-space and
\alpha_{Y}
is a right/left
Y^{\bullet}
-space, i.e.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X^{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Y^{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X^{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Y^{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{Y}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}

\lozenge

Definition A.4
.

A
generalised morphism

\displaystyle[Z]:X^{\bullet}\rightsquigarrow Y^{\bullet}

is given by an isomorphism class of right
X^{\bullet}
-
Y^{\bullet}
-bibundles.

\lozenge

Definition A.5
.

A Lie groupoid
X^{\bullet}
is
Morita equivalent
to Lie groupoid
Y^{\bullet}
if there exists a generalised morphism

\displaystyle[Z]:X^{\bullet}\rightsquigarrow Y^{\bullet}

which is also a left
X^{\bullet}
-
Y^{\bullet}
-bibundle.

\lozenge

Definition A.6
.

A
smooth orbifold

X
is the Morita equivalence class of a smooth, proper, foliation Lie groupoid
X^{\bullet}
. Given such an equivalence class, we will write

\displaystyle[X^{0}/X^{1}]:=[X^{\bullet}].

A
morphism of orbifolds
is given by an equivalence class of generalised morphism

\displaystyle[[Z]]:[X^{\bullet}]\rightsquigarrow[Y^{\bullet}].

\lozenge

Remark 55
.

In every equivalence class of an orbifold
[X^{\bullet}]\subset\textbf{LieGrpd}
there exists a proper, étale groupoid representing the orbifold.

Remark 56
.

Notice that the orbit space/coarse moduli space
X^{0}/X^{1}
just carries the data of a topological space. The stacky quotient
[X^{0}/X^{1}]
“remembers” the isotropy.

Definition A.7
.

The
tangent bundle
of an orbifold
[X^{\bullet}]
is defined by Morita equivalence class of the
X^{\bullet}
-vector bundle

\displaystyle\pi_{X}:TX^{0}\rightarrow X^{0},\quad\mu_{TX}:X^{1}\times_{s,\pi_{TX^{0}}}TX^{0}\rightarrow TX^{0},

where
\mu(g,V_{x})=Tg(V_{x})
for
g:x\to y
in
X^{1}
.

\lozenge

If
S^{\bullet}\subset X^{\bullet}
is subgroupoid such that all isotropy groups are conjugated to a finite group
\Gamma
, then the normal bundle
NS^{\bullet}\rightarrow S^{\bullet}
is naturally an
S^{\bullet}
-vector bundle, whereas the normal bundle
N|S|\to|S|
is a (quasi-)conical bundle on the coarse moduli space.

Definition A.8
.

The
frame bundle
of an
n
-dimensional orbifold
[X^{\bullet}]
is defined to be the Morita equivalence class of the
X^{\bullet}
-principal
\mathrm{GL}(n)
-bundle given by

\displaystyle f_{X}:Fr(X^{0})\rightarrow X^{0},\quad\mu_{Fr(X)}:X^{1}\times_{s,\pi_{X^{0}}}Fr(X^{0})\rightarrow Fr(X^{0}),

where
\mu_{Fr(X)}(\psi,f_{x})=T\psi\circ f_{x}
for
\psi:x\to y
in
X^{1}
.

\lozenge

The following corollary can be seen as the traditional definition of orbifolds via orbifold charts, i.e.
X
being a topological space covered by charts of the form
({\mathrm{Isot}}_{x}\ltimes U_{x})^{\bullet}
subject to gluing conditions.

Corollary A.1
.

[
Moe02
, Sec. 3.4]

Let
X
be a smooth orbifold presented by a proper étale Lie groupoid
X^{\bullet}
. For every open subset
U\subset X^{0}
we write
(X|_{U})^{\bullet}
for the full subgroupid whose objects are
U
. Since
X^{1}
is assumed to be proper and étale, for
x\in X^{0}
there exists an arbitrarily small neighbourhood
U_{x}
such that
(X|_{U_{x}})^{\bullet}
is isomorphic to the action groupoid
({\mathrm{Isot}}_{x}\ltimes U_{x})^{\bullet}
.

Definition A.9
.

The
dimension of a smooth orbifold
is defined as
{\mathrm{dim}}(X)\coloneqq{\mathrm{dim}}(U_{x})
which is independent of the point
x
.

\lozenge

Orbifolds are examples of stratified spaces. The stratification of
X
consists of the suborbifolds
S\subset X
for which
{\mathrm{Isot}}_{s}\cong{\mathrm{Isot}}_{s^{\prime}}
for all
s,s^{\prime}\in S
and

\displaystyle X^{1}/X^{0}=\bigcup_{[\Gamma_{\alpha}]}X_{\alpha}\in\textbf{Top}

where the union runs over all conjugacy classes
[\Gamma_{\alpha}]
of finite subgroups of
{\mathrm{GL}}(n)
. Notice, that every
X_{\alpha}
is a smooth (open) manifold.

We define a partial order on the set
\{X_{\alpha}\}
by

\displaystyle X_{\alpha}\leq X_{\beta}

if
\Gamma_{\beta}\subset\Gamma_{\alpha}
. Notice that since
\{X_{\alpha}\}
defines a stratification,
X_{\beta}\leq X_{\alpha}
implies
X_{\beta}\subset\overline{X_{\alpha}}
. Hence, we can define a prestratification of
X
by
X_{\tilde{\alpha}}=\{\bigcup_{\beta\leq\alpha}X_{\beta}\}_{\alpha}
. In particular, we say that two connected singular strata
X_{\alpha}
and
X_{\alpha^{\prime}}
intersect in a singular stratum
X_{\beta}
, if
X_{\beta}\leq X_{\alpha}
and
X_{\beta}\leq X_{\alpha^{\prime}}
or equivalently

\displaystyle X_{\tilde{\alpha}}\cap X_{\tilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}=X_{\beta}.

Notice, that
\overline{X_{\alpha}}=X_{\alpha}\sqcup\bigsqcup_{\beta\leq\alpha}X_{\beta}
.

Remark 57
.

The top stratum of an
n
-dimensional orbifold
X
, i.e. the open dense subset consisting of points with trivial isotropy will be denoted by
X^{reg}
. If the isotropy group
\Gamma_{reg}=1
, we call the orbifold effective. For the remainder of this paper we assume all orbifolds to be effective in order to avoid additional complications.

The following definitions quantify how severe or complex a given singularity can be. The depth measures the position of a stratum within the nested hierarchy of singularities, while the singularity type describes the local model and isotropy representation near the stratum. Together, these notions capture both the local complexity and the global stratified structure of the singular set.

Definition A.10
.

We define the
depth
of
X_{\alpha}
, as the length of the longest chain of inclusions of singular strata, i.e.

\displaystyle{\mathrm{depth}}(X_{\alpha})=\max\{i\in\mathbb{N}|X_{\alpha}=X_{\beta_{0}}\leq...\leq X_{\beta_{i}}=X^{reg}|X_{\beta_{k}}\neq X_{\beta_{k-1}}\}.

\lozenge

Definition A.11
.

Let
(X,g)
be a Riemannian orbifold and let
X^{sing}
denote the union of its singular strata. Let
S
be a stratum whose isotropy group is conjugated to a finite group
\Gamma\subset{\mathrm{O}}(n)
. We say

•

X
is of
singularity type A
at
S
, if
X
at
S\subset X
is locally modelled on

\displaystyle\mathbb{R}^{n-m}\times\mathbb{R}^{m}/\Gamma

such that
\Gamma
acts freely on
\mathbb{R}^{m}\backslash\{0\}
.

•

X
is of
singularity type B
at
S
, if
X
at
S\subset X
is locally modelled on

\displaystyle\mathbb{R}^{n-\sum_{i}m_{i}}\times\prod_{i}\mathbb{R}^{m_{i}}/\Gamma_{i}

such that
\Gamma_{i}
acts freely on
\mathbb{R}^{m_{i}}\backslash\{0\}
.

•

X
is of
singularity type C
at
S
, if it is neither type A nor type B

\lozenge

Remark 58
.

Let
S_{1}
and
S_{2}
be two singular strata of isotropy
\Gamma_{1}
and
\Gamma_{2}
. Then the intersection
S_{3}=\overline{S_{1}}\cap\overline{S_{2}}
is a singular strata of isotropy
\Gamma_{1}\Gamma_{2}\subset\Gamma_{3}
and
X
is singular of type B or C.

Notation
.

Throughout this paper we will denote by

\displaystyle{\mathrm{N}}_{G}(H)\hskip 28.45274pt\text{and}\hskip 28.45274pt{\mathrm{C}}_{G}(H)

the normaliser and the commutator subgroup of
H
in
G
.

Proposition A.1
.

Let
S\subset X
be a singular strata of isotropy type
\Gamma
. Then there exists a
{\mathrm{N}}_{{\mathrm{GL}}(n)}(\Gamma)\hookrightarrow{\mathrm{GL}}(n)
reduction of the restriction of the frame bundle
Fr_{X^{\bullet}}
to
S
.

Proof.

Let
X^{\bullet}
be a Lie groupoid representing the orbifold
[X^{0}/X^{1}]
. Let
S^{\bullet}
denote the sub Lie groupoid corresponding to
S\subset[X^{0}/X^{1}]
in the quotient space. Notice that the
X^{\bullet}
-frame bundle restricts to a
{\mathrm{GL}}(n)
-principal bundle of
S^{\bullet}
, i.e.

\displaystyle f_{X}|_{S}:Fr_{X^{0}}|_{S^{0}}\rightarrow S^{0}\hskip 28.45274pt\mu_{Fr_{X}|_{S}}:S^{1}\times_{s,\phi_{X^{0}}|_{S^{0}}}Fr_{X^{0}}|_{S^{0}}\rightarrow Fr_{X^{0}}|_{S^{0}}.

There exists a short exact sequence of
S^{\bullet}
-vector bundles

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TS^{\bullet}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TX^{\bullet}|_{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces NS^{\bullet}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

In particular, there exists a natural reduction to frames

\displaystyle f_{x}:H\oplus V\rightarrow TX^{0}

such that
p\circ f_{x}|_{V}\in{\mathrm{Fr}}(NS^{\bullet})
. A moment’s thought reveals that this reduction can be represented by smooth principal-
{\mathrm{N}}_{{\mathrm{GL}}(n)}({\Gamma})
-bundle
F_{{\mathrm{N}}_{{\mathrm{GL}}(n)}(\Gamma)}(S)\rightarrow S
.
∎

A.2.
G
-Structures

We begin by recalling the general framework of
G\subset{\mathrm{GL}}(n)
-structures orbifolds. These are constructed via reductions of the frame bundle of the orbifold.

Definition A.12
.

Let
X
be an orbifold and
Fr(X)
its
{\mathrm{GL}}(n)
-frame bundle. The
bundle of
G
-structures
on
X
is the homogeneous bundle

\displaystyle\gamma\colon Str_{G}(X)\coloneqq{\mathrm{Fr}}(X)/G\rightarrow X.

A
G
-structure on an orbifold is given by a section
\Phi\in\Gamma(X,Str_{G}(X))
. Such a section induces a
G
-reduction of the frame bundle by

\displaystyle Fr(X,\Phi)\coloneqq\Phi^{*}Fr(X)\hookrightarrow Fr(X)

pulling back the
G
-bundle
\phi_{/G}:Fr(X)\rightarrow Str_{G}(X)
.

\lozenge

Lemma A.1
.

Given a nested subgroup
G\subset H\subset{\mathrm{GL}}(W)
, there exists a natural projection map

\displaystyle Str_{G}(X)\twoheadrightarrow Str_{H}(X),

whose fibres are isomorphic to the homogeneous space
H/G
.

The existence of a
G
-structure on an orbifold can be obstructed by the nature of its isotropy groups. The following proposition clarifies the necessary condition.

Proposition A.2
.

Let
X
be an orbifold modelled on finite quotients of a finite dimensional vector space
W
and let
\phi:Fr(X)\rightarrow X
denote its frame bundle. If
Fr(X)
admits a reduction to a
G
-structure, then all isotropy groups are conjugated to subgroups of
G
.

Proof.

The isotropy group of a point
x\in X
can be identified with a conjugacy class of a finite subgroup
{\mathrm{Isot}}(x)\subset{\mathrm{GL}}(W)
. The frame bundle of
X
at
x
naturally reduces to frames

\displaystyle f:W\rightarrow T_{x}X

that normalizes the linearised action of the isotropy group, i.e. its representation in
{\mathrm{GL}}(T_{x}X)
. These frames form a
{\mathrm{N}}_{{\mathrm{GL}}(T_{x}X)}({\mathrm{Isot}}(x))
reduction of the frame bundle along the stratum containing
x
. If
X
contains strata whose isotropy is not conjugated to a subgroup of
G
, the reduction
{\mathrm{N}}_{{\mathrm{GL}}(T_{x}X)}({\mathrm{Isot}}(x))
can not be a
G
reduction. Hence,
Fr(X)
can not be reduced to a sub
G
-bundle.
∎

Example 8
.

Let
(W,\mathfrak{o})
be an oriented vector space. An orientation on an orbifold, locally modelled by finite quotients of
W
, is oriented if it admits a section of the orientation bundle

\displaystyle\mathfrak{o}(X)\coloneqq Fr(X)/{\mathrm{GL}}_{+}(W)\cong(\wedge^{top}T^{*}X)/\mathbb{R}_{>0}.

\clubsuit

Example 9
.

Let
(W,g_{0})
be an Euclidean vector space. The most important
G
-structure on an orbifold
X
locally modelled on
W
in Riemannian geometry is an
{\mathrm{O}}(W,g_{0})
-structure, i.e. a Riemannian orbifold structure. A Riemannian metric on
X
corresponds to a section of the homogeneous bundle

\displaystyle Met(X)\coloneqq Fr(X)/{\mathrm{O}}(W,g_{0})\rightarrow X

with fibres being

\displaystyle Met(W)\coloneqq{\mathrm{GL}}(W)/{\mathrm{O}}(W,g_{0}).

Given a section
g\in\Gamma(X,Met(X))
we obtain a reduction of the framebundle

\displaystyle Fr(X,g)\coloneqq g^{*}Fr(X)\hookrightarrow Fr(X)

by pulling back the
{\mathrm{O}}(W,g_{0})
-bundle
\phi_{/{\mathrm{O}}(W,g_{0})}:Fr(X)\rightarrow Met(X)
.

\clubsuit

We will now define the Clifford bundle of
X
. This is a finite dimensional orbifold bundle, such that given a section
g:X\rightarrow Met(X)
, the pullback of the Clifford bundle corresponds to the Clifford bundle of
X
with respect to
g
. This notion will be needed in the discussion of families of Dirac bundles later in this paper.

Definition A.13
.

We define the
Clifford bundle
of
X
to be the associated bundle

\displaystyle{\mathrm{Cl}}(X)\coloneqq Fr(X)\times_{{\mathrm{O}}(W,g_{0})}{\mathrm{Cl}}(W^{*},g_{0})\rightarrow Met(X).

Given a metric on
X
, i.e. a section
g:X\rightarrow Met(X)
, we have

\displaystyle g^{*}{\mathrm{Cl}}(X)={\mathrm{Cl}}(T^{*}X,g).

\lozenge

A.3.
G
-Structures on Fibrations

Throughout this paper we will work with geometric structures on fibrations. The following section is devoted to the discussion of such structures from the general perspective of
G
-structures as introduced in the previous sections.

Let
M
be an orbifold locally modelled on a vector space
W=H\oplus V
and
\pi:M\rightarrow B
a fibration. We define the group

\displaystyle{\mathrm{GL}}(W\to H)\coloneqq\{A\in{\mathrm{GL}}(H\oplus V)|A(V)=V,{\mathrm{pr}}_{H}\circ A(H)=H\}.

There exists a natural
{\mathrm{GL}}(W\to H)
-reduction of the frame bundle

\displaystyle Fr(\pi:M\rightarrow B)\hookrightarrow Fr(X)

of frames

\displaystyle Fr(\pi\colon M\rightarrow B)\coloneqq\{f_{1}\colon H\oplus V\rightarrow T_{m}M|f_{1}|_{V}\colon V\cong V_{m}\pi,\,T\pi\circ f_{1}|_{H}\in Fr(B)_{\pi(m)}\}.

We will now be interested in reductions of such fibered structures and their torsions. In particular, this will identify the torsion of such structures with geometric quantities. In general, we will set

\displaystyle G(W\to H)\coloneqq G\cap{\mathrm{GL}}(W\to H).

Notice that this group depends on the embedding of
G\subset{\mathrm{GL}}(W)
. We will now investigate fibred Riemannian structures.

Definition A.14
.

A
fibred Riemannian structure
is given by a section of

\displaystyle Fr(\pi\colon M\rightarrow B)/O(W\to H)\rightarrow M.

Here
{\mathrm{O}}(W\to H)=O(W)\cap{\mathrm{GL}}(W\to H)\cong{\mathrm{O}}(H)\times{\mathrm{O}}(V)
.

\lozenge

The unique torsion-free Levi-Civita connection
\varphi_{g}
on
Fr(M,g)
pulls back to
Fr(\pi\colon M\to B,g)
and decomposes into

\displaystyle\varphi_{g}=\varphi_{\oplus}+T_{\oplus}.

We will refer to
T_{\oplus}
as the torsion of the fibred Riemannian structure. This tensor is valued in

\displaystyle T_{\oplus}\in\Omega^{0}(Fr(\pi\colon M\rightarrow B),\wedge^{2}H^{*}\otimes V\oplus{\mathrm{Sym}}^{2}V^{*}\otimes H)^{{\mathrm{O}}(W\to H)}.

We will now identify the tensor
T_{\oplus}
with geometric quantities of the Riemannian fibration. These results will be used throughout this paper to understand the behaviour of Dirac operators on collapsing fibrations and to construct resolutions of tubular neighbourhoods of singular strata of
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-orbifolds. The broader discussion is based on the work of Gromoll and Walshap in
[
GW09
]
and Berline, Getzler and Vergne in
[
BGV92
]
.

The connection
\varphi_{\oplus}
induces a covariant derivative that can be identified with

\displaystyle\nabla^{\oplus}=\pi^{*}\nabla^{g_{S}}\oplus\nabla^{g_{V}},

i.e. the direct sum connection of the vertical connection and the lift of the Levi-Civita connection on the base space through the Ehresmann connection
H=VM^{\perp}
. Let in the following

\displaystyle X,Y,Z\in\mathfrak{X}^{1,0}(M)\hskip 28.45274pt\text{and}\hskip 28.45274ptU,V,W\in\mathfrak{X}^{0,1}(M)

and denote by
\nabla
the Levi-Civita connection of
g
.

Definition A.15
.

We define the two tensor
A\in\Omega^{1,0}(M,{\mathrm{Hom}}(H,VM))
by

\displaystyle A(X)Y=\nabla^{0,1}_{X}Y=\frac{1}{2}[X,Y]^{0,1}

and

\displaystyle S\in\Omega^{1,0}(M,{\mathrm{End}}(VM))\quad\text{by}\quad S(X)U=-(\nabla_{U}X)^{0,1}.

\lozenge

The second fundamental form of the fibre can thus be identified with the tensor

\displaystyle{\mathrm{II}}(U,V)=\left<SU,V\right>^{\flat}.

By taking the vertical trace we obtain the fibrewise mean curvature, i.e. the one form

\displaystyle k={\mathrm{tr}}_{g;V}{\mathrm{II}}.

Remark 59
.

For a Riemannian fibration, the fibrewise second fundamental form
{\mathrm{II}}
or equivalently
S
vanishes if and only if the fibres are totally geodesic. Furthermore, the tensor
A
vanishes if and only if the Ehresmann connection
H
is flat.

Proposition A.3
.

[
BGV92
, Prop. 10.6]

Given
X,Y,Z\in\mathfrak{X}(M)
the torsion tensor
T_{\oplus}\in\Omega^{1}(M,\wedge^{2}T^{*}M)
can be expressed by

\displaystyle T_{\oplus}(X)(Y,Z)=

\displaystyle g({\mathrm{II}}(X,Z),Y)-g({\mathrm{II}}(X,Y),Z)

\displaystyle+\tfrac{1}{2}\left(g(F_{H}(X,Z),Y)-g(F_{H}(X,Y),Z)\right.

\displaystyle+\left.g(F_{H}(Y,Z),X)\right).

Remark 60
.

[
BGV92
, Prop. 10.1]

The de Rham differential on
M
decomposes with respect to
H
and the Riemannian structure
g=p^{*}g_{S}\oplus g_{V}
into

\displaystyle{\mathrm{d}}=

\displaystyle{\mathrm{d}}^{1,0}+{\mathrm{d}}^{0,1}+{\mathrm{d}}^{2,-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathrm{d}}_{\nabla^{g_{V}}}-\hat{\iota}_{{\mathrm{II}}}+{\mathrm{d}}^{0,1}+\hat{\iota}_{F_{H}}.

In particular, let
{\mathrm{vol}}_{V}
denote the vertical volume form with respect to
g_{V}
. Then

\displaystyle{\mathrm{d}}k(X,Y)=-2{\mathrm{div}}(A(X)Y)\hskip 28.45274pt\text{and}\hskip 28.45274pt{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{vol}}_{V}=k\wedge{\mathrm{vol}}_{V}+\iota_{F_{H}}{\mathrm{vol}}_{V}.
